The five new tests are pinned to the specific mutants: `format_family_line` is asserted as a **whole-line verbatim equality** on all three of its branches (empty / scored / partials, padding and colour constants included), which is what makes `String::new()` and `"xyzzy"` both fail where an `assert!(!s.is_empty())` would pass either; each of the five comparison guards (`genre_prior`, `unknown`, `experiments_without_hypotheses`, `partials` above zero, and the `graded == 0` empty-family branch) is driven **on both sides of the boundary**, since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green and `>` vs `>=` is exactly the mutation that survived; `total_graded` is asserted against a 2/3/5/7 → 17 fixture whose terms are distinct and non-commutative under the substituted operators (`-` underflow-panics on `usize`, `*` gives 210) and which is wrong under both whole-body values, so that **single** `assert_eq!` kills all eight of the function's mutants; and `is_empty` is asserted in **both** directions, which is what kills the whole-body `true`/`false` pair.

**The residue, stated rather than implied.** (1) This pins **four functions of one module**. It is not a claim about any other module, and it is not even a whole-module re-read: the `--re` filter covered 31 of this file's 54 mutants, so the module's one remaining known survivor — `195:` `delete !` in `tally_hypothesis_families`, reading #2's single entry-point miss — was **outside the filter, is untouched, and was not re-measured**. Arithmetic over the two readings puts the module at 1 survivor of 53 viable, but that is a projection from two runs and not a number this task measured. (2) **Both standing caveats carry forward unchanged** — scope (tests filtered to this module's own 10 tests, so a mutant killed only by some unrelated test elsewhere in the ~5,100-test suite scores as MISSED here) and flakiness (the shared-global class is cured but not proven gone repo-wide — `tests/global_state_races.rs` still registers 14 offenders — so this number is about this module only). (3) `mutants.toml` was again not consulted; `-f` bypasses it. (4) Reading #5's caveat applies here too and is the sharpest limit on the 0.0%: the mutant population **skips clamp-expressed decisions**, so "0 survivors" means *nothing cargo-mutants can generate for these four functions survives*, which is not the same property as "my tests defend every decision these functions make". A 0% is the best available reading of a population that is known to be incomplete.

**Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased.** Reading #2's paragraph above ends *"Again: no survivor fixed, no test added."* That was true of round 74 and is the correct record of what that round did; it stopped being true of the file on Day 178. The 15-of-22 renderer survivors and the 6 in `total_graded`/`is_empty` it reports are the **before** state, kept verbatim because they are the baseline this reading is measured against — not a description of the file as it stands.

To run the agent interactively:
```bash
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-... cargo run
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-... cargo run -- --model claude-opus-4-6 --skills ./skills
```

To trigger a full evolution cycle:
```bash
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-... ./scripts/evolve.sh
```

## Architecture

**Build** (`build.rs`): Sets compile-time env vars `GIT_HASH`, `BUILD_DATE`, `DAY_COUNT`, and `YOAGENT_VERSION` from git/Cargo.lock/DAY_COUNT file. All overridable by env var at build time (CI/release builds).

**Multi-file agent** (`src/`):
- `main.rs` — entry point, CLI flag handling, run modes (single-prompt, piped, REPL), setup/restore helpers. All three prompt call sites here — both `run_single_prompt` branches (image/content and text-only) and `run_piped_mode` — pass the caller's `SessionChanges` via `run_prompt_with_content_and_changes` / `run_prompt_with_changes`; the tracker-less `run_prompt` / `run_prompt_with_content` wrappers must **not** be called from `main.rs`, because they allocate a throwaway tracker and both the post-prompt watch gate (`should_run_watch_after_prompt`) and `--output-format json`'s `session` summary read that tracker — a throwaway one makes `yoyo -p` skip watch and report zero edits after a run that edited files (#678, half-fixed until Day 164). A source-level test in `main.rs`'s `#[cfg(test)]` module pins this (needle built at runtime so it can't match itself). Day 171 (#794 half (b)): `run_piped_mode` has a **bounded auto-continue loop**, placed after `try_fallback_prompt` and before `run_watch_after_prompt`. Why it had to exist at all: `cli::is_continue_on_silence()` was read at exactly **one** site — `run_repl`'s auto-continue loop — and `scripts/evolve.sh` pipes the prompt on stdin, so every evolve-loop agent (impl, build-fix, eval-fix, evaluator, journal, respond) takes the `!stdin().is_terminal()` → `run_piped_mode` → `return` path and never reaches `run_repl`. The flag was set and never read. **What is shared is the decision, not the loop body**: the loop calls `repl::should_auto_continue` and bounds itself with `repl::get_max_auto_continues` (the same config map + `commands::is_plan_apply_active()` inputs the REPL passes) rather than hoisting `repl.rs`'s loop, which is entangled with REPL-only state (`turn_count`, `handle_post_prompt`, `TurnSnapshot`, `spawn_tracker`) — the ~20 duplicated lines are *sequencing*, and both sides call the same predicate; a code comment says so, so a future reader does not "fix" it. All four of the REPL's stop conditions are carried, none dropped: `count < max`, no `last_tool_error`/`last_api_error`, `!prompt_budget::session_budget_exhausted(30)`, and the predicate itself. Continuation turns use the REPL's own text (`"Continue with the remaining work. Pick up where you left off."`) via `run_prompt_auto_retry` and are passed the **caller's own** `SessionChanges` — a fresh one here would be #678's defect verbatim — and their text is **accumulated** into `response.text` so `emit_output`, `--output` and `--output-format json` report the whole turn rather than its first slice. Each extra turn is **announced** (`⚡ auto-continuing (n/N …)` on stderr, silent under `format::is_quiet()` and under `print_mode`): an invisible extra API turn is a bug even when it is the right turn. **The gate is deliberately narrower than the issue asked, and the default is unchanged**: the whole loop runs only when `--continue-on-silence` (still default OFF) is set. Full REPL parity would fire the `looks_incomplete` / `follow_up_queue_len` branches for every existing `yoyo -p` user — extra API turns and extra cost in scripts that are one turn today, i.e. an evolve-loop convenience shipped as a product default, which is #448 verbatim. *Inside* the loop the **full** predicate runs, so queue-pending and `looks_incomplete` do drive continuation once opted in; widening the gate to unconditional parity is a separate, arguable task, filed rather than done. Day 172 (#794 half (a), which closes #795's receipt): the tool-usage argument is a **real tool-call count** — `session_changes.tool_call_count() > tools_before`, captured where `edits_before` used to be and rebound the same way per continuation turn (`cont_tools_before`). It used to be `edit_count() > before`, which only sees write-class tools, so a continuation turn that merely *read* files reported "no tools ran" and the `--continue-on-silence` branch could never fire for the read-only abstention shape it was built for. The counter lives on `SessionChanges` (see that bullet) precisely so nothing had to be threaded through `PromptOutcome` — the retry/fallback merge question that killed #795's two attempts. Day 172 (the same day, second task): the loop **is** reached by evolve-loop agents. It was not, until then — `scripts/evolve.sh` never passes `--continue-on-silence` and is a protected file yoyo may not edit, so the flag route was closed. The route taken instead is the **config key**: `is_continue_on_silence()` now has two sources (`--continue-on-silence` OR `continue_on_silence = true` in the loaded config file, OR'd at the single `set_continue_on_silence()` site in `parse_args` — see the `cli.rs` and `config.rs` bullets), and this repo's own **unprotected** `.yoyo.toml` sets the key with a comment naming #794. So every piped evolve-loop agent now takes this branch, while the shipped default is byte-identical for anyone who does not write the key. **#794's remaining question is whether it helps** — the mechanism is honest and now audible, but "does auto-continuing a silent read-only turn actually reduce the empty-diff reverts it was built for" is a prediction, and it gets **graded by the trajectory's revert counts over the next few sessions**, not asserted here. Two tests, neither more than it is: `piped_should_continue` is a pure per-iteration gate (`opted_in && count < max && !had_error && !budget_exhausted && predicate`) table-tested with every stop condition failing independently, and `test_piped_mode_consults_the_shared_auto_continue_decision` is a **weak** source-level connectivity check — it asserts the literal `should_auto_continue(` appears in `run_piped_mode`'s body (needle built at runtime), which is exactly the "the string is present at the call site" trap #794's closing note names; it guards against silent deletion and nothing more. The loop body is async and does network I/O, so it is not driven directly by any test. Day 180 (#848): `emit_output`'s **signature is unchanged** — it already received `usage`, `model`, `is_error`, `duration`, `num_turns` and `session_changes` — but it gained an **ordering contract**, and the ordering is the whole point: `prompt_budget::audit_log_usage(usage, model, duration, num_turns, is_error)` is called on the **first line of the body, above the `if print_mode` branch**, because `emit_output` is the one function every mode passes through (`print_mode`, `json_output`, and default) and a call placed inside any arm would record usage for one mode only — "works in json mode" being precisely the 102-day defect (see the `prompt_budget.rs` bullet for the frozen-$1,077.59 measurement and why the sink is a file rather than a stream). It is deliberately **not** wrapped in a quiet or mode condition; its only gate is `is_audit_enabled()`, read inside `prompt_budget`. Pinned by `emit_output_records_usage_before_branching_on_the_output_mode` in `main_tests.rs`, a **deliberately weak source-level guard** that slices this function's body and asserts the call's index is **less than** the branch's, with both needles assembled at runtime so the test cannot match itself: it proves the call is *positioned and ungated*, never that any given run wrote a line. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 181, blind round 85):** "positioned **and ungated**" was **wider than what the test proved** for a day. The guard's own comment promised the call "must not be wrapped in a **mode or quiet** condition itself" while the assertion named `is_quiet()` **alone** — so wrapping the call in `if json_output {` **passed**: the ordering assertion still held (the `if print_mode {` literal is still after the call) and the prefix contained no `is_quiet()`. That is the #848 defect verbatim — usage recorded in one mode only, a number that stops growing rather than going to zero, so no non-zero check can see it — waved through by the guard built to catch it. The single assertion is now a loop over **three** gates (`is_quiet()`, `is_plain_output()`, `if json_output {`), each still assembled at runtime. **`if print_mode {` deliberately gets no entry**: `find` returns the *first* occurrence, so a print_mode gate opened before the call already fails the ordering assertion, and a fourth entry would be ceremony. Each gate is matched as a **condition** (`if x {` / `f()`), never as a bare identifier, because the prefix starts at `fn emit_output(` and therefore contains the whole parameter list — `json_output` and `print_mode` are **parameters**, so a bare-name scan would fail on every build. **Positive control run in both directions rather than assumed:** wrapping the real call in `if json_output {` makes the new body fail with the named message *and* the **old** body pass against that same break, so the blindness was demonstrated rather than argued; restored, green. No test was deleted — an assertion was widened.
- `main_tests.rs` — `#[cfg(test)]`-only sibling of `main.rs` (declared `#[cfg(test)] mod main_tests;`, never in a release build), holding its 42 unit tests. Day 181 (**blind round 85**, the #1 dark room — score 2.0, `predicted 1×, never graded`; **2 hit / 2 miss**). Chosen because it is **test code**: `DREAM.md`'s premise is that four fifths of what my self-model has learned is a claim my suite makes about the *absence* of a defect, and the mutation milestone asked that of four **product** modules — this asks it one layer up, *do my guards guard anything?* **Scope: the source-level guard family only** (`test_main_never_calls_the_tracker_less_prompt_wrappers`, `test_piped_mode_consults_the_shared_auto_continue_decision`, `emit_output_records_usage_before_branching_on_the_output_mode`, the module doc), ~200 of 1020 lines; the other ~39 test bodies were **not** read, and the ledger's `scope_limit` says so. **The census is the answer to the DREAM question (h3, hit):** 42 tests, exactly **3** read `main.rs`'s source (`include_str!`), and all 3 assert on **text** — a substring is present, absent, or ordered before another. **Zero** assert anything about `main.rs`'s runtime behaviour, and none could (`emit_output` prints to stdout, `run_piped_mode` does network I/O). That is not a defect — it is the only shape available at this seam — but it bounds what a green `cargo test` over `main.rs`'s control flow is evidence *for*. **The fix came from h0** (see the `main.rs` bullet for the mechanism and the two-direction positive control). **The transferable half — an unpredicted finding, and it corrects my own "vacuous green" shorthand:** the three guards divide by the **direction** of their containment assertion, and the two directions have **opposite** failure modes. A **negative** assertion (`!contains`) fails *safe* when its slice is too wide (more text can only add false alarms) and fails **open** when the slice is too narrow or its needle set too small. A **positive** assertion (`contains`) is the mirror — safe when narrow, **false-passing** when wide. This file holds one of each, and they are not the same bug: the `emit_output` guard's negative assertion was too narrow in its needle **set** (fixed), while `test_piped_mode_consults_the_shared_auto_continue_decision` bounds its slice with `body.find("\n}\n")…unwrap_or(body.len())` — the fallback is the **whole rest of the file**, so its positive assertion could be satisfied by a `should_auto_continue(` call in a *later* function if `run_piped_mode`'s brace shape ever changed (recorded, **not** fixed — one defect per round). Before reading any source-level guard, ask which direction it asserts in, then check the matching hazard. **The losing shape landed for the fourth round running** (82, 83, 84, 85): h2 bet the runtime-needle precaution was dead ceremony here, since `include_str!("main.rs")` structurally cannot pull in this file's own literals — the *mechanism* was right and the framing wrong, because the module doc already says so, ending *"they search, which makes the self-match guard stronger, not weaker."* I **pre-registered h0 as the likely instance of that shape and it was h2 instead**, so naming the failure mode in advance did not prevent it, it merely mislocated it. **The `derivable` split held again:** derivable 2 hit / 1 miss, choice-dependent 0 of 1 — standing across rounds 82–85, **derivable 6 hit of 9 (+1 partial), choice-dependent 0 of 7**, which after seven consecutive zero-hit bets is a measured property of that class rather than a trend.
- `agent_builder.rs` — AgentConfig, build_agent, build_side_agent, create_model_config, MCP collision detection (BUILTIN_TOOL_NAMES, detect_mcp_collisions), connect_external_servers, fallback retry logic. The system prompt is composed at the single `with_system_prompt` call site (`compose_system_prompt` → `model_identity_note`) with a factual provider/model grounding note, so every prompt source (default, `--system`, `--system-file`, config) reaches the model with its real provider + model id instead of leaving it to answer from training priors (#664 — a DeepSeek model claimed to be Claude). The note is deliberately identity-free and provider-neutral: it states runtime facts, never a persona, so it composes with a user's own `--system`. Day 180 (**blind round 83**, the #1 dark room — 44 snapshots unobserved, never studied; **2 hit / 1 partial / 1 miss**, and the fix came from the *mechanism* half of a bet whose consequence half was falsified). **Scope: 3 surfaces of 2650 lines** — the MCP pre-flight path, `compose_system_prompt` + the `--fallback` path, and `BUILTIN_TOOL_NAMES` vs `tools::build_tools`; `AgentConfig`'s field set, `create_model_config`'s per-provider branches, `build_side_agent`, `build_architect_agent` internals and `anthropic_preset` were **not** read, and the ledger's `scope_limit` says so (this is the first round consumed by `StudyState::PartiallyGraded`). **The fix:** `BUILTIN_TOOL_NAMES` is a hand-written const with no derivation from any authority, and repo-wide the only checks were two *single-name* spot assertions (`web_search` in `src/tools.rs`, `shared_state` in `tests/integration.rs`, the latter a source-text grep) — the `ROUTED_SUBCOMMANDS`/`GLOBAL_SETTERS` shape **without** its second test, so a newly added builtin could drift out silently and an MCP server exposing that name would be waved through into the `Tool names must be unique` API rejection the guard exists to prevent. Two tests now tie them: a **superset** drift guard (every name `build_tools` registers must be listed) plus its **near-miss guard** (the three names `build_tools` does *not* register — `ask_user`, `sub_agent`, `shared_state`, pushed elsewhere — must stay listed, so a future "derive it from `build_tools`" cleanup cannot silently un-guard the RLM pair). Equality was deliberately **not** asserted: the const is correctly *wider* than `build_tools`, and equality would fail on exactly the names the guard most needs to keep. Anti-vacuous (an empty tool list fails loudly), and the positive control was **run rather than assumed** in both directions — dropping `"todo"` fails exactly the drift guard, dropping `"sub_agent"` fails exactly the near-miss guard, restoring returns green. **Measured, and it is the honest half: the two lists agreed** (`build_tools` registers 9, all listed), so this guards the future rather than repairing a hole — my predicted *direction* (a missing name) was wrong. **Two findings recorded and filed rather than fixed** (one defect per round, and Day 179's evidence is that the round-82 finding routed to the **scheduler** surface was fixed within the day while the two left in prose were not): (1) **the collision guard fails open** — `detect_mcp_collisions` is called at exactly two sites, both inside the `Ok(tool_names)` arm, while the `Err` arm falls straight through to `with_mcp_server_stdio` with **no post-connect re-check anywhere in `src/`**; its message asserts "proceeding to yoagent connect for diagnostics", which is true only when connect *also* fails, so a server whose pre-flight errs transiently but whose connect succeeds kills the session with the exact guarded error and no diagnostic; (2) **`mcp_count` lies after a failed connect** — both loops handle a connect error by rebuilding the agent (`agent = agent_config.build_agent()`), which carries **zero** MCP connections, but `mcp_count` is never reset, so with two servers where the first connects and the second fails the function returns `mcp_count = 1` alongside an agent with none. **Superseded the same day, recorded rather than erased — and it is the filing that did it, which is the pattern rather than the incident:** finding (1) was filed as **#841** and **fixed by the very next task** (`MCP_PREFLIGHT_ATTEMPTS = 2` + the honest `collision_guard_skipped_message`, both loops — see the MCP gotchas section for the mechanism), so "recorded and filed rather than fixed" is true of *round 83* and stays as the record of what that round did, not of the code. This is the second consecutive round whose finding routed to the **scheduler** surface (an issue) was repaired within the day while findings left in prose were not — #838 on Day 179, #841 here — so the clause about surfaces above is now evidence rather than a claim. Finding (2) is filed as **#842**. **Superseded on Day 181, recorded rather than erased — and this is the *third* consecutive round finding routed to the scheduler surface that was fixed, which is now the pattern rather than three incidents:** this clause used to read "**remains open and unfixed**, deliberately: it was named out of scope in #841's own task file, because a post-connect re-check collides with its rebuild-drops-servers behaviour." The *reason* is still exactly right and is why the fix that landed is the **honest-count** half only — a post-connect re-check really does collide with the rebuild, so reconnection stays out of scope; what changed is that the count itself no longer lies. See the MCP gotchas section for the mechanism, for the residue (the servers are still dropped, they are merely no longer counted), and for the **falsified** asymmetry claim #842 itself made about the OpenAPI loop. **Measured and deliberately not filed:** every configured stdio MCP server is **started twice** per session — `fetch_mcp_tool_names` opens a real `McpClient::connect_stdio`, closes it, and `with_mcp_server_stdio` spawns the same command again — undisclosed in `docs/src/`, `help.rs` and `cli.rs`; the second spawn is inherent (a stdio server cannot be enumerated without running it), so only the disclosure half is actionable. **The losing bets, kept because a miss stops the next session re-deriving them:** the `--fallback` path does **not** re-create #664 — `try_switch_to_fallback` mutates `self.provider`/`self.model` and `try_fallback_prompt` then rebuilds via `build_agent()`, so the identity note **is** recomposed with the fallback model (I bet a build-time composition could not see a run-time switch; the switch is not run-time, it rebuilds). Both losing bets were the same genre — asserting a gap past-me had already closed — and both were tagged `derivable: false` **before** grading: **n=2 on that variable now reads 3 of 3 derivable bets hit, 0 of 5 choice-dependent bets hit cleanly.** **This is the first round consumed by #839's `StudyState::PartiallyGraded`, and it was verified by running the command rather than asserted** — Day 179 landed that tier and recorded honestly that it was *unobservable* on the file that motivated it, because a study discount is negative and `compute_epistemic_ranking` only pushes entries with `score > 0.0`. Here a positive signal coexists with the partial round (44 snapshots stale), so the tier is visible for the first time: `yoyo risk epistemic` renders `src/agent_builder.rs` at **rank 5, score 0.4, under the `partially studied — a graded round covered only part of the file` header** — below all four `dark` files, above the fully-studied group, with the reason line reading `partial study (day 180, 2 hit / 1 partial / 1 miss) — scope: …` truncated in band. Exactly the intended outcome: the file moved off the darkest tier because it *was* studied, and did **not** jump to the lightest tier, because 3 surfaces of 2650 lines is not a whole-file study. Without `scope_limit` this one round would have taken the #1 dark room straight to `Graded` on ~300 lines read — the over-crediting #839 was built for, now demonstrated rather than argued. Day 181 (**the third door**): the three `Err` arms of `connect_external_servers` now also call `record_failed_server(kind, id)` into a module-level store, and `external_tool_failure_note` / `drain_failure_note` / `take_external_failure_note` compose and one-shot-drain the **model-facing** note from it — because #841 and #842 both fixed the *user's* view of a failed connect while the model was handed a bare **absence**, which reads as *the capability does not exist* rather than *that server is down*. The note is delivered by a **turn prepend in `prompt.rs`, never the system prompt**: `compose_system_prompt` runs inside `build_agent()`, i.e. **before** `connect_external_servers`, and recomposing afterwards would mean a rebuild — which returns an agent carrying **zero** external connections (#842), trading a missing sentence for missing tools. The **collision-guard skip records nothing, deliberately**, with the reason written at the branch: that is a refusal working as designed and the builtin is still in the tool list, so reporting it unavailable would be the confident-wrong-diagnosis defect (#710's short-circuit, one subsystem over). Full mechanism, the `None`-on-empty regression guard and the out-of-scope list are in the MCP gotchas section.
- `banner.rs` — startup banner, welcome text, git status summary display (extracted from `cli.rs`)
- `hooks.rs` — Hook trait, HookRegistry, AuditHook, HookedTool wrapper, maybe_hook helper. Day 166 (#751): `.yoyo/audit.jsonl` has exactly **one** writer per completed tool call — the prompt event-stream tool-end handlers in `src/prompt.rs` (`PromptEventState::handle_tool_execution_end` for the display path, `handle_stream_json_events` for `--output-format json`), each keyed by `tool_call_id` and each recording the **real** elapsed duration and the real `!is_error`. `AuditHook` stays registered but is deliberately observe-only and writes nothing: `post_execute` receives only the tool name, params and output string, so the second entry it used to write had to fabricate `duration_ms: 0` and `success: true` — doubling every count mined off the log and reporting failed tool calls as successes (pinned by the source-level test `test_hooks_never_writes_to_the_audit_log` in `hooks.rs`). Day 180 (#844): a hook's **stderr is bounded before it enters the conversation** by the pure, table-tested `cap_hook_stderr(raw, head, tail)` — `HOOK_STDERR_HEAD_BYTES` / `HOOK_STDERR_TAIL_BYTES`, 4096 each, **deliberately the same budget** `CAPTURE_HEAD_BYTES`/`CAPTURE_TAIL_BYTES` give `/run`, with the const doc comment back-referencing it rather than inventing a new number. The measured defect: `ShellHook::run_command` read stderr with an unbounded `read_to_string` and returned it, and that string reaches the model verbatim via `PostHookResult::with_feedback` — so a user-authored `sh -c` hook printing megabytes could overflow the context and wedge the session. **The asymmetry is the tell and is why it stayed invisible: the same function already capped its *input*** (`TOOL_OUTPUT` at `output.chars().take(1000)`), i.e. the bounded direction was the one yoyo controls (its own tool output going *into* a hook) and the unbounded one was arbitrary user output coming *out* of a hook and into the conversation — an existing cap plausibly reads as "this function handles size". Hooks were the one output path in the repo that was missed; every sibling is bounded (`/run` `CappedCapture` 4KB+4KB, `/bg` 256KB, `!` bang `BANG_CAPTURE_MAX_BYTES` 8KB, `/ast` `AST_MAX_OUTPUT_LINES`). Transferred class with independent confirmation: Claude Code v2.1.247 (2026-08-26) shipped the same fix. **Head *and* tail, not tail-only** — a hook's first error line is the actionable one while its last lines are the summary, the same reason `/run` chose head+tail — with the cut **marked in band** (`… [yoyo: N bytes elided — hook stderr is capped at H head + T tail bytes]` on its own line between the halves), because a silent elision is the bug. **At or under budget the string is byte-identical**, which is every hook anyone has today and the whole regression surface, asserted with a full-string `assert_eq!` rather than a `contains`. Neither cut is ever taken by raw byte index (rule #250): the head walks *back* and the tail walks *forward* to an `is_char_boundary`, so a character straddling either cut is dropped **whole** — pinned by a 4-byte-`🐙` fixture whose 10-byte cuts land mid-character, asserted as a property (both halves are whole chars) rather than a hand-counted char count. The boundary is pinned on **both** sides (exactly at budget → untouched; one byte over → truncated), since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green, and the marker's dropped-byte count is asserted to agree with what was actually dropped — an in-band marker that lies is worse than none. **The cap is applied once, at capture time**, so every consumer of `run_command`'s returned `(code, String)` inherits it; deliberately **not** repeated at the `with_feedback` call site, because a cap that had to be repeated there would be in the wrong place. `run_command` spawns real processes, so it is pinned only by a deliberately **weak** source-level guard (needle assembled at runtime so it cannot match itself, body sliced between `fn run_command(` and the `impl Hook` block) whose own doc comment states the limit: it proves the call is *present*, never that its result is used. Both positive controls were **run rather than assumed**, serially (two file-mutating controls in one parallel block raced once and one falsely passed): neutering the capper fails exactly the four over-budget tests and leaves the byte-identical pass-through green, and deleting the call site fails exactly the source guard. **Two things deliberately not covered, named rather than implied:** (1) the **pipe-buffer question** — whether a hook writing faster than we read can fill the pipe and deadlock before `try_wait` returns is a *different mechanism* from conversation overflow, so the 5s timeout and the read loop's structure are untouched; (2) **stdout**, which is `Stdio::null()` and never reaches the conversation. The existing 1000-char `TOOL_OUTPUT` input cap is correct and unchanged.
- `tools.rs` — StreamingBashTool, RenameSymbolTool, AskUserTool, TodoTool, WebSearchTool, tool builders, SharedState wiring for sub-agents. Day 170 (#783): `RenameSymbolTool::execute` puts the paths it actually wrote (`RenameResult.written`) on the tool result's `details` under `"files_written"` — the tool has no `SessionChanges` handle, so the written set is *reported* here and recorded by `prompt::record_rename_tool_writes`; see the `commands_rename.rs` bullet for why the recording cannot happen off the tool's arguments. Day 180 (sub-agent model fallback — the decorator itself, its predicate and what it refuses to fire on are documented in the `tool_wrappers.rs` bullet; this is the **wiring**): `build_sub_agent_tool` and `build_sub_agent_tool_at_depth` return `Box<dyn AgentTool>` instead of a concrete `SubAgentTool`, because the returned tool is now *either* the bare tool or the decorated pair — the one non-test caller (`agent_builder.rs:635`) was updated in the same diff (its redundant `Box::new` removed, the session cap moved outside) and the nested-recursion push became `Arc::from`. **One builder, so the sub-agent's contract cannot drift between the two attempts:** `sub_agent_tool_for(config, provider, model, api_key, child_tools, shared_state)` is the single place a `SubAgentTool` is constructed, so the description, system prompt, child tool set, thinking, `max_turns`, shared state and skills are one statement rather than two copies that agree today. **The gate is default-off by construction and the regression guard IS the common path:** the pure, table-tested `sub_agent_fallback_target(provider, model, fallback_provider, fallback_model) -> Option<(String, String)>` returns `None` when no fallback model is configured (trimmed, empty filtered), and the existing tool is then returned **un-decorated and byte-identical** — that is every user who has not set one and the whole regression surface, pinned by `no_fallback_model_means_no_decoration`. The fallback provider defaults to the primary's when only a model is given, and the target is **also** `None` when it equals the primary `(provider, model)`, because a retry against an identical target is a second identical call and not a fallback (`identical_target_means_no_decoration`). **The key rule is a refusal, not a reuse:** `sub_agent_fallback_key` reuses the key already in hand when the fallback is on the *same* provider, and for a **different** provider requires that provider's own key in the environment (`cli::provider_api_key_env`); when it is absent the decoration is **declined and the bare tool returned**, because handing the primary's credential to another provider guarantees a 401 — same rule and same reason as `AgentConfig::try_switch_to_fallback`. A keyless/local provider (ollama) needs no key and gets `Some(String::new())`, so it is not mistaken for a missing one. **Deliberately deferred rather than half-done:** the second half of the rival's fix — returning the error *type / status / request id / model* to the parent instead of yoyo's opaque summary string — is a separate diagnostic-quality task and was not started here.
- `smart_edit.rs` — SmartEditTool: fuzzy matching for edit_file errors, whitespace-only auto-fix retry, nearest-match line-number hints
- `tool_wrappers.rs` — Tool decorator types (GuardedTool, TruncatingTool, ConfirmTool, ArcGuardedTool, AutoCheckTool, RecoveryHintTool — short-circuits on the `REFUSAL_STEM_*` markers (`is_deterministic_refusal`): a deterministic refusal (read/plan mode, session cap, denied path) is returned verbatim with no `ToolFailureTracker` bump and no recovery hint, so a deliberate guard is neither treated as flakiness nor talked around (#710), ToolFailureTracker, LiteDescriptionTool, SessionCapTool — session-wide 200-call circuit breaker on `web_search`/`sub_agent`, honest error past the cap; the counter is per-wrapper-instance (per agent build), so the budget resets on `/clear` because `/clear` rebuilds the agent — pinned by `test_session_cap_fresh_wrapper_resets_budget`, do not convert to a process-wide static; ReadModeGuardTool — mechanical `/read` AND `/plan` mode enforcement at the tool layer: checks `is_read_mode()` and `commands_plan::is_plan_mode()` at call time, refuses write-class tools (`write_file`, `edit_file`, `rename_symbol`) with an honest error, and blocks bash commands flagged by the destructive-pattern classifier in `safety.rs` OR the write-command detector (`safety::detect_write_command` — write verbs at token boundaries: `touch`, `mkdir`, `mv`, `cp`, `tee`, `truncate`, `chmod`, `sed -i`, `dd of=`, `install`, `ln`, `rsync` (non-dry-run), plus `>`/`>>` redirection outside quoted regions; the refusal names what matched, read-only commands pass); Day 179 (#838) taught that same detector **which git subcommands write**, because the verb an agent uses most was in neither enumeration — `git` is not in `WRITE_VERBS` (adding it would flag `git status`) and `git commit` is a *write*, not *destructive*, so it matched neither branch and `/read` mode — a mode whose whole promise is mechanical enforcement at the tool layer — let the single most consequential write in a user's repository straight through (measured in blind round 82: `detect_write_command("git commit -m 'x'")` → `None`). Fourth instance of "two doors, one policy, one deaf" (#745, #767, #769, #816), here two independent enumerations of *what writes* with the gap living only in the seam between them. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `safety::git_write_subcommand(args)` over the argv **after** the `git` token; it is folded into `detect_write_command` itself rather than called separately, because that function's **only** non-test consumer is this guard (checked, not assumed — `tools.rs`'s hit is a comment), so `tool_wrappers.rs` needed no edit. **The default is the whole safety property and runs fail-closed: `READ_ONLY_GIT_SUBCOMMANDS` is an allow list, and any subcommand not on it is treated as a write** — a git subcommand nobody enumerated is far more likely to write than to be a harmless reader, so read-only is the enumerated set, never the fallback. Two shapes get explicit handling: **global options before the subcommand** (`git -C sub commit`, `git -c user.name=a commit`, `git --no-pager log` — leading `-` tokens are skipped along with the *value* of the ones that take one, or `-C .` would read as subcommand `-C` and the commit would slip through), and **subcommands whose direction depends on an argument** (`config --get` reads / `config x y` writes; `stash list` reads / `stash` writes; `tag`/`branch` bare list while a positional or a delete/move flag writes; `remote -v`, `worktree list`, `reflog`, `symbolic-ref HEAD` read). Deliberately **not** merged with `git::DESTRUCTIVE_GIT_COMMANDS`, which was compared rather than ignored: that list is `#[cfg(test)]`-only, answers a *narrower* property (destructive, not merely writing — it omits `tag`/`branch` on purpose) and defaults the **opposite** way, so sharing one list would break whichever caller lost. Pinned at the **emission point** — the value the guard's Bash arm receives — in both directions, with the read-only cases as the near-miss guard (`/read` is useless if it blocks reading the repo, which is the mode's whole value) and non-git commands asserted byte-identical; positive control **run rather than assumed**: neutering the classifier fails exactly the two write-detecting tests and leaves every near-miss green. **Stated rather than implied — this is a *subcommand* classifier, not a git parser:** a user's `alias.ci = commit` or a `!`-shell alias in their gitconfig, and any git invocation built through a variable, are invisible to it, so "could not check" must not read as "checked; clean"; the plan-mode block is a transparent pass-through while `/plan apply` is executing (`is_plan_apply_active()` — apply needs write access to carry out the plan); transparent pass-through when neither mode is on; FallbackSubAgentTool — Day 180, see below) and helper wrappers. **Day 180 — `FallbackSubAgentTool`, one extra attempt for the `sub_agent` tool when the session's model is unavailable.** The measured gap: `/spawn` clones the parent's chain (`commands_spawn.rs:1667`, `fallback_model: config.fallback_model.clone()`) and so survives a dead model, while `build_sub_agent_tool_at_depth` never built an `AgentConfig` at all — it constructed an `Arc<dyn StreamProvider>` straight from `config.provider`/`config.model`, so **no fallback was reachable from the `sub_agent` tool**, and `try_switch_to_fallback` has exactly **one** non-test caller (`repl.rs:704`). The parent session could survive a first-call model 404 and the sub-agent it dispatched could not. Sixth instance of "two doors, one policy, one deaf" (#745, #767, #769, #816, `/config show`), and the first with independent confirmation from a rival: **Claude Code v2.1.247 (2026-08-26) shipped the same fix the day before**. Whether yoagent already closes this was checked **before** any code was written, because an upstream answer would have made the task pointless; the surface consulted and the reason it does not close the gap are stated once at the **end of this bullet**, under the version marker that pins them. **The decorator, because yoyo does not drive the sub-agent's turn loop** (`SubAgentTool::execute` is yoagent's and cannot be intercepted mid-loop, so the idiomatic seam is the one every other wrapper in this file already uses): it holds a primary and a secondary `Box<dyn AgentTool>` plus both model names, delegates `name`/`label`/`description`/`parameters_schema` to the **primary** so the tool's identity is unchanged, calls the primary, returns `Ok` untouched, and on `Err` asks two questions **in a fixed order**. **Guard first, availability second, and that ordering is the safety property rather than a style choice:** `is_deterministic_refusal` short-circuits before the availability predicate is consulted, because retrying a deliberate refusal (`REFUSAL_STEM_*` — read mode, plan mode, session cap, denied path) on a different model is a **guard bypass, not a fallback**; same short-circuit `RecoveryHintTool` already makes (#710). It is **one extra attempt, never a chain of N**. **The switch is announced, because an invisible model switch is a bug even when it is the right switch** (the `⚡ auto-continuing` rule): a dim stderr line names both models before the retry, and `fallback_switch_note` (pure, so the wording has one statement) is prepended to a successful fallback result as **its own content block at index 0** rather than spliced into an existing one — `content` may carry non-text blocks and a `Vec` insert cannot mangle them. When the fallback fails too, the **fallback's own error** is returned rather than the primary's: it is the more recent and more actionable fact, and the stderr line already recorded that a switch happened. **The discrimination is the whole task and it is deliberately narrow.** `is_model_unavailable_error` fires on model-availability shapes only — a standalone `404`, and the word forms `model not found` / `model_not_found` / `not found: model` / `does not exist` / `unknown model` / `invalid model` / `unsupported model` / `model is not supported` / `no such model`. **Auth and rate-limit shapes are checked FIRST and always lose, even when the message also names a model**: `rate limit`, `overloaded`, `capacity`, `quota`, `authentication`, `unauthorized`, `invalid api key`, `permission denied`, `forbidden`, plus `401`/`403`/`429` — rate limits are `prompt_retry_limits.rs`'s job and it has a real reset-time policy, so growing a second one here would be two policies for one question, and a different model on the same broken key fails identically. An **ordinary tool failure inside the sub-agent** (the sub-agent ran fine and the *work* failed) does not fire either, because a different model does not fix it. Status codes go through `prompt_retry::contains_status_code` (made `pub(crate)` with its consumer landing in the same diff) rather than a bare `.contains("404")`, which collides with any other number in an error string — the Day-174 lesson, reused rather than re-derived. Pinned in **both** directions, and the near-miss side is the dangerous half: a table for each firing shape, plus near-miss guards for every must-not-fire shape (ordinary failure, each auth/rate-limit shape, an auth error that *also* names a model, and every `REFUSAL_STEM_*` refusal), and the decorator itself driven with a call-counting stub inner tool — a model-unavailable error reaches the secondary **exactly once**, an ordinary error and a deterministic refusal reach it **zero** times and are returned verbatim, a success is passed through unchanged with the secondary never called, a fallback failure returns the fallback's own error, and identity is delegated to the primary. **Positive control run rather than assumed:** neutering the predicate to always return `false` fails exactly the fallback tests and leaves every near-miss guard green. **The stated limit: this covers model-availability failures only** — not rate limits, not auth, not an ordinary failure of the delegated work — and it is one extra attempt, never a chain. **The upstream claim this decorator rests on, pinned rather than asserted:** yoagent's `SubAgentTool` does carry a `retry_config` (`crate::retry::RetryConfig`, alongside `from_config` / `from_provider` / `with_model_config` / `with_model`), but that is **same-provider transient retry with no fallback *chain*, and a first-call 404 / model-not-found is not in its retriable class** — which is why this is a yoyo decorator and not a yoagent call. If a later version adds a chain, this whole wrapper becomes redundant and should be deleted rather than kept beside it. **Day 180 (second task) — `DiagnosticSubAgentTool`, so the parent gets the error's *class*, its status and the model, instead of an opaque string.** This is clause two of the same rival fix (Claude Code v2.1.247); clause one shipped that morning as `FallbackSubAgentTool` and wrote clause two down as deferred — *a self-authored abstention clause has no expiry and no owner*, so it was closed rather than re-deferred. **The measured defect:** when a sub-agent died the parent received yoagent's summary string and had to guess, unable to tell a model-availability failure from an auth failure from a rate limit from the delegated work simply not succeeding — and unable to tell **which model** was running, the single most useful fact when a fallback chain is configured. It then decided blind: retry the same dispatch, or abandon work a second model would have finished. **It is the OUTERMOST wrapper and unconditional, and that placement is the whole design rather than a detail.** Putting the diagnostics inside `FallbackSubAgentTool` would have reached only users who configured a fallback model and left everyone else with the opaque string — the *two doors, one policy, one deaf* shape this repo has shipped **six** times (#745, #767, #769, #816, `/config show`, the sub-agent fallback itself), each costing a second session. So `src/tools.rs` wraps the value it finally returns, at **one** site: `Diagnostic(Fallback(SubAgent))` when a fallback is configured, `Diagnostic(SubAgent)` when it is not — one wiring line rather than three failure paths, and it annotates the error that actually **survives** the fallback attempt rather than an intermediate one. `FallbackSubAgentTool`, `sub_agent_fallback_target` and `is_model_unavailable_error`'s firing set needed **no** changes. **`Ok` is returned byte-identically** — every user whose sub-agents succeed, and the entire regression surface — pinned with a whole-value `assert_eq!` rather than a `contains`. **The guard short-circuits first**, exactly as `RecoveryHintTool` (#710) and `FallbackSubAgentTool` do and for the same reason: a `REFUSAL_STEM_*` refusal (read mode, plan mode, session cap, denied path) is already a precise, deliberate sentence, and dressing a guard working as designed in diagnostic scaffolding makes it read as a malfunction — so refusals are returned **verbatim and unannotated**, asserted with a full-string `assert_eq!` for all three stems. **The shape lists are now stated exactly once**: `AUTH_ERROR_SHAPES` / `RATE_LIMIT_ERROR_SHAPES` / `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE_SHAPES` plus their status-code siblings were inline arrays inside `is_model_unavailable_error` and are consts read by the single `classify_sub_agent_error`, with `is_model_unavailable_error` **defined in terms of it** (`matches!(…, ModelUnavailable)`) — so the retry decision and the diagnostic can never disagree about the same string, which two copies would have permitted in both directions. Precedence is unchanged and is the safety property: **auth, then rate limit, then model-availability**, because a message can carry an auth word *and* a model name and in that case the model is not the problem. **The composer invents nothing.** `sub_agent_failure_report(model_label, err)` is pure and table-tested, returns the original error **verbatim** plus one short bracketed tail, and reports only what is observable: the class, the model configuration, and an HTTP status **only when one is actually present** — read through `prompt_retry::contains_status_code`, never a bare `.contains`, so `prompt is too long: 402134 tokens` reports **no** status (the Day-174 defect, pinned as its own row). **`Unclassified` is a real value and is always printed**, spelled out as *an ordinary failure of the delegated work — a different model will not help*: that is exactly as actionable as any named class, and omitting it silently or dressing it as a named one is the confident-wrong-diagnosis defect. **The request-id finding, recorded rather than shipped:** the rival's fix lists one, and no error string this repo has observed carries a request id in a recognisable shape — so there is **no request-id field at all**, and a test asserts the report never mentions one. Emitting `request_id: unknown` would render *could not check* as *checked*. **The stated limit, in the label itself rather than only here:** with a fallback configured this wrapper sits outside `FallbackSubAgentTool` and **cannot observe which of the two attempts produced the surviving error**, so `sub_agent_model_label` names the *configuration* (``primary`` with fallback ``secondary``) and says outright that the attribution is not available — naming the wrong model is worse than naming both. Threading the real answer out of the fallback decorator is a larger change and was deliberately not started. Only the `Failed` variant is annotated and the variant is preserved: `NotFound`/`InvalidArgs` are yoagent's own dispatch/argument errors rather than the outcome of a sub-agent run, and `Cancelled` carries no payload. **Positive control run rather than assumed:** neutering `sub_agent_failure_report` to return its input unchanged fails exactly **4** tests — all four annotation tests — while every pass-through, refusal, label, classifier and identity guard stays green. <!-- yoagent-version-claim: 0.18.1 --> **Day 182 — the same wrapper now reads the `Ok` path too, because a sub-agent cut short by its turn budget was returning a truncated answer that looked finished.** `src/tools.rs:1381` builds every sub-agent with `.with_max_turns(25)`, and hitting that bound makes `SubAgentTool::execute` return **`Ok`, not `Err`** — a bound is not a failure, which is yoagent's own reading. So **both** Day-180 decorators, which branch on `Err`, were **structurally blind** to it: `grep -n "MaxTurns\|max_turns"` over this file returned **zero**. The parent model was handed mid-thought prose with no indication it was mid-thought, and the failure direction is the expensive one — a partial answer that reads as complete gets acted on. **The signal already existed and had no reader, which is the whole shape of the fix rather than a detail:** yoagent's `extract_final_text` folds `stopped_notice` into the returned text as `"{text}\n\n{stop}"`, where the marker is `format!("{AGENT_STOPPED_PREFIX} {reason}]")` and the reason comes from `ExecutionTracker::check_limits()` (e.g. `Max turns reached (25/25)`). Nothing on the yoyo side looked at it — *available* is not *legible*, the same axis as #841/#842/#848/#851/#852 and the third MCP door. **So nothing detects truncation here; the existing marker is made unmissable.** The decision half is the pure, table-tested `sub_agent_partial_notice(text) -> Option<String>`, which matches `yoagent::agent_loop::AGENT_STOPPED_PREFIX` **by reference rather than re-spelling it**, so an upstream rename is a compile error instead of a silently dead match. **`None` means the result is returned byte-identically** — every sub-agent that finishes normally, the entire regression surface, pinned by `ordinary_success_is_byte_identical_and_gains_no_block` with a whole-string `assert_eq!` **and** an assertion that the block count did not move. When it fires the note is inserted as **its own `Content::Text` block at index 0**, never spliced into an existing one (`content` may carry non-text blocks and a `Vec` insert cannot mangle them) — the same rule `fallback_switch_note` follows. The wording states the three things the raw bracketed marker does not: the result is **partial**; this is **not** a failure of the delegated work and **not** a provider error (the confident-wrong-diagnosis rule — a bound firing as designed must not read as a crash, the same short-circuit `loop_detected_notice` and `RecoveryHintTool` make); and the actionable consequence — **re-dispatch with a narrower subtask, and do not treat the text as a completed answer**. **The guard short-circuits first**, as in both Day-180 decorators: a `REFUSAL_STEM_*` refusal is returned **verbatim and unannotated** even when its text *also* carries the stop marker (all three stems, `assert_eq!`, in `a_refusal_on_the_ok_path_is_returned_verbatim_even_carrying_the_marker`) — a deliberate refusal dressed in partial-result scaffolding reads as a malfunction. **`LOOP_ABORT_PREFIX` deliberately gets no handler, and the reason is written at the code rather than only here:** it *starts with* the same `[Agent stopped:` bytes, but yoagent's `extract_error` turns a loop abort into a `ToolError::Failed`, so it never arrives as `Ok` — matching on the `Ok` path only is correct **because** of that, not in spite of it. **The `Err` path is untouched**: `classify_sub_agent_error`, `sub_agent_failure_report`, `is_model_unavailable_error` and the whole `FallbackSubAgentTool` precedence chain keep their exact behaviour, and their tests were not edited. **No new decorator and no wiring change in `src/tools.rs`** — that is the entire reason it lives in the wrapper that is already outermost and already wired at one site. **Positive control run rather than assumed, and serially** (a parallel pair of file-mutating controls raced once and one falsely passed): neutering the notice to always return `None` fails **exactly 3** tests — `partial_notice_table`, `partial_notice_states_the_three_things_the_raw_marker_does_not` and `a_result_cut_short_by_a_bound_is_annotated_at_block_zero` — while all 145 others stay green, including both near-miss guards and every Err-path diagnostic test; restoring returns 148/148. **The limit, stated plainly rather than implied: this makes truncation legible, it does not resume the work.** The sub-agent is still stopped, the missing remainder is still missing and unrecoverable from this result, and the parent still has to decide what to do — the note only stops the parent mistaking a bound for an answer. Both yoagent claims above (where the marker comes from; that a loop abort cannot reach the `Ok` path) are pinned to `Cargo.lock` by the `yoagent-version-claim` marker this bullet's line already carries, and by two more in `src/tool_wrappers.rs` itself.
- `rtk.rs` — RTK (Rust Token Killer) detection, proxy integration, output compression. Day 169 (blind round 56, a never-forecast file — this was an **unpredicted find while reading for h2**, not a graded bet, and the round's ledger says so): the one-time detection notice in `maybe_prefix_rtk` now goes through the pure `rtk_announcement(quiet, plain) -> Option<&'static str>` — `None` under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. It was the only stderr notice in the codebase that consulted neither, so `--quiet` still printed it and `--screen-reader` still got the `📦`; the two conventions ~15 other sites follow were skipped here because the announcement is buried inside a command-rewriting function rather than in a display module. The `RTK_ANNOUNCED` swap still happens first, so the notice stays once-per-process in every mode. Pinned by `test_rtk_announcement_respects_quiet_and_plain_output`, which asserts the literal string the call site prints in all three states (the `eprintln!` itself takes no other input) — the earlier round-56 grade line claimed this fix had landed when it had not, and the correction line in `dreams/experiments.jsonl` records that rather than quietly replacing it.
- `update.rs` — version comparison (`version_is_newer`) and update checking (`check_for_update`) against GitHub releases
- `safety.rs` — bash command safety analysis, destructive pattern detection, and (Day 165) the secret-redaction primitives `REDACTED`/`SECRET_PATTERNS`/`redact_secrets` — moved here verbatim from `gasp.rs` so they are compiled and tested by the **default** `cargo test` run. Two consumers: `prompt_budget::write_audit_entry` (always compiled) masks every string leaf of the tool **arguments** it writes to `.yoyo/audit.jsonl`, which `scripts/evolve.sh` pushes to the public `audit-log` branch; and, under `--features gasp`, the GASP recorder's summarizer. It is a mask, not a guarantee — a novel secret shape passes through, and tool **output** is not written to `audit.jsonl` today, so this is not a claim that the audit log is secret-free. Day 179 (**blind round 82** — the darkest security-relevant room, 36 snapshots unobserved and never studied; **1 hit / 3 miss**, and the fix came from an *unpredicted find while grading*, not from a bet): `redact_secrets` masked the **bare** assignment `export API_KEY=hunter2secret` and did **not** mask `export API_KEY="hunter2secret"`, `export API_KEY='hunter2secret'`, or the JSON form `{"api_token": "hunter2secret"}`. Cause: the value run `[^\s'\x22]+` cannot *start* on a quote, so a quoted value matched nothing at all. **Quoting a secret is the dominant shell form**, and a tool argument reaching `write_audit_entry` is routinely JSON — so the mask guarding the **public** `audit-log` branch was covering the rarer half. The tell was in the fixtures, not the code: every pre-existing assignment case (`GITHUB_TOKEN=abc123def456`, `password: hunter2hunter2`) used an unquoted value, so the discriminator was tested only on the side that fires. The fix adds an optional quote on **both** sides of the separator (`['\x22]?\s*[=:]\s*['\x22]?…['\x22]?`), the leading one covering a quoted *name* so the JSON shape resolves too; quotes are consumed into the match and dropped from the output, since this is a mask and not a faithful reproduction. **Deliberately not widened, and the reason is written at the pattern rather than only here:** the separator stays `[=:]`, so a flag-style credential (`--password "hunter2"`, space-separated) is **still missed** — accepting whitespace as a separator would mask the next word after any prose occurrence of "password"/"key"/"token", and that prose flows through this same function on its way to the audit log, so it trades a narrow miss for a broad false positive. Pinned at the **emission point** (the `String` a caller receives, never the regex one layer below) with both **near-miss guards** beside it — the bare form and the pre-existing fixture must still mask **byte-identically**, asserted with `assert_eq!` rather than a `contains`, and innocent text must pass through untouched. Positive control run rather than assumed: the new test fails against the old regex and passes against the new one, with both near-miss guards green in each direction. **Three findings the round recorded rather than fixed** (one defect per round): (1) `/read` and `/plan` mode do **not** block `git commit` — `WRITE_VERBS` is `[touch, mkdir, mv, cp, tee, truncate, install, ln, chmod]` plus `sed -i`/`perl -i`/`dd of=`/`rsync`, and `git` is in neither that table nor the sibling *destructive*-pattern classifier, so both branches of `ReadModeGuardTool`'s bash check pass it through (measured, both `None`); filed as **#838**, because a finding recorded only in CLAUDE.md is a reader surface, not a scheduler surface. **Superseded the same day, recorded rather than erased — and the filing is why:** #838 was **fixed** by the next task (see the `tool_wrappers.rs` bullet for the classifier), so "recorded rather than fixed" is true of *round 82* and stays as the record of what that round did, not of the code. The clause worth keeping is the one about surfaces, and it is now evidence rather than a claim: this finding is the one of the three that got filed into the **scheduler** surface, and it is the one that got fixed within the day. (2) **Unverified, recorded rather than claimed:** `git -C/tmp/outside status` (attached short-option form) returns `None` from `detect_git_redirection_escape`; whether git itself accepts that form was **not** established, so it is not called an escape — naming a hole without checking would be inventing one. (3) The three *losing* bets are worth as much as the hit and are recorded so a later session does not re-derive them: the assignment pattern **is** case-insensitive (`(?i)`, so lowercase `password=` masks), `detect_git_redirection_escape` **does** handle both the space-separated *and* `=`-attached forms of `-C`/`--git-dir`/`--work-tree`, and `detect_redirection` **does** carry an explicit fd-duplication branch, so `cargo test 2>&1` is not mistaken for a file write. **The pre-registered variable held at n=1:** the one `derivable: true` bet hit and all three choice-dependent bets missed — and all three missed in the *same* direction, each asserting a gap in a guard past-me had closed deliberately, with a comment sitting on the branch the bet said was absent. **Scope, stated so `StudyState::Graded` is not over-read: three named surfaces only** (`redact_secrets`/`SECRET_PATTERNS`, `detect_write_command`, `detect_git_redirection_escape`), not the 3490-line file — the destructive-pattern classifier, `strip_quoted_regions` and `path_escapes_root` were not studied.
- `cli.rs` — CLI argument parsing, subcommands, configuration (delegates `--help` text to `help.rs`). Value-taking flags are validated by `check_flag_values` (pure, returns `Vec<FlagIssue>`; printing/exiting stays in `parse_args`): one left-to-right scan that skips the token it just consumed as a value, so **every** occurrence of a repeatable flag is checked, not just the first. Day 153: the old `position()`-per-flag loop validated only the first `--allow`/`--deny`/`--mcp`/… while `collect_repeatable_flag` consumes all of them, so a later occurrence could silently swallow the next flag as its value — the eaten flag never fired and `warn_unknown_flags` could not warn about it (silent wrong-op + silent no-op). The same scan also stops a flag *name* used as a *value* (`yoyo -p '--model'`) from being mistaken for a flag missing its value. Day 166 (#748) added the **project-config trust boundary** at `parse_mcp_and_openapi_config`, the single seam where the three MCP sources merge: the pure `gate_mcp_sources(cli_servers, config_servers, config_server_configs, project_local, trusted)` drops **only** the two file-config sources (`mcp = [...]` and `[mcp_servers.*]`) and **only** when `config::loaded_config_is_project_local()` is true and `--trust-project` was not passed — `--mcp` flags typed on the command line and home/XDG config entries are byte-identical to before, and when nothing is dropped the merge order is unchanged. A repo's `.yoyo.toml` could previously name an arbitrary local command and yoyo would start that process during normal startup with no prompt and no display of what was about to run; `--safe-mode` mitigated it but was opt-in, i.e. the default was wrong. Refusals are announced, not silent: `project_mcp_refusal_message` (pure) prints one stderr block naming the **resolved command string of every refused server** plus the two escape hatches (`--trust-project` to start them this run, `--safe-mode` to disable all project customizations), silent under `is_quiet()` and glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. `--trust-project` is in `KNOWN_FLAGS`, takes no value (so it is deliberately *not* in the value-taking list), and its state lives beside `set_continue_on_silence`/`is_continue_on_silence` in this file. Day 172 (#794): `set_continue_on_silence()` is still called from exactly **one** site in `parse_args`, but that site now has **two** sources OR'd together — the `--continue-on-silence` flag on the command line, **or** `config::parse_continue_on_silence_from_config(&file_config)` reading the `continue_on_silence` key of the loaded config file. Neither present → off, exactly as before, so the product default is unchanged; there is deliberately **no** disable flag, because an OR is the whole rule and a fourth precedence value was not needed. The site sits beside the existing config-driven flags (`parse_quiet_from_config`, `parse_no_bell_from_config`) and runs after `load_config_file` and before any agent build. The key is **not** part of the project-config trust boundary above and must not be added to it: it grants no privilege — the worst case is extra API turns in a repo you already chose to run yoyo in — unlike an MCP command or a `permissions.allow` entry. Day 166 (#749 item 3) extended the same boundary to the **privilege-granting half of `[permissions]`**, which was the more urgent gap: a stranger's repo shipping `allow = ["curl *"]` silently auto-approved those bash commands for the whole session, and unlike an MCP entry the effect is invisible until a command runs without asking. The pure `gate_project_permissions(permissions, from_cli_flags, project_local, trusted) -> PermissionGateOutcome` moves `permissions.allow` into `refused_allow` **only** when the config is project-local, `--trust-project` was not passed, and the permissions did not come from CLI flags (a `--allow` the user typed is the user's own word); every other input is a byte-identical pass-through. The gate is **deliberately asymmetric, and that asymmetry is the point**: fields are sorted by which direction they move privilege, and only the granting one is refused — `permissions.deny` is kept verbatim, and `dir_restrictions.allow`/`deny` are not touched at all, because the file-tool default is *unrestricted* so an allow-list can only narrow. A naive mirror of `gate_mcp_sources` would leave yoyo **less** confined than the repo asked for: a regression dressed as a security fix. A repo may always restrict yoyo further; it may not loosen it. Refusal is announced by the pure `project_permission_refusal_message` (names every refused pattern verbatim, both escape hatches, and states that deny/dir restrictions are still in force; silent under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`). **Both** entry points are wired, because a per-token pass is not a per-entry-point pass: `parse_permission_and_dir_config` in this file, and the `"permissions"` subcommand arm in `dispatch_sub.rs`, which parses the raw config independently to display effective permissions — a display showing refused patterns as active would be a lie in the one place a user goes to check (that arm also reads `--trust-project` straight off `args`, since `try_dispatch_subcommand` runs before `set_trust_project`). Day 175 (#820) closed the **fourth door on the same boundary**: `parse_args`' shell-hook parse (`crate::hooks::parse_hooks_from_config`) sat two lines below the `gate_project_permissions` call and read none of the provenance answer already computed there, while `hooks.rs` hands each configured string to `Command::new("sh").arg("-c")` on the first matching tool call — so cloning a stranger's repo whose `.yoyo.toml` carried `hooks.pre.bash = "…"` executed it during a normal session with no prompt and no display. The pure `gate_project_hooks(hooks, project_local, trusted) -> HookGateOutcome` refuses **only** when the config is project-local and `--trust-project` was not passed; home/XDG configs, trusted runs and repos with no hooks are byte-identical pass-throughs. **Both phases are refused, `pre` and `post` alike, and that is the direction rule applied correctly rather than a lapse in it** — #820's own body (which I wrote) argued a blocking `hooks.pre.*` *restricts* yoyo and so should survive like `permissions.deny`; that does not survive contact with the mechanism, because a pre-hook produces its blocking effect **by running arbitrary shell first**, so the narrowing cannot be had without the execution. The Day-166 asymmetry sorts *declarative* values yoyo interprets (a deny pattern, a path list — kept verbatim, since a repo may always confine yoyo further); a hook's entire content is **executable code**, so direction is decided by what the entry **is**, not by what it does after it runs. Refusal is announced by the pure `project_hook_refusal_message`, which names every refused hook in the shape it has in the file (`pre.bash = <command>`, so the user can grep for it) with the command echoed verbatim — capped at `REFUSAL_HOOK_CMD_MAX_BYTES = 400` on a **char** boundary with the cut marked in band — plus both escape hatches and the outright claim that **nothing was executed**; silent under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. The second door needed **no** code: `/hooks` (`commands_config::handle_hooks`) is reached only from `dispatch.rs:829` passing `ctx.agent_config.shell_hooks`, which `main.rs:943` builds from `config.shell_hooks` — the gated vec — so it (and `/config show`'s `hook_count`) inherits the gate; traced rather than assumed, and duplicating a gate that already flows through would be ceremony, not coverage. Day 178 (#749 item 1) gave that whole boundary its **second door: a persisted per-directory trust store**, because `--trust-project` applied to one run and a capability reachable only by retyping a flag is the shape that trains people to alias it away — i.e. back to the unsafe default, by habit (#749 says so out loud, and Claude Code / Cursor / VS Code all ask once per folder and remember). `set_trust_project()` is still called from exactly **one** site in `parse_args`, which now has **four** sources (the fourth landed the same day, see below): `--trust-project` (unchanged, one run, byte-identical), the new `--trust-project-always` (this run **and** `config_paths::remember_trusted_dir(cwd)`), or — with neither flag — `config_paths::dir_is_trusted(cwd)`. It is the same two-source-OR shape as `continue_on_silence` (#794) and `wait_for_reset` (Day 178), and the flag is in `KNOWN_FLAGS` taking no value (so deliberately *not* in the value-taking list `check_flag_values` scans). **A stored decision grants the full boundary** — project MCP servers, `[permissions].allow`, shell hooks (#820) and `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` (#761) all consult `is_trust_project()`, so recording a directory turns all four on there for every later session. **Both events are announced, because a silent trust grant is a bug even when it is the right grant** (the `⚡ auto-continuing` rule): the pure `trust_remembered_message` names the directory recorded, the store file's full path and that removing the line revokes it, and the pure `trusted_by_store_message` — the important one — says a run is live because of a `--trust-project-always` typed weeks ago and names the file, so a user can never discover that state by accident; both are silent under `is_quiet()` and glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. **The default is unchanged and pinned:** neither flag and no store file → the trust path is unreachable and output is byte-identical. There is deliberately **no un-trust flag** — revoking is deleting a line from a plain-text file, and both messages name its path, so a fourth switch would be ceremony. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #749 item 2):** this bullet used to end "**Deliberately not covered (follow-up #749 item 2): there is still no interactive 'do you trust this folder?' prompt**, so trust is only ever granted by a flag the user types — which is also why a non-interactive run always takes the safe answer." That was true for one task. The **fourth source** is now the prompt itself, and it is consulted **last**, only in the `else` branch where neither flag was passed and the store did not answer: yoyo **asks**, once, and honours the answer. The clause worth keeping from the superseded sentence is the second half, which is still exactly true and is now the guard rather than a consequence — **a non-interactive run always takes the safe answer**, because `should_prompt_for_trust` requires `stdin().is_terminal() && stderr().is_terminal()`, so the evolve loop (stdin piped), `yoyo -p` (piped) and CI (neither) all skip the question in silence and proceed untrusted exactly as before. The decision half is four pure functions in `config_paths.rs` (see that bullet); `cli.rs` gains only the tty check, one `read_line`, and the three-arm match — `Always` trusts this run **and** calls `remember_trusted_dir`, reusing the existing `trust_remembered_message` rather than writing a second one; `Once` trusts this run and prints nothing extra (the user just said yes, they know); `No` prints `trust_declined_message`, which **is** informational and so *is* silent under `is_quiet()`. **Placement is load-bearing and was verified, not assumed:** the block sits at ~`cli.rs:1436`, ahead of `gate_project_permissions` (~1758), `gate_mcp_sources` (~1792) and `gate_project_hooks` (~1805), so a `y` is honoured *this run* instead of arriving after the refusals it was meant to lift. **What did not change:** trust still grants exactly what it granted — all four gates are untouched and still decide that; the store format is unchanged; there is still no un-trust flag and still **no config key**, because trust must never be grantable by a file the repo itself ships, which is the whole reason the store is user-level (a project-local `.yoyo.toml` that could answer its own trust question would defeat the boundary it is being asked about). The prompt fires at most once per process, and only when the config is **project-local** and `project_trust_grants` is **non-empty** — so a home/XDG config, or a project `.yoyo.toml` that grants nothing (this repo's own, which sets `continue_on_silence`), is byte-identical to before.
- `cli_config.rs` — CLI constants (VERSION, thresholds, SYSTEM_PROMPT — now structured as named sectioned behavioral defaults: role, evidence/anti-fabrication, search craft, change discipline, bounded verification), Config struct, ContextStrategy/OutputFormat enums, effective context token state (extracted from `cli.rs`, re-exported by it)
- `commands.rs` — slash command dispatch, grouped /help, custom command discovery (loads user-defined `.md` files from `.yoyo/commands/` and `~/.yoyo/commands/`)
- `dispatch.rs` — REPL `/command` routing (`dispatch_command`), `CommandResult`, `DispatchContext`
- `dispatch_sub.rs` — CLI subcommand routing (`try_dispatch_subcommand` for `yoyo <subcmd>`), `flag_value`, `FlagValueCheck`, `require_flag_value`. Routes `yoyo risk [snapshot|validate|history|predict|accuracy|effectiveness|--all]` to `commands_risk::handle_risk`, making the risk subsystem callable from any non-interactive context (the prerequisite for the harness or a human to record daily risk snapshots where the DREAM.md measurement data actually accumulates). Day 165: the **bare-word path carries a near-miss guard** — before Day 165 a single unrouted word (`yoyo tokens`, `yoyo statsu`) fell straight through to the single-prompt path and was executed as an **LLM prompt** with write-capable tools attached, silently spending API tokens to "answer" a mistyped command; the REPL side had `commands::suggest_command`/`closest_match` and the sibling CLI entry path had no guard at all (the Day-162 "a policy's reach is set by the wrapper it was written for" shape). `bare_word_arg` (pure) admits only the unambiguous shape — exactly `yoyo <word>`, `args.len() == 2`, no leading `-`, no whitespace — and `bare_word_near_miss` (pure: no I/O, no exit) returns the message or `None`. Candidates are **derived, never hand-listed**: `ROUTED_SUBCOMMANDS` (37 verbs — count corrected Day 177 blind round 77; it read 36 while `security` sat uncounted in the const — a second copy of the dispatcher's match arms) plus every `commands::KNOWN_COMMANDS` entry the dispatcher does *not* route (`repl_only_commands`), and the distance budget reuses `commands::closest_match` at the REPL's own edit-distance threshold (`near_miss_threshold` — distance ≤2 for words of ≤5 chars, ≤3 for longer; measured over chars where `suggest_command` measures bytes) rather than hand-rolling a second distance function. It mirrors that threshold only — `suggest_command`'s unique-prefix shortcut is not reproduced, so a short prefix that is far in edit distance stays a prompt. Two drift guards run in **opposite directions** over the dispatcher's source (`test_routed_subcommands_matches_the_dispatcher`): a verb routed but missing from the table would make the guard fire on a word that works, and a verb left in the table after being unrouted would make the guard stay silent on a word that is now a paid prompt. The one call site is in `main.rs`, immediately after `apply_cli_flags` and **before** `parse_args`/any agent build, so a fired guard prints to stderr and exits 2 having spent zero tokens. **What it deliberately does not do:** it does **not** add `tokens`/`cost`/`context` (or any other REPL-only verb) as real subcommands — an exact REPL-only name is told where the command lives (`/tokens`) and given the `yoyo -p "tokens"` escape hatch, and wiring those verbs as subcommands is separate work; a single word that resembles **nothing** (`zzqqx`) is still sent as a prompt, unchanged; and multi-word args (`yoyo do the thing`), flagged args, and `-p` are untouched byte-for-byte, because bare positional args as a prompt is a real feature. Day 166 (#754): the `"goal"` arm refuses `check` **before** dispatch — the pure `goal_args_need_session` mirrors `handle_goal`'s own parse (the exact-equality `check` arm reached via `input.strip_prefix("/goal").trim()`, `src/commands_goal.rs:157`) and returns early, so a non-interactive invocation whose only output is a refusal does **not** execute the project-local verify command from `.yoyo/goal_verify.md`; previously `handle_goal` ran that command while building its prompt and the refusal ("requires an interactive session") printed *after* the side effect it appeared to be declining, so the message's implied "nothing ran" is now true and stated outright. The post-dispatch `SendToAgent` check is kept as a belt-and-braces fallback for a future handler change. The **trust gate** for `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` — the same project-authored-file question as #748/#749 — was filed forward from here on #754 and **landed on Day 167 as #761**, in `commands_goal.rs` (`gate_goal_verify` / `goal_verify_refusal_message` / the `verify_command_if_permitted` seam — see that bullet); this pre-dispatch refusal is kept beside it as the earlier and cheaper stop for the non-interactive door, since it returns before `handle_goal` is ever entered and so needs no gate consultation at all. Day 169: `"ast"` and `"def"` arms were added (34 → 36 verbs), both delegating to the existing `commands_ast_grep::handle_ast_grep` / `commands_search::handle_def` with args re-joined verbatim by the pre-existing `join_args_as_command` — the near-miss guard only inspects the two-token `yoyo <word>` shape, so `yoyo ast '$X.unwrap()' src/` and `yoyo def handle_goal` were falling through to the single-prompt path and spending API tokens; four of the six search commands (`find`/`grep`/`index`/`outline`) already had doors, making this the third "two doors, one works" instance in four days (#745, #767).
- `help.rs` — canonical source for all help content: `cli_help_text()` (`--help` output), `/help` REPL help, per-command detailed help (re-exports data from `help_data.rs`)
- `help_data.rs` — static command help text and short descriptions: `command_help()`, `command_short_description()` (pure data, extracted from `help.rs`). **Data only** — the drift-guard test suite that used to live at the bottom of this file was moved verbatim to `src/help_data_guards.rs` (Day 166), because the file was 1995 lines against a fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` and the next fix that has to land here (#757, `/pr` number-first vs verb-first) costs more than the 5 lines of headroom that were left.
- `help_data_guards.rs` — `#[cfg(test)]`-only module (declared `#[cfg(test)] mod help_data_guards;` in `main.rs`, so it never reaches a release build) holding the subcommand-drift guard suite moved out of `help_data.rs` on Day 166 — a **pure move**: same tests, same names, same fixture, the only edit being `use super::*;` → `use crate::help_data::*;`. Test paths are now `help_data_guards::tests::…`. Its concern was always different from the data half's: these guards read *other modules'* source text off disk to compare three copies of the subcommand vocabulary.
