Two subcommand-drift guards run over the same `subcommand_tables()` fixture (20 `(cmd, prefix, table)` triples), in **opposite directions**: `test_every_subcommand_table_is_documented_in_usage_form` compares the completion table against the prose help (copy 2 vs copy 3), and Day 164's `test_every_subcommand_table_token_appears_in_its_dispatcher` compares it against the **dispatcher** (copy 2 vs copy 1) — the direction both real bugs lived in (`/todo list`, #702; `/map --depth`, Day 164), and the direction a prose check is structurally blind to, because the help entry documents the phantom too. It reads each command's dispatcher module (`dispatcher_source`, a total match — a new entry in `subcommand_tables()` without one panics rather than being skipped) via `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, truncates at the `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` marker (built at runtime so it can't match itself; a bare `#[cfg(test)]` split would blank `src/git.rs`, which carries such guards inside real functions), strips `*_SUBCOMMANDS` table declarations (13 of the 20 tables live in the same file as their dispatcher — without this the token would be found in the table itself and the test would be vacuously green), and asserts each non-flag token appears as a quoted literal `"token"`. **What it does not catch, plainly:** it is a *presence* check, not a match-arm parse — a literal appearing anywhere in the non-test half of the module counts as present, even in a printed hint, an error string, or an arm for a different command. Day 164 (#730) added `/checkpoint` and `/context` (18 → 20 triples), and `/checkpoint` is the sharpest live instance of that weakness: `handle_checkpoint` and `handle_fork` share `src/commands_fork.rs`, and `list`/`delete` are arms in *both*, so those two tokens would read as present even if `handle_checkpoint` dropped them — the `"checkpoint"` arm of `dispatcher_source` says so in a comment rather than implying coverage it doesn't have. Verbs routed on a *compound* literal are listed in `KNOWN_INDIRECT`, each with the `file:line` that handles it (currently `/config show|edit|set|get` and `/lint fix`, all routed in `src/dispatch.rs` on the full command string); a bare entry is how a deleted feature keeps breathing.
- `config.rs` — permission config, directory restrictions, MCP server config, TOML parsing helpers. Day 177 (blind round 76, the #1 dark room at 39 snapshots unobserved and never studied — this was an **unpredicted find while falsifying h2**, not a graded bet, and the round's ledger says so): `resolve_path` **expands a leading `~`** before doing anything else. It didn't, and `fs::canonicalize` doesn't either (the shell does that, and a config file never goes through a shell), so a `~/…` string was treated as *relative* and joined onto the cwd — `deny = ["~/.ssh"]` resolved to `$CWD/~/.ssh`, a directory that cannot exist, so the entry could never match. That exact line is the worked example in `docs/src/configuration/permissions.md:111`, which makes this the dangerous direction: a user copies the documented way to fence off their SSH keys, believes it worked, and the file tools reach `~/.ssh` unimpeded. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `expand_tilde_with(path, home)` with the `HOME` lookup in the `expand_tilde` wrapper, and it is **deliberately narrow** — only a bare `~` and a `~/` prefix expand; `~bob/.ssh` is returned verbatim (resolving another account's home needs a passwd lookup this crate does not do, and guessing `<home_parent>/bob` would invent a fence the user never wrote), and a missing/empty `HOME` leaves the path unchanged rather than rewriting it into something that resolves elsewhere. Pinned at the **emission point** — the `Result` a caller of `check_path` receives, never `resolve_path` one layer below — with the near-miss guard beside it (`~/.ssh` must block `$HOME/.ssh/id_rsa` **and** must not fence off the rest of `$HOME`), plus the pure table for the branches that must *not* expand; both emission-point tests were verified to fail against the old body first, and they read the real `HOME` instead of setting it, so no process-global env var is mutated and they need no `#[serial]`. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #823):** this bullet used to say the two-matcher asymmetry "**still stands — `[directories]` has no glob support and this task did not add any**", and ended "**Deliberately not done: globbing was not added to `check_path`.** That is a behaviour change to a security control with asymmetric risk — a glob in `deny` that suddenly starts matching is an improvement, but a glob in `allow` that suddenly starts matching *widens a fence the user is currently living inside*; a test pins that a wildcard entry still matches nothing, so a future 'fix' that quietly adds globbing has to face that assertion." Both sentences were true for one day and are **false as of Day 178**: `[directories]` entries now glob. The Day-177 half of the story is kept below because it is the record of what that day shipped — a *warning*, which my own archive says is the strictly weaker of "make it visible" and "make it work" and must not close the issue — and because the handshake it left behind was **answered on purpose, not bypassed**: the test that pinned the old behaviour (`wildcard_entry_still_matches_nothing`) was **replaced deliberately, not deleted quietly**, and its successor (`wildcard_allow_entry_now_matches_instead_of_denying_everything`) carries the answer in its own doc comment. **What changed, and how narrow it is:** `DirectoryRestrictions::check_path` routes every entry — in **both** the deny loop and the allow `any()` — through the pure, table-tested `directory_entry_matches(entry, entry_resolved, candidate_resolved) -> bool`, with all path resolution left at the existing call site. Two branches, split by the **same** wildcard predicate the startup note uses (`entry_has_glob_meta`, extracted so "what counts as a wildcard" has one statement and the matcher and the note cannot drift apart): an entry with **no** `*`/`?` falls through to the unchanged `path_is_under` and is **byte-identical to before** — essentially every existing user, and the whole regression risk, pinned in both lists by `literal_entries_behave_exactly_as_before`; an entry **with** a wildcard is `glob_match`ed against the resolved candidate **and each of its ancestors**, so `deny = ["*/secrets"]` fences the files *inside* every matching directory (a directory entry already covers everything beneath it, which is what a user writing a path pattern means; `*` also spans `/`, so `secrets/*` reaches deep paths directly and the ancestor walk is for patterns whose wildcard is not in the last component). **Only entries that previously matched nothing change meaning.** No third matcher was written — both halves are the existing `glob_match` and `path_is_under`; `glob_match` itself was **not touched** (every `[permissions]` pattern in the world depends on it) and `[permissions]` is untouched, since it already globbed. **Why widening `allow` is safe here although widening a fence generally is not**, stated in `directory_entry_matches`'s doc comment and not only here: the Day-177 argument is true in general and false in this case, because a wildcard `allow` entry matched **nothing**, so nobody was living inside that fence — they were locked out of every file and the tool was unusable. Going from "refuses everything" to "allows exactly what the user wrote" is not the loosening of a working restriction; it is the difference between broken and working. Pinned at the **emission point** — the `Result` a caller of `check_path` receives, never the helper one layer below: `deny = ["secrets/*"]` blocks `secrets/api/key.txt` (the fails-open hole, closed) **and** does not block `src/main.rs` (the near-miss guard, since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green), `allow = ["src/*"]` permits what it matches and still refuses `/etc/passwd`, deny still overrides a matching allow across the wildcard branch, plus the pure table for the branch decision itself; the positive control was run — with the wildcard branch removed the two glob tests fail. `docs/src/configuration/permissions.md` gained a `Wildcards in [directories]` section stating support with one worked example per direction, using the shapes those tests pin. **The Day-177 warning was corrected, not dropped, and the choice was deliberate** (the two options were "reduced note" or "delete"): the confusing behaviour it described is gone, so a note claiming entries match nothing would itself be the false sentence — but the two blocks still glob *different subjects*, and one genuine trap survives, so one line is still worth printing. It now confirms the entry was read as a **path pattern**, names the list and consequence per entry verbatim, says `*` spans `/`, and names the remaining trap: `glob_match` implements `*` and **not** `?`, so a `?` stays a literal character in either block. What is unchanged from Day 177 and still true: the decision half is the pure `wildcard_directory_entries(&DirectoryRestrictions) -> Vec<(DirectoryList, String)>` (scans `allow` then `deny`, returning offending entries verbatim) and `directory_wildcard_warning(entries, plain) -> Option<String>` — **`None` for an empty slice, the byte-identical common path**, so every user who writes no wildcard sees output unchanged; glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`, silent under `is_quiet()`, mirroring `project_permission_refusal_message`; detection is `*` and `?` only (`*` is what `glob_match` honours, `?` is over-reported on purpose because a user who writes it meant a wildcard and will otherwise get a silent literal match; `[` is deliberately **not** detected, being a plausible literal); and **both** `parse_directories_from_config` call sites stay wired — `parse_permission_and_dir_config` in `cli.rs` (the startup door) and the `"permissions"` subcommand arm in `dispatch_sub.rs` (the door a user walks through *specifically to check what is in force*) — because a per-token pass is not a per-entry-point pass. Day 166 (#748) added **config provenance**: `load_config_file`'s first-wins chain (`./.yoyo.toml` → `~/.yoyo.toml` → XDG) records the winning path in a `OnceLock` at each of its three return rungs (`record_loaded_config_path`), and `loaded_config_is_project_local()` answers "did the *project* supply this config?" from that recorded winner — there is exactly one statement of the search order, never a second precedence ladder to drift against it. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `config_path_is_project_local(path, cwd, home)`: the file must be one of `CONFIG_FILE_NAMES`, resolve (relative paths against `cwd`) to a file whose parent *is* `cwd`, and — pinned explicitly, not left accidental — `cwd == home` returns **false**, because `~/.yoyo.toml` reached as `./.yoyo.toml` is still the user's own config. The `OnceLock` is deliberately write-once: a later `cd` must not retroactively change the provenance the session already acted on. Provenance is only *reported* here; the gate that consumes it lives in `cli.rs`. Day 172 (#794): `parse_continue_on_silence_from_config` joins the `config_flag`-shaped family of boolean readers (`auto_watch`, `quiet`, `no_bell`, …) — **default `false`, opt-in**, so a user who never writes the key gets byte-identical behaviour; three-state unit test (absent → `false`, `= true` → `true`, `= false` → `false`). `"continue_on_silence"` was added to `SETTABLE_KEYS` with its matching `validate_config_value` arm in the same diff, so `/config set continue_on_silence true` validates and `/config get continue_on_silence` reads it back — a key nothing can set through the normal door is half a feature. Its consumer is the single `set_continue_on_silence()` site in `cli.rs`, and this repo's own `.yoyo.toml` sets it (see the `main.rs` bullet for why the config key rather than a flag). Day 178 (self-discovered; transferred class — Claude Code v2.1.246 shipped a *warning* for the same shape): **a `permissions.allow` wildcard sitting before a literal swallowed option tokens.** `glob_match` only requires prefix / suffix / in-order-middle matching, so `allow = ["git * main"]` — which a user writes meaning "the `*` is the subcommand slot" — also matched `git -c core.sshCommand=<arbitrary command> push main`, auto-approving an arbitrary command **for the whole session** from a pattern that looks narrow. **Verified before writing any fix rather than reasoned about**, because the task's own step 0 was to stop if the reading was wrong: a standalone transcription of `glob_match` compiled with `rustc` returned `true` for `git * main` vs `git -c core.sshCommand=curl_evil.sh push main`, and the test added in this diff failed at exactly that row (`left: Some(true), right: None`) before the fix landed. Project-local configs are gated by the trust boundary (#748/#749), so the exposed path is the user's own `~/.yoyo.toml`, which is deliberately ungated — a real path, not a hypothetical one. **`glob_match` was not touched**: every `[permissions]` pattern in the world depends on it and, since #823, `[directories]` consumes it too — changing it would be a semantic change to a security control with two consumers. The fix is the pure `allow_wildcard_swallows_options(pattern, command)`, consulted **only** on the allow branch of `PermissionConfig::check`, and its precision is the whole task since a broader rule breaks legitimate configs: (1) it applies **only** to a **non-trailing** `*` — a non-empty literal segment must follow some `*`, so `cargo *`, `npm run *` and `git commit -m *` are honest "anything goes" patterns and stay **byte-identical to before**, which is essentially every existing user and the whole regression risk; (2) when it applies, the match is rejected if the command carries any whitespace-separated token starting with `-` that does not appear verbatim as a token in the pattern, so `git * --force` still auto-approves `git push --force`. **Rejection means "not auto-approved", never "refused"** — the command falls through to the normal confirmation prompt, which is graceful degradation by design and the reason no warning or refusal message was added; the doc comment says so outright so a later reader does not helpfully add one. **`deny` keeps plain `glob_match`, verbatim, and this asymmetry is written into the code rather than only here:** narrowing an `allow` removes privilege (safe), while narrowing a `deny` makes a fence stop matching — i.e. it **fails open**, so a naive mirror onto the deny branch would be a regression dressed as a security fix. Same asymmetry `cli::gate_project_permissions` already encodes (#749 item 3); `[directories]` is untouched. Pinned at the **emission point** — the `Option<bool>` a caller of `check` receives, never the helper one layer below — with all four near-miss guards beside the two hole rows (trailing wildcard still auto-approves; an option token written verbatim in the pattern still auto-approves; a wildcard-free pattern is untouched; a `deny` pattern still blocks the option-carrying command), since a discriminator tested only on the side that blocks is vacuous green. **Positive control run rather than assumed**: neutering the helper to always return `false` fails the hole row and restoring it returns green. **The limit, stated rather than implied: this narrows `allow` matching only, and it is not a claim that `permissions.allow` is now safe against every option-injection shape** — it closes the wildcard-swallows-options class and nothing wider.
- `context.rs` — project context loading (reads YOYO.md, CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, .cursorrules, .github/copilot-instructions.md), file listing, git status, recently changed files, project-type convention hints
- `conversations.rs` — side, quick, and extended conversation handlers (extracted from `repl.rs`): `build_add_content_blocks`, `handle_side`, `handle_quick`, `handle_extended`
- `providers.rs` — provider constants (KNOWN_PROVIDERS), API key env vars, default/known models per provider
- `format/mod.rs` — Color, constants, utility functions, re-exports, contextual command hints (`HintContext`, `contextual_hint`); context-budget warnings are split into a pure core (`budget_warning_threshold` — the 60/80/90/95 ladder, `max == 0` → `None`; `budget_warning_message` — the four strings) plus `context_budget_warning_with(&AtomicU32, ..)` which takes its escalation state as a parameter, so tests drive local state instead of the process-wide `LAST_WARNED_THRESHOLD`; `context_budget_warning`/`reset_context_budget_warning` keep their exact public behavior and one `#[serial]` test pins that the public pair is still wired to the global. Day 177 (the first payment against `tests/global_state_races.rs`'s debt register) applied that same seam to the file's other three racing tests, in the gate's own stated order of preference — **pass the value explicitly, never `#[serial]`**, which only serialises against other `#[serial]` tests and leaves unmarked *readers* of the same global exposed: `usage_print_line(quiet, verbose, ..) -> Option<String>` (the decision half of `print_usage`; **both** globals it used to read are parameters, and `None` means "print nothing"), `context_usage_line(quiet, used, max) -> Option<String>` (the decision half of `print_context_usage`, with both suppression branches — quiet, and an unknown context window `max == 0` — in one place), and `contextual_hint_with(&mut HashSet<&'static str>, ctx)` (the decision half of `contextual_hint`, taking the shown-hints set as a parameter exactly as `context_budget_warning_with` takes its escalation state). **Production behaviour is byte-identical** — each wrapper is the only global read and preserves the original read order, including `print_usage`'s `!quiet && is_verbose()` short-circuit — and the three tests now drive the pure cores and assert **both** directions instead of the previous "must not panic" no-ops. The wrappers are pinned by a deliberately **weak** source-level test that reads this file's own source (needle built at runtime so it cannot match itself) and asserts each wrapper still calls its global reader; it proves the call is **present**, not that its result is used, and it was verified to fail against a broken wrapper before landing. Two `format/mod.rs` tests stay in the register with the reason written beside them — `test_enable_quiet_is_callable` and `test_disable_notify_is_callable` have no core to extract, because the function under test *is* the setter and the assertion is `f() == f()`; see the shared-global gate paragraph above. Day 179 (#836): `color_enabled()` is **pinned to `true` under `cfg(test)`**, with its decision half extracted beside it as the pure, table-tested `color_enabled_from_env(no_color_set) -> bool`. The measured defect: `COLOR_DISABLED` is a write-once `OnceLock` seeded from the ambient `NO_COLOR` with **no setter**, so **20 tests across 8 modules** (`git.rs` 5, `format/highlight.rs` 5, `format/markdown.rs` 2, `format/cost.rs` 2, `commands_search.rs` 2, `commands_git_review.rs` 2, `commands_retry.rs` 1, `commands_map.rs` 1) asserted whichever branch the *environment* chose — `cargo test` green, `NO_COLOR=1 cargo test` **20 failed** on tests that had not changed. That outranks its size because `scripts/evolve.sh` turns any `cargo test` failure into `git reset --hard`: an inherited `NO_COLOR` would revert a whole session's valid work while reading as "the task broke the build", the flattering-to-alarming misattribution shape. **`true` is the byte-identical value** — without `NO_COLOR` set (the state the suite passes in today) `color_enabled()` already returned `true` — so `cargo test` is unchanged and only the `NO_COLOR=1` run moves, from red to matching it; verified by running both, **5198 passed / 0 failed each**, against 5177/20 before. Fixed at the one seam rather than by editing 20 tests in 8 files, and production is untouched (`cfg(test)` code never ships; the release body is unchanged). **The tradeoff, named rather than smuggled:** no test observes the colour-*disabled* branch through the global — but none could before either (write-once, env-seeded, no setter), so that was ambient luck, not coverage; what replaces it is the `color_enabled_from_env` table asserting the *decision* in **both** directions, since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. A doc comment at the pin says all of this so a later reader does not delete it as dead weight. **`is_plain_output()` (`--screen-reader`) is a different switch** — an `AtomicBool` with a real setter — and was deliberately left alone; every `plain` parameter and glyph-free path is unaffected.
- `format/diff.rs` — LCS-based line diff algorithm, colored unified diff rendering. Day 179 (self-discovered; transferred bug class — Claude Code v2.1.246 shipped the same fix: *"a severe transcript slowdown when a diff contained a very long single line (e.g. a base64 string); such lines now render truncated with a marker"*): a diff is bounded on **two independent properties**, and only one of them existed. `MAX_DIFF_LINES = 20` bounds how **many** lines render; `MAX_DIFF_LINE_WIDTH = 500` bounds how **wide** one of them may be. The second is the one that was missing, and a single line is exactly the shape that walks past the first: a diff touching a base64 blob, a minified JS bundle, a one-line JSON or a lockfile hash is *one* line, so it passed the line-count bound untouched and rendered whole, through ANSI colouring, into the user's terminal. This is on the display path for **every `edit_file` result**, which is why it is `Kind: product` — my own diffs are hand-sized Rust, so the defect is invisible from where I sit, and it is the silent half of the two-audience rule (a wrong default draws complaints; a missing capability draws nothing, forever). It was also the one place the in-band-elision rule was not applied — `/run`, `/ast`, `/bg output` and `never_forecast_clause` all mark their cuts — because the cut did not exist. **`MAX_DIFF_LINE_WIDTH` is a judgment threshold, not a measurement**, and the const's doc comment says so: nothing measured says 500 is right; it is the point past which a diff line stops being readable and starts being a terminal hazard. Measured in **bytes**, matching `CappedCapture` in `commands_run.rs` rather than inventing a second unit — for the shapes it exists for (base64, hashes, minified source) bytes and columns are the same number. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `truncate_diff_line(line, max) -> Option<String>`: **`None` means at-or-under budget and the caller emits the line verbatim**, which is every line of every hand-sized diff and so the whole regression surface, and `Some(_)` is the kept prefix plus a marker in the vocabulary the codebase already uses (`… [yoyo: N more bytes elided — diff lines are capped at N bytes]`) — a silent elision is the bug. It never indexes by a raw byte offset: the prefix walks back to an `is_char_boundary` first, so a multi-byte char straddling the cut is dropped **whole** rather than split (rule #250, and a base64 blob is exactly where a multi-byte neighbour sits). **The truncate-before-colour ordering is load-bearing, not stylistic, and a comment at the call site says so** so a later reader does not "simplify" it: cutting a string that already carries ANSI escapes can land *inside* an escape sequence and leave the terminal stuck in a colour, and would drop the trailing `RESET` entirely — bounding the raw content first keeps every escape the function emits balanced. Applied at the three **content-line** emission points only (`Keep`/`Delete`/`Insert`, now sharing one truncation site); hunk headers and the `···` separator are short by construction and were deliberately left alone rather than blanket-wrapping every write. Pinned at the **emission point** — the string a caller of `format_edit_diff` receives, never the helper one layer below — in both directions: a 5000-char blob renders truncated with the marker and does not reach the terminal whole, a long *context* line is bounded too (same display path), every rendered line still ends with a colour reset, and the **near-miss guard** asserts an ordinary diff renders **byte-identically** via a whole-string `assert_eq!` rather than a `contains` — a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. The boundary is pinned on both sides (exactly at the cap → not truncated; one byte past → truncated). **Positive control run rather than assumed:** neutering the helper to always return `None` fails exactly the five truncation tests and leaves all 19 others green, including both near-miss guards and every pre-existing diff test. **Deliberately not done:** `truncate_diff_preview` and the other renderers were **not** swept — a sweep transfers the fix and silently drops the burden of proof, so the second door is named here rather than smuggled in.
- `format/output.rs` — tool output compression, filtering, truncation, batch summary, indentation. Day 164: `filter_test_output` collapses pass lines only when the same output also carries a runner **summary** line (`test result:`, pytest `=== N passed ===`, jest `Tests:`, go `ok pkg 0.12s`) or a cargo `running N tests` header — a `✓` glyph is a shape, a runner summary is provenance, and collapsing on shape alone was deleting non-test bash output (npm/docker/eslint/yoyo's own `/doctor`, and observed live eating `/risk accuracy`'s `✓ src/…` hit lists) while labelling it "N passing tests omitted"
- `format/highlight.rs` — syntax highlighting for code, JSON, YAML, TOML. Day 169 (#759): under `norm == "rust"` **only**, a `'` no longer unconditionally opens a string/char literal. Rust lifetimes (`&'a str`, `fn f<'a>(…)`, `'static`, `'_`) never close their tick, so from that character on the *rest of the line* rendered in string colour — every Rust source line containing a lifetime displayed wrong, anywhere yoyo prints highlighted Rust (file display, `/find`/`/grep` context, tool-output rendering). The discrimination is **not** a second implementation: `is_rust_char_literal` delegates to `commands_refactor::char_literal_len`, the same helper `significant_braces` uses, made `pub(crate)` with this consumer in the same diff — so the char-literal-vs-lifetime rule has one statement and one table test, not a third quote scanner drifting from the other two. That reuse also closed a gap the highlighter's own deleted copy had: `'\u{1F600}'` now highlights as a char literal instead of rendering unstyled. **Named classes covered** (this is not a claim to lex Rust): `'x'`, `'\n'`, `'\''`, `'\u{7d}'` char literals highlight as before; a `'` that opens no *closed* literal is emitted as plain text, so the lifetime tick is inert and the code after it keeps its normal colours. **Deliberately unchanged:** every non-Rust language — Python, JS, shell and the rest still treat `'…'` as a genuine string, byte-identical to before, because the guard is gated on the language. **Cross-line state (Day 173) — the gap to its sibling is now closed for every delimiter class either of them models, and what is left out is named rather than implied:** this highlighter used to be **line-based with no cross-line state at all**, so a `/* … */` block comment and a string literal spanning lines were both mis-coloured from the second line on, while `significant_braces` carried both facts in a `BraceScanState` (#771 items 1+2, Day 171 — but that issue was about the *scanner that rewrites files* and touched nothing on this display path). Three Day-173 tasks carried those facts over here into `HighlightState`, which `highlight_code_line_with(lang, line, &mut state)` threads across lines: (a) `block_comment_depth`, a **depth** not a bool, so Rust's nested `/* /* */ */` is one comment while the other C-family languages close on the first `*/`, and a stray `*/` at depth 0 is ordinary code rather than an underflow; (b) `open_string`, a plain `"…"` left open at end of line, set **only** where such a literal really spans lines (`multiline_strings` — Rust today), whose content is emitted string-coloured so `/*`, `//` and keywords inside it are inert and ordinary highlighting resumes just past the closer. (c) Day 173 (#806) carried the remaining two delimiter classes — Rust **raw** strings (`r"…"` / `r#"…"#` at any hash count, `b` prefix allowed) and **backtick** literals — which turned that field from a `bool` into `Option<StringDelim>`, so the delimiter *type* is carried rather than merely the fact that something is open. It is **not** a third scanner: `commands_refactor::raw_string_open` and `close_open_string` were made `pub(crate)` with this consumer landing in the same diff, the highlighter's own private single-purpose `close_open_string` copy was **deleted**, and `StringDelim` grew one variant (`Backtick { escapes }`) whose match arm was added at the same time — the same one-rule-one-table discipline #759 used for `char_literal_len`. Backticks are gated per language by `backtick_strings(norm) -> Option<bool>`: `js` → spans lines and honours `` \` ``, `go` → spans lines with **no** escapes (a `\` before the closing backtick does not protect it, because Go's backtick literal is raw), everything else `None`, so a backtick in rust/c/python stays inert and those languages are byte-identical. **Still not carried, and this is the whole residue:** `${…}` interpolation inside a JS template literal is treated as string content, not code, so an interpolated expression is not highlighted — this colours a line, it does not lex one. `highlight_code_line(lang, line)` is kept as the stateless wrapper and **is** the byte-identity baseline the regression tests assert against: languages with no block comments (python, shell, yaml, toml, json) and every non-Rust language reset the carried flags, and a line with no carried state and no block comment takes a single-segment fast path, so their output is byte-identical to the pre-state behaviour. Pinned at the **emission point**: tests call `highlight_code_line` / `highlight_code_line_with` and assert the string a caller receives (lifetime case, `'x'`/`'\n'` pass-through, unchanged python/js/shell single-quoted strings, and — for the cross-line half — line *sequences* driven through one carried state: a raw string's second line, an `r##"` hash count, a JS template literal, a Go raw string, the js-vs-go escape-rule difference, a backtick left inert in rust/c, plus a byte-identity check of the stateless path for the single-line forms of all of them).
- `format/highlight_lang.rs` — the eight per-language predicates and tables the highlighter reads (`normalize_lang`, `lang_keywords`, `lang_types`, `comment_prefix`, `supports_block_comments`, `block_comments_nest`, `multiline_strings`, `backtick_strings`), all `pub(super)` and each answering only *what is true of language X?* — none of them touches `HighlightState`, a `&mut` scanner, or an output string, which is the seam the split was taken at. A **pure Day-174 move** out of `format/highlight.rs` (2048 → 1682 lines) to clear the fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` gate, which the file was sitting 48 past inside the 50-line grace band: no body was rewritten, no test moved (all 95 stay in `highlight.rs` and were not edited), and nothing about highlighting behaviour changed. The names above are still referenced by the `format/highlight.rs` bullet — they simply live one file over.
- `format/cost.rs` — pricing, cost display, token formatting; fleet-model pricing (claude-fable-5, opus-5, opus-4-8, sonnet-5, haiku-4-5) is read at runtime from yoagent 0.9's preset `ModelConfig.cost` via `agent_builder::anthropic_preset` (preset is the source of truth), all other models use the local pricing table. That "preset is the source of truth" rule is what `test_estimate_cost_sonnet_5_preset` derives its expected value from rather than hardcoding a literal — yoagent 0.16.6 corrected the sonnet-5 preset from $3/$15 to $2/$10 (it had been carrying Sonnet 4.6 pricing) and main was red for 31 hours reporting an upstream *fix* as a yoyo regression. Day 177 corrected a **false mechanism** in that test's comment and recorded the superseded claim rather than erasing it: it read "because `Cargo.lock` is gitignored", which was true when written and went stale on 2026-08-24 at `0577bfe7` (which tracked the lockfile and added `--locked` to every CI invocation that resolves dependencies). The route the comment described is therefore closed; the reason to derive from the preset is not. Verified at correction time: `git ls-files --error-unmatch Cargo.lock` succeeds and `git check-ignore -v Cargo.lock` finds nothing. This is the `src/gasp.rs` class exactly — a wrong mechanism in a comment, read as authoritative by the next agent who opens the file, which cost eight sessions on #683 — and a dated measurement of my own repo decays just like one of a dependency, so the present tense was what made it read as a standing fact. Day 178 (reading #7, recorded in full above): the file's **two judgment clamps were extracted behind `fn` boundaries** — `context_fill_fraction(used, max)` (the bar never over-draws past full; an unknown window is empty, not full) and `breakdown_divisor(total)` (`total.max(1)`, so a zero total cannot divide by zero and the floor is 1 rather than a special-cased branch) — **purely to widen the mutant population**, since cargo-mutants' `FnValue` genre is function-granular and so a clamp is invisible to the dial until it owns a `fn`. Production behaviour is **byte-identical** (pure moves of an expression, no threshold/rounding/branch change), the count went **350 → 355**, and each has a table test covering both the ordinary pass-through row *and* the case the clamp exists for (`used > max` → exactly `1.0`, `max == 0` → `0.0`, `total == 0` → `1`) — a clamp tested only where it does not fire is vacuous green, and the pass-through rows are specifically what kill the `FnValue with 1` mutant. The file's other two `.max()` sites (`:545`, `:606`) are **`Iterator::max` reductions, not clamps**, and were deliberately left alone with a comment saying why.
- `format/markdown.rs` — MarkdownRenderer for streaming markdown output
- `format/tools.rs` — Spinner, ToolProgressTimer, ActiveToolState, ThinkBlockFilter; plain-output (screen reader) switch: `set_plain_output`/`is_plain_output` (AtomicBool, set by `--screen-reader` in `cli.rs`, which also enables the existing no-color path) — when on, Spinner prints one static "running <tool>…" line and ToolProgressTimer never repaints (no `\r`, no ANSI cursor escapes); default off, byte-identical to prior behavior. Day 174 (blind round 63, the ranking's darkest file at 203 snapshots unobserved): that parenthesised promise was **false at teardown**, and this doc line was itself the assertion the code broke — `ToolProgressTimer::stop` and `ToolProgressTimer::drop` gated the erase on `stderr_is_terminal()` **alone**, so `--screen-reader` printed its one linear line via `plain_tool_progress_line` and then wrote `\r\x1b[K` straight into a screen reader's stream. The twin was the tell: `Spinner::stop`/`drop` were already correct (`stderr_is_terminal() && !is_plain_output()`), so one struct obeyed plain output at teardown and its sibling did not — the paint path was swept, the teardown path was not. All **four** teardown sites now route through one writer (`write_teardown_clear`) over the pure, table-tested `teardown_clear_sequence(is_terminal, plain) -> Option<&'static str>`, which returns the literal erase only for a non-plain terminal; `animations_enabled` was deleted because its only two callers were two of those four sites, so the rule has one statement rather than three. **Non-plain users are byte-identical** — the predicate the Spinner already used is the one now shared, and only the plain branch changes. Pinned at the emission point (`write_teardown_clear` writes exactly the returned string and nothing else, so the tested string is the string a caller receives): the four-state table, a plain-mode assertion that the emitted text contains neither `\r` nor `\x1b` and is empty, and a source-level guard that all four sites still share the one writer (needle built at runtime so it cannot match itself). **What the round did not find, stated rather than implied:** `ThinkBlockFilter::flush` (called at `prompt.rs:685`) takes the buffer but never clears `in_block` — harmless today because `PromptEventState::new()` builds a fresh filter per prompt, so nothing reuses one; and neither struct consults `is_quiet()`, which is deliberate, not an oversight — `--quiet`'s documented scope is "informational stderr output (config/context loading messages)" (`help.rs:175`) and a self-erasing animation is neither.
- `gasp.rs` — GASP recorder wiring, **recording** (#683 steps 1–2). A **default-off `gasp` cargo feature** (`Cargo.toml`: `default = []`, `gasp = ["yoagent/gasp"]` — a plain `cargo build`/`cargo test`, which is what yoyo runs while modifying itself, never compiles `yoagent-state`) gates the whole module, which is declared `#[cfg(feature = "gasp")]` in `main.rs`; nothing changes in a default build. An **env-gated open** (`open_recorder_from_env` → `GaspRecorder::open(root, "yoyo", "yoyo-inproc", GoalRef::Existing(id))`; the worker id is deliberately distinct from the retiring sidecar's, because mixed-writer history is what that field is for) yields a recorder that `main.rs` hands to `gasp::install`, a process-global `OnceLock` (a session opens at most one store — a second install is a bug, not a reconfiguration, so it is ignored rather than swapping stores mid-session). **Run and tool events are now recorded**: `tee_prompt` / `tee_prompt_messages` call `GaspRecorder::recording_sender(task, Some(forward_tx))` and `Agent::prompt_with_sender(...)` / `prompt_messages_with_sender(...)`, returning the *forwarded* stream so the caller sees exactly what `agent.prompt*` would have given it, and `None` when no recorder is installed. `src/prompt.rs` has **exactly four** agent-start call sites (`run_prompt_once`, `run_prompt_once_with_messages`, `run_prompt_stream_json`, `run_prompt_stream_json_with_content`) and **all four** route through one local seam pair (`start_prompt` / `start_prompt_messages`) which tees when a recorder is installed and falls back to `agent.prompt*` otherwise — one seam, so recording is on for all four or none; a partial sweep reads as done. The recording `JoinHandle` is deliberately detached: the task ends when yoagent drops the sender at loop end. **Redaction ships in the same diff, on purpose** — recorded summaries land in a *shareable* git repo, which is a one-way door, so the recorder is opened with `.with_summarizer(crate::safety::redact_secrets)` and every persisted tool-arg/output summary passes through the pure `redact_secrets` first (which lives in `src/safety.rs` since Day 165, so it and its tests **are** exercised by the default test run) (masks `sk-…`/`sk-ant-…`, `ghp_`/`gho_`/`ghu_`/`ghs_`/`ghr_`, `github_pat_`, `AKIA…`, and `KEY=`/`TOKEN=`/`SECRET=`/`PASSWORD=` style assignments with `[redacted]`, keeping the variable name so a redacted log still says *which* credential appeared). It is a mask, not a guarantee: a novel secret shape passes through. The decision half is pure and testable: `plan_from_env_values(root, goal)` reads the two env values as `Option<&str>` and returns three explicit states — `Disabled` (no `YOYO_GASP_STATE_DIR`; the normal case on every machine that isn't the evolve runner — silent, warns nothing, creates nothing, needs no credentials), `Misconfigured` (dir set but `YOYO_GASP_GOAL_ID` unset — the operator asked for recording and won't get it, so this one says so), and `Open`. A configured-but-nonexistent directory degrades to `None` silently; an open failure returns `None` after **one** honest stderr line naming what failed. It never propagates, never panics, never aborts startup — a session must not break because instrumentation did. Day 167 (#683 item 5) ported the **named half** of `tools/gasp-emit`'s session-graph emission into this module: **`session-start` (`session_start`) and `task` (`task_planned`)**, plus the `ensure_goal` helper they share and the `DEFAULT_GOAL` const value, all verbatim. Day 168 added the third arm, **`session-end` (`session_end`)**: it resolves the goal through the existing `session_goal`, names the outcome default (`DEFAULT_SESSION_OUTCOME = "done"`, via `session_outcome`, so absence has a name instead of being absorbed by an inline `unwrap_or`), parses the sidecar's comma-separated `--extra` projection list with `parse_extra_paths` (**the same three operations in the same order** — `split(',')` → `map(str::trim)` → `filter(!is_empty)` — because a differently-parsed path list writes a *different boundary commit*), then `record_run_finished` → `commit_run` → `release_lease` and **returns** the boundary sha rather than printing it (the sidecar printed because it was a CLI; in-process the caller decides). Unlike its two siblings it has **no** store-generic `*_in` body, and that is a constraint rather than an oversight: `commit_run`/`release_lease` are `GitEventStore` *inherent* methods, not `EventStore` trait methods, so it cannot be written against a generic store and cannot be driven against a scratch one — the two pure helpers are table-tested, `session_end` itself is compile-tested only, and its doc comment says so. **`task-result` was ported on Day 177** — all four sidecar arms now live here. It had been unported-but-unblocked since Day 172; the port finally landed as a creator-authored patch after **eight** revert receipts (#763, #765, #782, #785, #787, #789, #803) recorded sessions that opened `src/gasp.rs`, read the then-authoritative "unreachable" claim, and exited with an empty diff. The blocker was never upstream: `ProjectRef`/`ArtifactRef` (0.16.4) and `PatchStatus` (0.16.5) are re-exported from `yoagent::gasp`, verified again at port time. Like its three siblings it shipped **dormant** at port time (`#[allow(dead_code)]`, consumer named as #683 items (3)+(7)) — no longer true as of #827/#831; see the correction below. The stale claim that stopped it read as follows — recorded rather than erased, because it is the whole mechanism: the module doc in `src/gasp.rs` asserted it was *unreachable* because it names `ProjectRef`, `ArtifactRef` and `PatchStatus` (`patch.base_project_ref`, `patch.artifacts`, `update_patch_status(…, PatchStatus::Promoted, …)`, `tools/gasp-emit/src/main.rs:166-268`), and all three are reachable as of yoagent **0.16.5**: `ProjectRef`/`ArtifactRef` landed in 0.16.4 (#115) and `PatchStatus` in 0.16.5 (#117), so `task-result` is **portable — no upstream blocker remains**. Day 172: that stale sentence was the *mechanism* behind five empty-diff reverts on #683 item (5) (#765, #782, #785, #787, #789) plus the docs-only #788 — every one of those agents opened `src/gasp.rs`, read an authoritative "unreachable" that had been true against 0.16.3 and went stale when the pin moved, and exited without a diff; `CLAUDE.md` said the opposite, but the half an agent opens to do the work is the half that said impossible. Both the module doc and the inline comment above the session-graph section now state the correction *and* record the superseded claim (a correction that erases the wrong claim teaches nothing about why five sessions died), and the corrected claim carries a machine-readable `yoagent-version-claim` marker pinned to `Cargo.lock`'s resolved `yoagent` version by `tests/doc_version_claims.rs` (see the dependency-version-claim gate above) — an integration test, because this module is behind the default-off `gasp` feature and a `#[cfg(test)]` guard inside it would never run in a plain `cargo test`. Day 177 (#821) widened that test from one hardcoded file to a scan and **retired its old exactly-once assertion**: once the marker is a convention rather than a single instance, "exactly one in the repo" forbids the second consumer, so the invariant that survives is *every marker agrees with `Cargo.lock`* plus *at least one marker exists*. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased:** this bullet then read "that task corrected the claim and nothing else: `task-result` remains unported — unblocked is not ported". True from Day 172 until Day 177, when the port landed; the distinction it drew (unblocked is not ported) is still the right one and is why the gap stayed visible for five days instead of reading as closed. Compile-verified Day 171 against the command's *complete* type surface (12 types, extracted mechanically from `tools/gasp-emit/src/main.rs:166-268` rather than read): clean. That mechanical extraction matters — the operator's earlier "it builds" claim on #683 was **wrong** because the probe covered only the types named in the first ~60 lines and missed `update_patch_status`; the contradiction between that claim and this file's then-correct note is what sent Day 170 into a prove-the-blocker task that was reverted (#785). yoyo's skepticism was right both times. Shipping a `task-result` that silently dropped `base_project_ref`/`artifacts`/`update_patch_status` would be exactly the "half that reads as complete" the creator forbade. Naming the half is the deliverable, not a caveat: a partially-ported tier that names its parts is fine, one that reads as complete is not, so the module doc comment and this bullet both name all four arms, state that every one of them is dormant with zero wired consumers, and state that no `*_in` body is behaviourally tested. The sidecar mixes decisions with I/O and that shape was **not** copied — the decisions are plain non-async functions with table tests (`goal_for_kind` — the `--kind product` reroute to `goal_product_value`, every other kind keeps the goal it was given; `standing_goal_title` / `standing_goal_summary` — **byte-identical** to `tools/gasp-emit/src/main.rs:66-79`, because a goal id and its node text are *identities in a shared store*, so a reworded constant is a different graph rather than a nicer string; `task_node_id` — the `task_{run_id}_{num}` shape; `default_session_task_label`), and the three entry points that *can* be written generically (`session_start` / `task_planned` / `task_result`) delegate to a store-generic `*_in` body — `session_end` cannot, for the inherent-method reason above. **That generic split does not buy a behavioural test, and the docs no longer claim it does:** this bullet and two doc comments in `src/gasp.rs` used to say the `*_in` bodies are "testable against a scratch store"; they are not, because `MemoryEventStore` lives in `yoagent-state`, which `Cargo.toml` deliberately does not take as a direct dependency, and `yoagent::gasp` does not re-export it (probed at port time: the name does not resolve). **Zero tests drive any `*_in` body.** That sentence is itself a claim about what an external dependency cannot do, so it carries the second live `yoagent-version-claim` marker (`src/gasp.rs`, in `task_result`'s doc) — if upstream ever re-exports the store it goes stale silently and the `*_in` bodies stay untested for no reason. What is tested is the pure decision half of each arm (`goal_for_kind`, `standing_goal_title`, `task_node_id`, `eval_command_or_default`, `parse_extra_paths`) and the node-id shapes; the split is kept because it is the right shape and becomes testable the moment upstream re-exports the store. They take a `&GraphSession` — a **directly-opened** `GitEventStore` plus `YoAgentState::load`, built by `open_graph_session(plan, resume, worker)` (the third parameter since Day 179, #828 item 2 — see the end of this bullet) — and read `session.state()` / `session.store()` / `session.actor()` / `session.goal()`. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #831).** This sentence used to read: *"They take the `&GaspRecorder` and read `recorder.state()` / `recorder.actor()` / `recorder.goal()` — a second `GitEventStore` on the same root is **never** opened, because a GASP repo is single-writer behind a 600s lease and a second store collides with the recorder's rather than cooperating (measured Day 165; it is the whole reason the sidecar cannot run alongside)."* The lease fact it cites is still true and is **not** what was wrong; the conclusion drawn from it was, because it answered a question nobody was asking. Two *concurrent* stores do collide — but `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` emits **one event per process**, four short-lived `yoyo gasp` invocations per session, which are **sequential and never concurrent**, so there is no second live store to collide with. What there is instead is the opposite hazard: yoagent 0.16.6's `GaspRecorder::with_store` closes any run a *previous* process left open as `"interrupted"` on **every** open, so call 2 interrupted the run call 1 opened. Measured in #831: call 3 emitted `run.finished`, `task.created` landed **into a finished run**, and `session-end` failed with `cannot finish <run>: no run is open` — **no boundary commit**, so the session's state was never pushed, **with every call still exiting 0**. That last clause is why it survived the door landing and why the test pins *ordering*, not success. The fix is what `tools/gasp-emit` always did and is confined to how the arms acquire their handle: open the store directly, and `resume_open_run()` on every arm **except** `session-start` (`gasp_cli::needs_open_run`, table-tested), so an open run survives the process boundary instead of being closed by its own next event. **`GaspRecorder` remains the correct handle for the in-process run/model/tool tier (#683 item (3)) and that path is byte-identical** — `open_recorder`, `open_recorder_from_env`, `install`, `tee_prompt` / `tee_prompt_messages` and `prompt.rs`'s `start_prompt` seam were not touched: there it is one process and one open, so interrupt-on-open is the right rule. Pinned by `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs`, which spawns **four real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_yoyo` processes** (the defect does not exist inside one process — the interrupt happens *at open* — so a single-process test calling all four arms against one handle passes on the broken code) against a scratch git repo and asserts **position**: `run.finished` is the **last** domain event, there is **exactly one** of them (two means a run was reaped mid-session), `run.started < task.created < run.finished`, and `session-end` reports a boundary commit. Ordering rather than presence is load-bearing — both columns of #831's measured table emit all the same event kinds, so an exit-code or presence-set check **passes on the broken behaviour**; the presence set is kept only as the anti-vacuous guard, so a log that recorded nothing cannot satisfy "`run.finished` is last" by having nothing to order. Verified against the defect and not only in the passing direction: reintroducing `GaspRecorder::with_store` into `open_graph_session` produced `[goal.created, run.started, run.finished, task.created, …]` with `session-end` failing and committing no boundary. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #827 — and it went stale silently, which is the point).** This bullet used to read: *"All four **ship dormant and that is the correct outcome**: they compile, they unit-test, they record nothing, because their only consumers are #683 items (3)+(7) (the operator-lane env bridge) and wiring the env vars here would destroy the sidecar's session record — every path still lands in `RecorderPlan::Disabled`. `session_start`, `task_planned`, `session_end` and `task_result` are therefore the only items carrying `#[allow(dead_code)]`, each narrowly scoped with a comment naming (3)+(7) as the pending consumer."* True until `0aec6cf5` (Day 178, 21:24), when #827 gave all four a **CLI door** — `yoyo gasp <arm>`, parsed by `gasp_cli::parse_gasp_args` and run by `run_gasp_command`, routed from `dispatch_sub.rs:747` — and **that task did not update CLAUDE.md**, so this file asserted "dormant, records nothing" for a day about arms that had a live door. Checked at correction time rather than assumed: `grep -n 'allow(dead_code)' src/gasp.rs` returns **nothing** (the three remaining ones are in `gasp_cli.rs`, on the plain-build path), and `run_gasp_command` builds its plan from the `--state-dir` flag via `plan_from_env_values`, so it lands in `RecorderPlan::Open`, not `Disabled`. What is **still** true and is the part worth keeping: `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` still invokes the `tools/gasp-emit` sidecar, so the **operator lane has not been swapped** — that atomic swap is #683 item (7) and the env bridge is item (3), both still pending, and both were blocked behind #831 rather than behind anything upstream. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 180) — and it is the *second* stale status claim in this one bullet, which is the pattern rather than the incident.** The sentence immediately above was true when written and is false as of creator commit `b573e523`, which landed **#683 item (7)**: `tools/gasp-emit` is **deleted** (`ls tools/` → no such directory) and `scripts/gasp_shim.sh:43,89` shells `target/gasp-yoyo/debug/yoyo gasp <arm>` — so **the operator lane IS swapped and `src/gasp.rs` is the emitter**. The separate target dir is deliberate, not incidental: a featured build over the shared `target/debug/yoyo` is the #832 uplift hazard, where every `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*` consumer resolves to whichever build ran last. The clause about **item (3)** stays, and is now *measured rather than assumed*: `grep -n 'YOYO_GASP_STATE_DIR' scripts/gasp_shim.sh` returns two hits and **neither is an export** — `:150` is the line *"DO NOT export YOYO_GASP_STATE_DIR / YOYO_GASP_GOAL_ID here"* and `:193` says an unset value keeps the recorder unreachable on purpose — so `plan_from_env_values` still lands in `RecorderPlan::Disabled` for every evolve-loop process and the in-process run/model/tool tier records **nothing** in the operator lane. **What changed is the *reason*, which is the transferable half:** it used to be "a second store would collide with the sidecar's"; the sidecar is gone and the hazard is not, because the shim still *opens and holds a run* for the whole session (now via `yoyo gasp session-start … session-end`), so exporting the bridge today reproduces the Day-165 lease theft verbatim — `run.finished` written against a live run, then `session-end` failing with `cannot finish <run>: no run is open`, and the whole session's record lost. `scripts/gasp_shim.sh:150-194` states it as an **architecture** question, not a wiring one: either the session tier moves inside the agent process, or the run/model/tool tier is written through these same short-lived calls. Also corrected: the sentence below reading "**(7) touches `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` and `tools/gasp-emit/`, both on the harness's protected list, so yoyo cannot land it**" was a true statement about *my* lane and it is now moot — the creator landed it. And every `tools/gasp-emit/src/main.rs:NN` citation in `src/gasp.rs` and `src/gasp_cli.rs` is now a **historical** reference rather than a missing file; both module docs say so, and it is read with `git show b573e523^:tools/gasp-emit/src/main.rs`. Those citations are deliberately kept: they record where a constant or parse rule came from, and why some may not be reworded (a goal id is a node identity in a shared store, so a nicer string is a different graph). The pure helpers are genuinely called by `ensure_goal` / `session_start_in` / `task_planned_in` / `session_end`, so they need no allow. **Two things remain untrue and should not be overstated:** the **default** `cargo test` run exercises nothing in this module — the feature is off, so it compiles into nothing, and `cargo build --features gasp` / `cargo test --features gasp` / `cargo clippy --all-targets --features gasp -- -D warnings` have to be run **by hand** locally (the redaction half is the one exception, and only because it no longer lives here); CI *does* run the featured test and the featured clippy (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:45,52`, since Day 165 `9dcccf48`), so a featured clippy warning is a red CI rather than a nit — the older claim that "CI does not build `--features gasp`" was true when written and has been false since that commit. And recording failures are logged by **yoagent** via `tracing` while the event stream keeps flowing — yoyo's UI does not surface them. **Day 179 (#828 item 2) — `--worker` is honoured, for the graph tier only, and the flag that was parsed-announced-and-ignored is gone.** `gasp_cli::parse_gasp_args` accepted `--worker` and then printed a stderr line saying it had **no effect**, because the graph-tier open hardcoded its id — *a capability with a description and no consumer*, which is the same shape as the "unreachable" claim that cost eight sessions. It matters on the one axis the #683 ordering constraint is about: a GASP repo is single-writer behind a **600s lease**, and `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` passes `--worker evolve-shim-$$`, so the pid suffix is what gives **overlapping sessions distinct lease identities** — a property the sidecar had and the port did not, and the operator cannot swap them until the door can honour it. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `gasp_cli::graph_worker_id(Option<&str>) -> String`, deliberately in `gasp_cli.rs` because that file compiles on the **plain** (non-`gasp`) build path, so `cargo test` actually reaches it — a decision behind the default-off feature would be untested by the run that gates every task. `None` → `DEFAULT_GRAPH_WORKER_ID`, and an empty or whitespace-only value → the same default, because **an empty worker id is not an identity**: refusing to open a store under one beats inventing a name. That fallback is announced by `worker_fallback_note` (a flag was typed and dropped, so say so — silent under nothing, since this is the operator lane), and the note is `None` when no flag was passed or the flag was real; both directions are pinned. **Passing no flag is byte-identical to before** — `DEFAULT_GRAPH_WORKER_ID` is the same literal `"yoyo-inproc"` the open hardcoded — which is the whole regression risk, and `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` is its check: those four real processes pass no `--worker`, so they exercise the default path unchanged. **`gasp::WORKER_ID` is a second, separate constant carrying the same literal, and the two must not be merged** — a code comment at the site says so: it is the **in-process** run/model/tool tier (#683 item (3), `open_recorder` / `open_recorder_from_env`), which is deliberately fixed, because a caller-supplied `evolve-shim-$$` there would make an in-process event indistinguishable from a sidecar-written one, and mixed-writer history is exactly what that field is for. Only the graph tier takes the flag. **Stated plainly: this does not complete #683.** Item (3) (the env bridge) and item (7) (the atomic sidecar retirement) remain **operator-lane and unshipped** — (7) touches `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` and `tools/gasp-emit/`, both on the harness's protected list, so yoyo cannot land it and an attempt reverts the whole task. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 180):** the sentence immediately above is false for item (7), which the **creator** landed at `b573e523` — `tools/gasp-emit` is deleted and the shim shells `yoyo gasp`. Its reasoning was sound and is the part worth keeping: those two paths *are* protected, so (7) was never yoyo's to land, and "unshipped" was accurate about **my** lane on the day it was written. **Item (3) is still open, and that is measured** — `scripts/gasp_shim.sh:150` explicitly refuses to export `YOYO_GASP_STATE_DIR` / `YOYO_GASP_GOAL_ID`, so the in-process tier stays `RecorderPlan::Disabled`; see the correction earlier in this bullet for why the hazard outlived the sidecar. What this removes is the last blocker inside yoyo's own lane: the door can now be handed the shim's argv without losing a property the shim depends on. **Day 180 (#847) — every session kind now gets its own node, and the run tier is untouched.** The measured defect, from the live `yoyo-gasp` graph (8,653 events): **394 of 691 runs (57%) had no node other than the run itself** — evolve 295 of 297 runs carried a task node, while **social (318), skill-evolve (68) and dream (8) carried zero**, because only `evolve.sh` ever calls the `task` arm. For those sessions `run.finished` was the sole evidence they happened. It is the prerequisite for #683 item (3): yoagent closes any open run as `"interrupted"` on every recorder open, so a run belongs to one process while the shim's run spans ~7 — freeing runs for processes is the unlock, and it cannot be done while 57% of sessions have nowhere else to live. **The change is additive and that is the whole promise:** `record_run_started` / `record_run_finished` are byte-identical, so the graph gains a node and loses none, which is what makes it independently verifiable *before* the switch that follows. `session_start_in` records the node **after** `record_run_started` (it belongs inside the run, exactly as a planned task does), and its id reuses the one id convention rather than inventing a second — `session_task_node_id(run_id)` is `task_node_id(run_id, SESSION_TASK_NUM)` → `task_{run_id}_session`, which cannot collide with a planned task because `task_planned` is only ever called with the shim's decimal `--num`. `session_end` closes it with `update_task_status(…, TaskStatus::Done, Some(outcome))`, and **the position is load-bearing in both directions**: before `record_run_finished`, because `run.finished` must stay the last domain event (which `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` pins), and before `commit_run`, or the boundary commit would not carry it. The status says the session *ended* and the outcome string says *how*, verbatim, in the reason — `session_outcome` accepts any string, so mapping it onto the `TaskStatus` enum would invent a verdict taxonomy the value does not have. **That close is guarded by an existence check, and the sentence that used to sit here was false — recorded rather than erased, because a live graph is what disproved it (Day 181, #849).** The superseded claim read: *"That close is fail-soft on purpose, and the reason is measured rather than defensive: `update_task_status` hard-errors with `node not found` when the node is absent, and propagating that with `?` would abort `session_end` **before** `commit_run` … The failure is **named on stderr** rather than swallowed, because a silent degradation is the bug."* **Nothing was ever named on stderr, because nothing ever errored.** In `yoagent-state` 0.5.0, `update_task_status` (`state.rs:457`) records the `task.status_changed` event **first, unconditionally**, then applies the `UpdateNode` op, and performs **no node-existence check anywhere** — verified by reading it, not assumed. So the `if let Err(…)` was dead twice over: the append had already happened by the time any `Err` could return, and the error most likely never returned at all — the corruption surfaced later, at **fold** time, in a different process. Measured consequence: `session-start` ran on the pre-#847 binary and `session-end` on the post-#847 one, so an `UpdateNode` landed for a node that was never created, and the published `yologdev/yoyo-gasp` checker went **7/7 → 5/7** (`fold failed: node not found: task_run_day180_20260827T232710Z_session`, failing replay and restore). Because `YoAgentState::load` then fails on the dangling node, `yoyo gasp <arm>` cannot open the store at all and the shim degrades via `_gasp_off` — so **every later session still runs, still passes, and records nothing**. Silent, as usual. **The transferable shape, which is the whole lesson: a guard on the *result* cannot protect a side effect that happens before the result.** For an append-only event store, catching the error does not unwrite the event; the check has to be on the **call** — does the node exist? — not on its return value. This is my own archived rule ("a fallback cascade assumes every failed attempt was a no-op, but some tools fail dirty") walked into anyway. **The fix is an upstream-blessed shape, not an invention:** `session_end` asks `session.state().get_node(NodeId::new(…))` first and only then updates — the same shape yoagent's own `gasp.rs:182` uses and the same shape `ensure_goal` already uses two functions up in this very file; `NodeId` was already imported, so no new dependency. **Both branches speak and stay distinct:** an absent node prints that the close was *skipped* and names the node (a skipped close is a real event, not nothing, and it is expected for any session whose `session-start` predates #847), while the pre-existing `Err` arm is **kept beside it rather than replaced** — "the node was absent" and "the update errored" are different facts with different remedies, and folding them would be the absence-absorbed-by-a-convenient-neighbour defect. The run tier still closes either way, for the original #831 reason, which was the one true half of the superseded sentence. **Position is unchanged in both directions** — still above `record_run_finished` (so `run.finished` stays the last domain event) and above `commit_run` (or the boundary commit would not carry it); #849 *guards* that block, it does not relocate it. **The happy path is byte-identical** — when the node exists the emitted events and their order do not change, which is every session from #847 onward and the entire regression surface; `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` is its guard and needed no edit, since its four real processes run one binary throughout so the node always exists. Pinned additionally by `tests/gasp_session_end_guard.rs`, a **deliberately weak source-level ordering guard** that slices `session_end`'s body and asserts the `get_node` call's index is **less than** the update's (needles assembled at runtime); it is **not** feature-gated on purpose — `src/gasp.rs` sits behind the default-off `gasp` feature and `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` carries a file-level `#![cfg(feature = "gasp")]`, so both compile to zero tests under the plain `cargo test` that gates every evolve task, and a text scan needs no feature, so this guard runs in the loop's own gate and has a consumer that actually reads it (hence no `REGISTERED_FEATURE_GATED_TESTS` entry). Its stated limit: it proves the check is *positioned*, never that it fires. Positive control **run rather than assumed**, and serially: with the existence check moved *after* the update the ordering guard fails with the exact receipt, while both near-miss guards stay green; restoring returns all three to green. **The limit, stated plainly: this makes the pairing self-healing going forward and does NOT repair the live graph**, which is still non-conformant and needs an operator decision (what title/status a repaired node carries, whether to append an in-band `observation`) — surfaced on #849, deliberately not done here, since it writes to an external repo and check 4 (append-only in git) passes today and must keep passing. The README badge stays as it is: it becomes true again only when the graph is repaired, not when this guard lands. **The label was deliberately left kind-blind, and this bullet says so rather than implying it names the kind:** `default_session_task_label` still returns `"evolve session day {day}"`, which is now reached by social, skill-evolve and dream — 57% of runs, none of them an evolve session. Two checked reasons, written at the function rather than only here. (1) It is a **fallback the operator lane never reaches**: `scripts/gasp_shim.sh:149` passes `--task` *unconditionally*, and each non-evolve caller passes its own label (`scripts/social.sh:350` `"social session …"`, `scripts/skill_evolve.sh:274`, `scripts/dream.sh:265` `"dream cycle …"`), so the three kinds that would be mislabelled are exactly the three that never see this string — the shim's own shell-side default (`gasp_shim.sh:102`, the same words) is reached only by `evolve.sh:391`, which passes no label and *is* an evolve session, so it is correct there. (2) The `kind` that would name it correctly **is not reachable here**: `GaspCommand::SessionStart` carries no `kind` field (its siblings `Task` and `TaskResult` do), and the shim never passes `--kind` to `session-start` — it uses its `kind` positional only to build the run id — so plumbing one through would add a parsed field with no producer, which is the "definition without its consumer" shape this repo has reverted three tasks over. The sidecar-parity test that pins that string was **left intact**, not weakened. **Verified on emitted events, not on `Ok`** — this module is behind the default-off `gasp` feature, so a `#[cfg(test)]` guard here never runs under a plain `cargo test`: `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` was **extended** (no second harness) and its four real processes now also assert that the session node's `task.created` exists, that it is created **inside** the open run, that it is closed **before** the run, and — the near-miss guard — that the **planned** task node survives beside it, so this is an addition and not a rename. Every pre-existing run-tier assertion is unchanged and is the regression guard: `run.started` first, `run.finished` last, exactly one `run.finished`, and `session-end` still reports a boundary commit. Positive control run rather than assumed: with the new `record_task` removed the session-node assertion fails while every run-tier assertion stays green. **Explicitly not done, named rather than implied:** the session-spanning run is **not** stopped, `YOYO_GASP_STATE_DIR` / `YOYO_GASP_GOAL_ID` are **not** exported (doing so today reproduces the Day-165 lease theft and loses the session record), `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` is untouched, and the `observation` node recording the run-semantics discontinuity belongs with the switch, not with this prerequisite. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased — and it is the third stale status claim in this one bullet, which is the pattern rather than the incident:** the sentences above say Day 167 and Day 168 ported `session-start` and `session-end` from `tools/gasp-emit` "all verbatim". That was true of the port and is the correct record of what those days did; it is **false as a description of the code as of Day 180**, because both arms now emit an event the sidecar never did. Parity with the sidecar is in any case no longer a property worth preserving — the creator deleted `tools/gasp-emit` at `b573e523`. **Day 181 (#851) — the commit artifact is attached ONLY for a promoted verdict, and the empty list is the honest reading of a reverted task rather than a fallback.** The defect was reported from *outside*, by the yoyo-dashboard maintainer reading the published graph: on day 173 patches `_1` and `_2` both carry `fb60556e` — a task that was **thrown away** claiming the commit of the one beside it that **shipped**. `task_result` built `patch.artifacts = vec![ArtifactRef::new("git-commit", …).with_hash(post_sha)]` unconditionally, before the verdict was applied further down by `update_patch_status`, while `verdict` was already a parameter of the same function — **the information was in scope and simply not consulted**. **Why that sha is actively misleading rather than merely meaningless, which is the whole severity of it:** `scripts/evolve.sh` reverts a failed task with `git reset --hard PRE_TASK_SHA`, so by the time the arm runs HEAD is back where the task *started* — and in a two-task session whose first task shipped, that **is task 1's commit**. A rejected patch therefore did not record a random sha; it recorded the single most confusable value available. **The fix is one conditional and one hoisted binding:** `let promoted = verdict == "promoted";` moved above the `StatePatch::new` block, so there is **exactly one statement** of what promoted means, read by the artifact, the eval status and score, the decision status and reason, the patch status, and the `record_failure` branch — a second spelling (`verdict == "promoted"` written again at the artifact) is precisely how the two halves drift, and this function would then decide the artifact one way and the status another. **Empty, never a sentinel**, and that is a claim about truth rather than a default: no artifact *is* the true statement, because a reverted task produced nothing, whereas a placeholder would be a second thing every reader has to special-case and is the *placeholder that renders plausibly* shape this repo has already paid for (#843's `/dev/null`). `base_project_ref` still records `pre_sha`, so the patch still says where it started from. **The promoted path is byte-identical**, which is the whole regression surface. **Tested on the emitted event payload, never on `Ok`** — this module sits behind the default-off `gasp` feature, so a `#[cfg(test)]` module here never runs under the plain `cargo test` that gates every task; the assertions **extend the existing `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs`** rather than starting a second harness, since it already spawns real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_yoyo` processes against a scratch repo. Both directions, because a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green: `verdict = "rejected"` → the `patch.proposed` event carries an **empty** `artifacts` array; the **near-miss guard** `verdict = "promoted"` → exactly one `git-commit` artifact whose `hash` *and* `uri` carry `POST_SHA`, with `PRE_SHA`/`POST_SHA` deliberately distinct so a patch that recorded the *base* commit cannot pass as one that recorded the result. **Anti-vacuous:** a helper asserts there is exactly one `patch.proposed` first, so a session that recorded no patch at all cannot satisfy "no artifact" by having nothing to carry. Every pre-existing ordering assertion in that file is untouched and green (`run.started` first, `run.finished` last and exactly one of them, `run.started < task.created < run.finished`, the session node, the boundary commit). **Positive control run rather than assumed, and serially** (two file-mutating controls in one parallel block raced once and one falsely passed): restoring the unconditional `vec![…]` fails exactly the rejected-verdict assertion while the promoted near-miss guard stays green, and restoring the conditional returns both to green — verified under `cargo test --features gasp`, since a plain run compiles this file into nothing. **The residue, stated rather than implied: history is NOT rewritten.** The log is append-only, so day 173's patches keep their wrong artifacts forever and the dashboard drops them defensively; this changes only what is written from here on.
- `git_commit_msg.rs` — the deterministic, token-free commit-message generator behind `/commit` (`generate_commit_message`, re-exported by `git.rs` so every call site still says `git::generate_commit_message`). Split out of `src/git.rs` on Day 174 as a **pure move** — the parent was at 1997 lines against the fatal 2000-line gate and the #811 fix plus its tests is ~260 lines; no body was rewritten by the move itself. Day 174 (#811) fixed three compounding defects that produced the real commit `c46d8453` — a 283-line production fix (`src/prompt.rs` +171, `src/repl.rs` +110) committed as `test(6 files): update code`, i.e. a message asserting the opposite of what the diff did. **Kind: product** — every yoyo user's `/commit` history carries this string, so the fix is language-agnostic, not "Rust files under `src/`". (1) The test rule was `path.contains("test")` with `any()`, so one fixture out of six re-typed the whole commit and any path merely carrying the letters (`src/commands_latest.rs`, `contest.rs`, `src/protest_handler.rs`) counted. It is now the pure `path_is_test(path)`, matching by **path shape**: a path component equal to `tests`/`test`/`spec`/`__tests__`, a file stem ending `_test`/`_tests`, or a filename containing `.test.`/`.spec.` — covering Rust, Go, Python, Ruby and JS/TS conventions in one predicate. The two cases that decide it are pinned by test in both directions: `src/commands_latest.rs` must **not** match, `src/main_tests.rs` must. (2) The prefix ladder was a first-match `if/else if` chain, so the type was decided by the *presence* of one file rather than by where the change is. `commit_file_category(path) -> CommitFileCategory` (`Test`/`Docs`/`Chore`/`Code`) plus `dominant_commit_category(&weights)` now pick the **largest total**, weighted by per-file changed lines parsed out of the diff the function was already walking — deliberately **no new git invocation**; a file with no counted lines (rename, mode change) still weighs 1. **Ties go to production code**: a mixed commit is neither a test commit nor a chore. Among the other three, ties fall back to the legacy ladder order (test, docs, chore) so nothing that used to be decided one way flips for free, and an empty diff defaults to `Code` rather than inventing a type. The prefix vocabulary is unchanged (`test`/`docs`/`chore`/`refactor`/`feat`) — this changed *how the winner is chosen*, not the set of types, and `refactor` vs `feat` still turns on the same `deletions > insertions * 2` test, now only once `Code` has won. (3) The summary said the least exactly when the commit was biggest: over 3 files the scope collapses to `"N files"` and any mixed diff read `update code`. Once the scope can no longer name the files, the summary spends itself on a fact the diff already knows — `{verb} {stem of the file with the most changed lines} (+N more)` — and deliberately **invents no description of intent**, which this function cannot know. Under 4 files the strings are byte-identical to before (`add changes` / `remove code` / `update code`), and the scope half is untouched at every size. Tested at the **emission point** (the string a caller receives, never a helper one layer below): the #811 file list now yields verbatim `feat(6 files): update prompt (+5 more)`; a lone `src/commands_latest.rs` does not type as `test`; a lone `tests/module_size.rs` still does (the near-miss guard, so the fix cannot regress the case the old rule got right). All four pre-existing tests were kept — `test_generate_commit_message_multiple_files` asserted only `msg.contains("4 files")`, which pinned the degradation rather than questioning it, so its scope assertion stands unchanged and two assertions were **added** beside a comment saying what it used to pin and why. **The AI-generated message path is untouched** — this is the deterministic fallback used when that path returns nothing usable (`commands_git.rs:1663`) and directly at `repl.rs:831` and `commands_git.rs:1380/1408`; nothing downstream sanity-checks its output, which is why the defect reached history silently. Day 178 (self-discovered) fixed a fourth defect on the same function, and it is the one the #811 work walked past: **every whole-file deletion was dropped from `files_changed` entirely.** git renders a delete as `--- a/<path>` / `+++ /dev/null`, and the walker only recorded a path on `+++ b/`, so the `+++ /dev/null` branch set `current = None` and recorded nothing — the deleted path appears *only* on the `---` line, which nothing read. All four downstream decisions were then computed over a list missing that file: the scope (a lone deletion gave `files_changed.len() == 0`, so `join` on an empty vec rendered the literal `()`), the category weights (a commit deleting only test files could not type as `test`), the `> 3 files` threshold (undercounted, so a 5-file deletion took the small-commit path), and the large-commit summary focus (picked from `files_changed`, so it could never name a deleted file). The visible symptom is the real commit **`26defce9`** in this repo's own history: `refactor(): remove code`, empty parens, for a diff deleting `mutants.toml`.
