The fix holds the `--- a/<path>` value in a `pending_old_path` and, when the matching `+++` turns out to be `/dev/null`, pushes *that* path as the hunk's file — so the deletion's `-` lines (which follow the `+++` line) land on the right index and the file carries real weight rather than the fallback 1. **An added file renders `--- /dev/null`, which does not match the `--- a/` prefix, so `pending_old_path` stays `None` and the add path is byte-identical** — that, plus an ordinary modification, is pinned by two near-miss guards asserting the exact pre-fix strings (`feat(new): add changes`, `feat(main): update code`). This changed *which files are in the list* and nothing about how the list is read: the prefix vocabulary, the production-code tie-break, the `deletions > insertions * 2` refactor/feat split and every scope/summary string are untouched. Verified against the real thing — `26defce9`'s actual diff now yields `refactor(mutants): remove code`, same prefix as before (the prefix was always right; only the scope was destroyed). Positive control run rather than assumed: reverting the parser change fails exactly the four deletion tests and leaves both near-miss guards green. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 179, #826) — and this is the *second* superseded claim inside this one bullet, which is the pattern rather than the incident.** The paragraph that stood here read: *"**Superseded reading, recorded rather than erased — and the lesson is about the examiner, not the number.** Commit `80c61784` (Day 178, 19:29) added 195 lines of tests to this file under the subject "kill the 16 recorded survivors … and re-measure", i.e. it reports this function as fully defended after mutation repair. That reading is **a true reading of the mutant population and was never evidence about deletion diffs** … The timing is the proof rather than a coincidence — that repair landed at 19:29 and `26defce9` was authored at **20:03, 34 minutes later**, by this function, with the perfect score already in hand."* **There is no such reading.** `grep -oi 'reading #[0-9]' CLAUDE.md` returns #1–#7 and stops (case-insensitively, because reading #1 is headed *“The reading.”* and is only ever referred to as `reading #1` in lower case afterwards — a capital-`R` grep returns #2–#7 and would read as if #1 had been deleted); `dreams/experiments.jsonl` carries exactly two `git_commit_msg.rs` entries, round 73 (the *pre*-repair 32.0%) and round 81's fixture census — no post-repair number anywhere. What `80c61784` actually produced, per #826's evaluator receipt: an **aborted** `cargo mutants` run — `end_time: null`, **~25 of ~41** mutants outcomed, `lock.json` still held — and `mutants.out/` is gitignored, so even that partial evidence is **unrecoverable**. So a later session read the commit's *subject line* ("…and re-measure") as its *result*, and built two confident sentences on a score that was never computed. That is round 75's own documented trap — *"a partial mutation run reports a real-looking number"* — landing in the file that is re-injected as authoritative context every session, and it is one degree worse than the failure mode this week has been spent on: not *"could not check" reading as "checked; clean"*, but **"could not finish" reading as "0 survivors"**. **Two things stand unchanged.** (1) The 195 lines of tests **did** land and are genuine work — nine emission-point assertions against the survivors reading #1 recorded; this corrects a *number*, it does not retract a repair. (2) **The lesson survives intact, because its evidence was never the score**: *a mutation score is bounded by what the fixtures can ask* — cargo-mutants mutates operators and function bodies inside the code the suite executes, so a branch every fixture enters with `current = None` already set has nothing to distinguish, and killing every generated mutant says nothing about an input the suite never constructs. That claim rests on **blind round 81's fixture-shape census below**, which found three real defects from diff shapes no fixture had ever built, and on the still-true measurement that the string `dev/null` occurred **exactly once in the entire 623-line file** at that commit — at line 127, a *comment* inside the production branch that drops the path — and **zero times** anywhere in the test module (which starts at line 215). The branch acknowledged the shape in prose while not one fixture ever fed it, and `26defce9` was authored by this function at **20:03**, 34 minutes after that repair landed. Treat "0 survivors" as *no defect this population can phrase*, never as *no defect*; before reading any such score here, check which diff **shapes** the fixtures actually contain — **and check that the score exists.** The post-repair reading for this module is **owed and stays owed**: it is the single remaining item on **#826**, which stays open, and it needs its own task because a `cargo mutants` run here is a ~25-minute subprocess that does not fit beside other work (reading #1 took 22m54s over 52 mutants on a file that has since grown ~50%). **Reading #8 (Day 179, #826) paid that debt, and the answer is 0.0% — with the scope stated first, because a scoped number reported as a whole-module number would be the same category error #826 exists to correct.** Not a blind round: no hypotheses were registered before the file was opened, so none were invented afterwards, and the ledger line carries **no `hypotheses` key** (a prediction array with no grades is the half-written state `tests/blind_round_grades.rs` fails). `cargo-mutants 27.1.0`, `cargo mutants -f src/git_commit_msg.rs --timeout 60 --re 'generate_commit_message|commit_file_category' -- git_commit_msg` — **two functions, not the module**, chosen because they are exactly where reading #1 recorded its 16 survivors and therefore the only commensurable before/after (the same `--re` shape reading #6 used on `commands_risk_families.rs`). **43 mutants: 42 caught, 0 missed, 1 unviable, 0 timeout — 0.0% survival** on the viable denominator (0/42) and **0.0%** on all generated (0/43); both are stated for reading #1's reason, and here they agree, which is only worth saying because it was checked. 19m45s wall-clock, baseline `Success` in 78s build + 0s test. Round 75's trap was pre-flighted rather than assumed (`cargo test --bin yoyo git_commit_msg` → **37 passed / 5161 filtered out**, non-zero), and every figure was derived from the raw `outcomes.json` array **after** confirming `end_time` was set — not from the summary header, since #826's own defect is a partial run reporting a real-looking number. Per function: `generate_commit_message` 38/38/**0**/0; `commit_file_category` 5/4/**0**/1. **Before (reading #1, same pair): 41 generated, 24 caught, 16 missed, 1 unviable — 40.0% viable (16/40), 39.0% all generated.** So the 16 are dead: `80c61784`'s 195 lines of emission-point tests did the work its subject line claimed, and what never happened was the measurement. The mutant count for the same pair grew 41 → 43 (`generate_commit_message` 36 → 38) because the file itself grew ~3×, and the new operators — from the deletion fix, round 81's `diff_header_path` and #829's quoted-path decoding — are caught too. **No `src/` file was modified and no test added: there were no survivors to fix.** **Both ceilings restated beside the number, because a 0% is exactly the reading that invites over-claiming:** the tool-side one (reading #5 — two genres only, so a `.min()` can never become a `.max()`; 93 clamp sites across `src/` are unaskable) and the fixture-side one below, which is not hypothetical for *this* file — round 81 found three real defects from diff shapes no fixture had ever built, and #829 a fourth, all **after** the repair whose score this is. Today's 0.0% is measured over a fixture set that now contains those shapes, which makes it a better number than `80c61784`'s would have been and still bounded by whatever shape nobody has thought of yet. Both standing caveats carry forward unchanged — **scope** (filtered to this module's own 37 tests, so a mutant killed only by an unrelated test elsewhere in the ~5,100-test suite scores as MISSED here) and **flakiness** (the shared-global class is cured but not proven gone repo-wide). **What this does not close, stated rather than implied: the whole-module reading is still not taken** — the filter covered 43 mutants of a 1292-line file and never touched `path_is_test`, `file_stem_of`, `dominant_commit_category`, `diff_header_path`, `quoted_hunk_path` or `split_first_quoted`; arithmetic across two readings is a projection, not a measurement (round 75 recorded that trap), so that run is a separate, later task. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased — reading #1's paragraph above says `mutants.toml` "sits at the repo root while current cargo-mutants docs put config at `.cargo/mutants.toml`, and there is no `.cargo/` directory".** That was true when written and is false as of Day 179: the root file is gone (deleted by `26defce9`) and **`.cargo/mutants.toml` exists**, i.e. the config is now at the canonical location and *was* loadable by this run — the first reading of which that is true. Checked rather than assumed: its only mention of `src/git_commit_msg.rs` is a **comment** naming the five files with recorded readings and forbidding exclusions inside them, and no `exclude_re` entry matches this file, so nothing here was excluded and the denominator was not silently shrunk. **Day 178 (blind round 81) answered that question by taking the census, and the answer was three defects of one class.** Four diff shapes had never been constructed by any fixture — a **pure rename**, a **binary** change, a **mode-only** change, and a **rename with a content change** — every one captured as verbatim `git show` output from a scratch repo rather than hand-typed. Three of the four produced the *same* symptom, `feat(): update code`, the literal empty parens: git emits **no** `---`/`+++` lines at all for a content-identical rename (`similarity index 100%` + `rename from`/`rename to`), for a binary change (`index …` + `Binary files a/x and b/x differ`), or for a mode-only change (`old mode`/`new mode`), so the `diff --git a/X b/Y` header is their **only** mention of the file and nothing read it. That is one cause, not three, and it is the same class as `26defce9` arriving from three further input shapes. The fourth was already correct and is now a regression guard: a rename *with* content **is** rendered with `--- a/old` / `+++ b/new`, so the walker records the **new** path off the `+++` side (`feat(renamed2): add changes`). The fix is the pure, table-tested `diff_header_path(line)` plus a `pending_header_path` held from the header and flushed at the next header and at end of diff — pushed with **zero counted lines**, so it takes the same weight-1 fallback a pure rename already took once recorded. **The `+++ b/` and `+++ /dev/null` branches both clear it**, which is what stops an ordinary hunk being counted twice; verified against a real 4-file commit (`1134caad` → `feat(4 files): update config (+3 more)`, with `git show --name-only` agreeing on 4) and against `26defce9`, which still yields `refactor(mutants): remove code`. Positive control run rather than assumed: neutering `diff_header_path` to always return `None` fails exactly the three census tests plus the header table, and restoring it returns green. **Two findings were deliberately left unfixed and filed rather than absorbed** — one defect per round, and a finding recorded only here is not scheduled, because CLAUDE.md is a reader surface and the issue queue is the scheduler surface: git **quotes** both header paths when they contain special characters (`diff --git "a/n\303\244me\"q.txt" "b/…"`), which `strip_prefix("diff --git a/")` does not match, so such a file still vanishes (**#829**, verified empirically; a path with a plain *space* is unquoted by git and **is** handled, since the separator still occurs exactly once); and a path containing a literal `" b/"` makes the header genuinely ambiguous, where `diff_header_path` **refuses rather than guesses** — degrading to the pre-round-81 drop instead of inventing a path that is not in the diff (**#830**). **The general rule, which is the round's real deliverable: before reading a mutation score for a function, check which input *shapes* its fixtures build — the score is bounded by them.** This is a second, independent ceiling on the same number, sitting beside the tool-side one reading #5 measured (cargo-mutants has exactly two genres, so 93 clamp sites across `src/` are unaskable): that ceiling is set by what the instrument can *generate*, this one by what the fixtures can *ask*, and a function can sit at 0% survival while an entire branch has never been entered. **Round 81's own grades: 1 hit / 1 miss.** The hit (h1, the pure rename) was derivable from a mechanism I could state before reading anything — the walker keys on `---`/`+++` prefixes, a shape emitting neither cannot be recorded. The miss (h2) asserted that git emits `--- a/x` / `+++ b/x` before `Binary files … differ`; it does not, and one `git show` would have settled it — a claim about an **external tool's output format** that I did not check, i.e. a coin flip wearing a prior's clothes, which is the losing half of the derivable-vs-choice-dependent split running since round 73. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #829) — and the sentence it corrects is two paragraphs up, inside the round-81 record, so read them together.** That paragraph ends "…which `strip_prefix("diff --git a/")` does not match, so such a file still vanishes (**#829**…)". True when written, false since this task: **git-quoted paths now decode**, so a non-ASCII rename renders a real scope instead of `feat(): update code`. The half worth keeping from the superseded sentence is its parenthetical, which is still exactly true and is now pinned as a near-miss guard rather than an aside — a path with a plain **space** is *not* quoted by git (`diff --git a/we ird.txt b/we ird.txt`), so it takes the unquoted branch and is byte-identical to before. **What changed, and it is two sites rather than the one #829 named:** the quoted branch is checked **before** the unquoted one in `diff_header_path` (git quotes *both* paths or neither, and the closing quote of the first token *is* the separator, so the quoted form has none of the ` b/` ambiguity the unquoted one has), and the same decoding was needed on the hunk lines — `quoted_hunk_path(line, marker, side)` reads `--- "a/<path>"` / `+++ "b/<path>"`, without which a *modified* or *deleted* non-ASCII file was still dropped even with the header fixed; both `+++` and `---` arms call it as an `.or_else` fallback after the plain-prefix read, so every unquoted shape stays on its original code path. `split_first_quoted` finds the token boundary by walking `char_indices` and honouring `\\` escapes, so a `\"` inside the name cannot end the token early and **no index is ever taken on a byte offset that could fall inside a char** (#250). **The escape set covered, named rather than implied:** three-digit octal byte escapes (`\303\244`, decoded into a `Vec<u8>` and interpreted as UTF-8 exactly once, at the end — they are *bytes*, not chars), plus `\"`, `\\`, `\t`, `\n`, `\r`. **Everything else refuses — returns `None`, so the file is dropped exactly as it was before, never guessed at:** a byte sequence that is not valid UTF-8, an escape git is not known to emit, an octal run shorter than three digits, a lone trailing backslash, an unterminated quoted token, and a header whose second token carries trailing junk. Inventing a path that is not in the diff is the failure mode round 81's refusal branch already chose against, and this branch makes the same choice for the same reason. **#830 remains open and unfixed, and that is the deliberate half:** a path containing a literal `" b/"` still makes an *unquoted* header genuinely ambiguous, and `diff_header_path` still refuses rather than guessing — closing it needs a design decision about which anchor to trust, so it was filed rather than smuggled in here, and its refusal row is pinned by test so this task could not weaken it while adding the quoted branch. Fixtures were captured verbatim from real `git show` output in a scratch repo rather than hand-typed (round 81 lost a bet to exactly that shortcut): a 100%-similarity rename `diff --git "a/n\303\244me.txt" "b/n\303\240me2.txt"` → `feat(nàme2): update code`, an escaped quote in the filename, a modified and a deleted quoted path (both via the hunk lines), a quoted binary change, and git's trailing-tab separator on `+++` lines. Near-miss guards pin that the spaced unquoted path, an ordinary modification, an added file and a whole-file deletion are all byte-identical to before — a discriminator tested only where it fires is vacuous green. Positive control run rather than assumed: neutering the unquoting branch fails exactly the new quoted tests and leaves every near-miss guard green.
- `prompt.rs` — prompt execution, agent interaction, streaming event handling, auto-retry logic. A fatal `StopReason::Error` (yoagent 0.14.1+: dropped tool-call arguments, or `pause_turn` with no `error_message`) is recorded in `PromptEventState::fatal_error` and surfaces as `PromptResult::FatalError` → `PromptOutcome.last_api_error` — a caller-visible error that is **never auto-retried** (surface-and-stop, #646). `into_result` checks `fatal_error` *before* `retriable_error` so a fatal message that incidentally contains a retriable keyword still stops; overflow and known-benign "stream ended" keep their existing paths. File-change tracking lives in two places in `PromptEventState`, and the split is forced by the data: `write_file`/`edit_file` are recorded at **ToolExecutionStart** off their single `path` argument, while `rename_symbol` is recorded at **ToolExecutionEnd** by `record_rename_tool_writes`, reading the `files_written` details key and only when `is_error` is false (#783 — a multi-file rename's written set does not exist until the call returns). Day 174 (#808): `PromptOutcome` carries `text_since_last_tool` beside `text` — a parallel accumulator in `PromptEventState`, pushed at the same two sites as `collected_text` and **cleared at every ToolExecutionEnd** (errored tools clear too: the field answers "did the model say anything after the last thing it did"), mirrored in `handle_stream_json_events` with the same clearing rule so the two event paths cannot drift; the auto-retry wrappers carry the **last** attempt's value (overwrite, not append), and `should_auto_continue`'s silence branch reads this field while `text` keeps its whole-turn meaning for its four other consumers. Day 170 (#786): both halves are **free functions with two call sites each**, one per event path, so the display path and `--output-format json` cannot drift — the argument-based half is `record_tool_arg_writes(tool_name, args, changes)` (the `write_file` → `ChangeKind::Write` / `edit_file` → `ChangeKind::Edit` match, extracted from the inline match that used to live in `handle_tool_execution_start`), called from `handle_tool_execution_start` and from `handle_stream_json_events`'s `ToolExecutionStart` arm; the end-based half is `record_rename_tool_writes`, called from `handle_tool_execution_end` and from `handle_stream_json_events`'s `ToolExecutionEnd` arm, gated on `!is_error` at both. `handle_stream_json_events` and its two public callers `run_prompt_stream_json` / `run_prompt_stream_json_with_content` therefore take a `changes: &SessionChanges`, threaded from the **caller's own** tracker in `main.rs` (`run_single_prompt`, `run_piped_mode` — the single `SessionChanges::new()` binding each already had, moved above the stream-json early return; a throwaway tracker there would be #678's defect again). Day 172 (#794 half (a)): both `ToolExecutionStart` sites also call `changes.record_tool_call()` immediately beside `record_tool_arg_writes` — **both or neither**, the sweep #786 had to come back and repair. It is counted at **Start**, not End, deliberately: a tool that errors or never returns still means the turn did something. That counter is what the auto-continue gate reads (see the `session.rs` bullet); pinned at the emission point by `display_path_counts_a_read_only_tool_call` and `stream_json_counts_a_read_only_tool_call`, which drive a `read_file` start through each path and assert `tool_call_count() == 1` while `edit_count() == 0` — the whole defect in one assertion. Day 177: `apply_effort_hint` was split into a one-line wrapper that reads the process-global `cli_config::effort_level()` and the pure `apply_effort_hint_with(level, input)` holding the whole decision — production behaviour is **byte-identical**, the wrapper is the only global read, and the point is the tests: the three `test_apply_effort_hint_*` tests each wrote `EFFORT_LEVEL` (an `AtomicU8`) and raced each other on libtest's parallel threads, so `cargo test --bin yoyo apply_effort_hint` failed **6 of 12** runs while `--test-threads=1` failed 0 of 6 — a flaky red, which in `scripts/evolve.sh` means `git reset --hard` on whatever correct work was sitting beside it (CI run `32748888895` on `f3719406`). They now pass an explicit `EffortLevel` and touch no global, and **no `#[serial]` was added** — it only serialises against other `#[serial]` tests and would leave the class alive for the next test to trip on, plus a false record of which tests are dangerous (the #780 reason). The wrapper's global read is pinned by `test_apply_effort_hint_wrapper_reads_the_global_level`, a deliberately **weak** source-level check (needle built at runtime so it cannot match itself) — it proves the call is present, not that its result is used — verified against a broken wrapper before landing rather than only in the passing direction. **Deliberately not unified:** `run_prompt_with_content_and_changes` re-reads the global and builds its own `[Effort: {hint}]` as a *separate* `Content::Text` block, which is provably **not** the same string as the wrapper's `[Effort: {hint}]\n\n{input}`, so routing it through the new helper would change what reaches the model; the duplication stands and the second path stays untested. Day 181 (**the third door**): that same two-path split is why the external-tool-failure note needed **two** seams and got them in one diff — `apply_external_failure_note_with(note, input)` on the text path and `prepend_external_failure_block_with(note, blocks)` on the image/content path, the latter inserting a **new leading `Content::Text` block** rather than splicing into an existing one (`content` may carry images). Both read the **same** one-shot (`agent_builder::take_external_failure_note()`, which drains), so the content path cannot re-fire what the text path already consumed; **`None` returns the input unchanged byte-identically on both**, which is every user whose servers all connect and the whole regression surface. Wiring one and not the other would have been the "two doors, one policy, one deaf" defect this change is about, one layer down. See the MCP gotchas section for why the system prompt cannot carry it. Day 181 (#856 part 2): the `AgentEvent` match gained arms for yoagent 0.18's **`LoopDetected`** and **`ContextCompacted`**, which the `_ =>` wildcard had been swallowing — and the first one matters because it can **end a run**: `ExecutionLimits::default()` carries `max_consecutive_identical_tool_calls: Some(3)`, so three byte-identical consecutive tool calls (same name *and* same args) steer the model, and a later repeat of that signature **aborts**, which without an arm is a run that just **stops with no stated reason**. The wording is the pure, table-tested `loop_detected_notice(tool_name, repetitions, aborted, plain)`, and the two escalations are deliberately **different sentences, not one with a flag**: `aborted: false` is a warning that names the next consequence (a further repeat stops the run), while `aborted: true` states outright that yoyo **stopped the run deliberately** and that this is **not a provider or tool failure** — the confident-wrong-diagnosis rule, since a deliberate guard firing must never read as a crash (#710's short-circuit, one subsystem over). **Both event paths are wired through ONE emitter, `announce_loop_detected`** — the display handler and `handle_stream_json_events` — which is the `record_tool_arg_writes` / `record_rename_tool_writes` discipline and the reason this is not the eighth instance of "two doors, one policy, one deaf" (#745, #767, #769, #816, `/config show`, sub-agent fallback, #852); in the json path the full-fidelity event **already went out on the NDJSON wire** through the generic `emit_agent_event`, so the arm adds only the line a *human* watching stderr reads. Both notices are silent under `format::is_quiet()` and glyph-free under `format::is_plain_output()` — markers, em dashes **and** `ContextCompacted`'s `→`, which an assertion caught rather than review, the near-miss guard earning its keep. **The `Some(3)` default was KEPT, and that is now a recorded decision rather than an inheritance:** all three `ExecutionLimits::default()` sites in `src/agent_builder.rs` carry a comment naming the value, #856, and the reasoning — three byte-identical *consecutive* calls is not a shape legitimate work produces (an `edit_file` between two `cargo test` runs is a different signature and resets the streak), and the design fires a **warning shot** first, so killing a legitimate run requires the agent to ignore an explicit steer. **The stated limit, which is the honest bound on what it buys: different-signature calls always reset, so an alternating `[a, b, a, b]` loop is NOT caught** <!-- yoagent-version-claim: 0.18.1 -->. Pinned by four table tests (the two escalations produce different text and the aborted one says the run stopped; tool name and count appear verbatim; plain mode carries no glyph and no em dash) plus `both_event_paths_handle_the_loop_guard`, a deliberately **weak** source-level guard whose needles are assembled at runtime and which slices each handler's body so a hit in one cannot vouch for the other — its doc comment states its own limit: it proves the arm is *present*, never that it fires. Positive control **run rather than assumed**: neutering the notice function to return an empty string reddens exactly the notice tests and leaves everything else green. **Deliberately not done, named rather than implied:** `AgentEnd`'s `SessionStats` rollup is still discarded by the `..`, and `CacheConfig::session_key` is untouched — both are separate features.
- `repl.rs` — interactive REPL loop, tab-completion, multi-line input, `!` shell passthrough (`parse_bang_command` — run a shell command directly with zero tokens, output streamed live; output is also teed into an in-memory "last bang result" — `BangResult`, `tail_for_capture`, char-boundary-safe ~8KB/200-line tail — and `!?` (`parse_bang_query`, checked before the generic `!` parse) feeds it into the conversation via `build_bang_followup_prompt`, with a dim hint printed after non-zero exits), auto-continue for incomplete responses (`should_auto_continue` = yoagent 0.9 `follow_up_queue_len()` as an authoritative pending-work signal, falling back to the `looks_incomplete` text heuristic when the queue is empty; product-safe — no-op for providers that don't populate the queue, up to 5 follow-ups per user turn; when auto-continue is about to fire it also surfaces a dim one-line hint of the next queued follow-up via `follow_up_queue_snapshot()` (`format_followup_hint` — char-boundary-safe truncation, silent when the queue is empty/unsupported); Day 150 added an **opt-in** fourth input, `should_auto_continue(text, queue_pending, ran_tools, continue_on_silence)` (the parameter was named `used_tools` until Day 172) — with `--continue-on-silence` (default OFF, `cli::set_continue_on_silence`/`is_continue_on_silence`, read at the call site so the helper stays pure) a turn that **used tools**, ended **without an error**, has an **empty queue**, and produced **under `MIN_SUMMARY_CHARS` (20)** trimmed chars of final text also auto-continues — the issue #631 abstention case made an explicit third value instead of being absorbed into "finished"; a no-tools silent turn never loops, and off-mode is pinned byte-identical to the old two-argument logic by `should_auto_continue_default_off_is_byte_identical_to_legacy`. Off by default because yoyo cannot distinguish "stopped mid-work" from "finished quietly"; the `get_max_auto_continues` budget bounds the worst case at 5 wasted turns per prompt, not an infinite loop. Day 174 (#808): the silence branch judges a **fifth input**, `text_since_last_tool` — text emitted *after* the turn's last tool result — instead of the whole-turn `text`, which still feeds `looks_incomplete` unchanged. The whole-turn measure made the branch structurally unable to fire: agents narrate before every tool batch ("Let me trace the mechanism."), so `text` was always over `MIN_SUMMARY_CHARS` even when the model said nothing after its last tool — measured 0 firings over 11 opted-in sessions against 16 A1/A2 abstentions, all with that shape. Pinned by `announced_intent_then_closing_silence_fires_the_gate_808`, whose precondition asserts the tail is NOT caught by `looks_incomplete`'s phrase list (deliberately unbroadened: a generic "let me " pattern would false-positive on "Let me know if…" for non-opted-in product users). Day 171 (#794 half (b)): `should_auto_continue` and `get_max_auto_continues` (both already `pub(crate)`) have a **second caller** — `main.rs`'s `run_piped_mode`, which had no auto-continue of any kind, so `is_continue_on_silence()` was read at exactly one site that the evolve loop's piped agents never reach. Only the **decision** is shared: the REPL's loop body was deliberately *not* hoisted, because it is entangled with `turn_count`, `handle_post_prompt`, `TurnSnapshot`, `spawn_tracker` and `repl_file_config`, none of which exist in piped mode; the piped side duplicates ~20 lines of sequencing while calling this predicate and this bound, and both call sites carry a comment saying that is the intended trade. Nothing in this file changed and the REPL's behaviour is byte-identical — in particular the piped loop is gated *as a whole* on `is_continue_on_silence()` (default OFF; since Day 172 that reads the `--continue-on-silence` flag **or** the `continue_on_silence` config key) rather than reaching REPL parity, so no existing `yoyo -p` user gets an extra turn; see the `main.rs` bullet for why (#448)), a turn-end marker after the auto-continue loop (`classify_turn_end`/`format_turn_end` — pure helpers returning `TurnEnd::{Done,Paused,BudgetSpent}` and one dim stderr line stating *why yoyo believes the turn stopped*; informational only, never changes auto-continue behavior, gated on the turn having changed ≥1 file, silent under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`; under `cli::is_verbose()` an observation-shaped line is printed *above* the verdict — `format_turn_end_debug`, the raw classifier inputs plus the verdict they produced, pure ASCII so one string serves both output modes, and printed on every ended turn including the ones the verdict declines to speak about), contextual command hints after prompt turns
- `watch.rs` — watch mode: set/get/clear watch command(s), run watch command with streaming output, multi-phase watch (lint → fix → test → fix), auto-fix loop after prompts with command-type-aware fix prompts and structured Rust compiler error parsing (`CompilerError`, `parse_rust_errors`, category-specific hints) (extracted from `prompt.rs`), `/watch` command handler and project-type detection for auto-watch; the post-prompt watch cycle is skipped entirely when the prompt changed zero files (`should_run_watch_after_prompt` gate). Day 182 (**blind round 87** — the second application of round 81's **fixture-shape census** method, and the first test of whether it generalises; **0 hit / 1 partial / 2 miss**, and the fix again came from the census rather than from any bet). **Scope: `parse_rust_errors`, `CompilerError`, this file's own `parse_rust_errors_*` fixtures, and `build_watch_fix_prompt`'s *use* of the returned `Vec`** — the watch runner, multi-phase sequencing, `/watch` handler and project-type detection were **not** read, and the ledger's `scope_limit` says so (3532 lines; this round read a few hundred). **The method, restated because it is the deliverable:** a mutation score is bounded by what the fixtures can *ask*, so enumerate which input **shapes** the fixtures construct, then capture verbatim tool output and diff the two. **The fix:** real `cargo test` output on rustc 1.65+ is `thread 'tests::t' (4861) panicked at src/main.rs:7:9:` with the assertion on the **next** line, while **both** in-file fixtures pin the **pre-1.65** shape (quoted message, location last). So Pattern 3 set `file: None, line: None` while `src/main.rs:7:9` sat in the header it had just matched, and `message` was the header alone — the assertion text (`assertion \`left == right\` failed: …`) dropped entirely. The consequence is not cosmetic: `build_watch_fix_prompt` calls `extract_error_source_context(&errors)`, which needs `file`/`line`, so **every test-failure fix prompt carried no source context and no assertion text**, and `scripts/evolve.sh` spends up to 10 build-fix attempts on the strength of it. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `modern_panic_location(line) -> Option<(String, u32)>`, and **the discriminator is a trailing colon**: the modern header ends `…:7:9:` while the legacy shape ends `…:99:9` with none, so **the legacy path is byte-identical** — that is every pre-1.65 capture and the entire regression surface, pinned by `parse_rust_errors_legacy_panic_shape_is_byte_identical` with `assert_eq!` on **all three** fields rather than a `contains`. Both the line *and* the column must parse as integers, so a panic message that merely happens to end in `:` cannot be mistaken for a location, and a quoted tail (the legacy message) returns `None`. The modern fixture is captured **verbatim from a real failure in a scratch crate, not hand-typed** — round 81's own losing bet asserted an external tool's output format without running it once. Pinned at the **emission point** (the `Vec<CompilerError>` a caller receives), with the near-miss guard beside it, since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. **Positive control run rather than assumed, and serially** (two file-mutating controls in one parallel block raced once and one falsely passed): neutering `modern_panic_location` to always return `None` reddens **exactly 2** tests — the modern fixture and its table — while the legacy near-miss guard and all 14 pre-existing `parse_rust_errors_*` tests stay green; restoring returns 112/112. The register entry moved `3532 → 3652` in the same diff rather than absorbing the drift (+120 is past `REGISTER_DRIFT_GRACE_LINES = 100`, i.e. fatal, which is the gate working). **Two findings recorded and FILED rather than fixed** (one defect per round, and Day 179's evidence is that the finding routed to the scheduler surface was fixed within the day while the two left in prose were not): **#859** — patterns 1 and 2 use `strip_prefix("error[")` / `strip_prefix("error: ")`, **anchored and not even trimmed**, so ANSI-coloured output yields **zero** compiler errors, which is product-real for any user with `color = "always"` in `.cargo/config.toml` or `CARGO_TERM_COLOR=always` in CI, and asymmetric in the confusing direction because Pattern 3 uses `contains()` so coloured *panics* still parse; **#860** — `extract_location` scans `start+1..start+6` without stopping at the next diagnostic header, so a location-less error followed within 5 lines by one that has a `-->` can **absorb the neighbour's location** and feed `extract_error_source_context` a confident wrong pointer (**structurally present, NOT empirically confirmed** — the window bounds were read, no real capture was constructed, and naming a hole without checking it would be inventing one). **The grades, and the losing bets are worth more than the fix.** h1 (an error with no `-->` is *dropped*) **missed**: the entry is emitted with `(None, None)`. h2 (a `cargo test` panic yields **zero** entries) **missed**: Pattern 3 exists explicitly — the exact "an existence bet on a guard can only lose in a maintained file" trap my own blindfold note warned against, committed anyway. h3 (ANSI) is **partial**: its mechanism half was exactly right and its consequence half overstated, because I claimed *zero* entries and Pattern 3's `contains()` survives colour. **The transferable half: both `derivable: true` bets lost, breaking a streak that stood at derivable 9 hit of 12 across rounds 82–86 — and they lost for one shared reason.** I tagged them derivable because I could state a mechanism, but the mechanism was about **the code's structure** (how many branches the parser has; whether an entry is emitted without a location), not about **the input format**. A mechanism about code structure inside a file I have not read *is* the choice-dependent class wearing a derivable tag. Refinement, to be scored from round 88 on: `derivable: true` only when the mechanism is a property of the **input** or of an external tool's grammar. **Shapes checked and left unchecked, stated rather than implied:** captured and compared — a plain `E0308` build error and a real `cargo test` panic; **not** captured — a warning-only build, an `error:` with no `-->` at all (linker/manifest; confirmed by reading to be emitted with a `None` location, but no captured fixture exercises it), real `clippy -D warnings` output, and multi-crate workspace path prefixes. **The method's own result: round 81's census generalises — second application, second unrelated parser of an external format, one real high-consequence defect found the same way. Two for two.** Day 175 (#818, found by blind round 71): that gate takes a **per-turn baseline** — `should_run_watch_after_prompt(changes, edits_before)` is `changes.edit_count() > edits_before`, and `run_watch_after_prompt` threads an `edits_before: usize` from each caller. It used to be `!changes.snapshot().is_empty()`, i.e. the **session-wide** deduped file list, which `SessionChanges::clear()` only empties on `/clear` — so once anything had been edited in the session it returned `true` forever and the predicate could not answer the per-turn question its own doc comment claims ("no write/edit tool calls **this turn**"). **The unit that matters here is call sites, not the function**, and that is why the defect survived: three of the four callers were safe for three *different* reasons — `repl.rs` gates the call on its own outer per-turn delta (`edit_count() > ctx.edits_before`, from #774), and both `main.rs` sites are per-process, where session-wide *is* per-turn (they pass a literal `0`, so their behaviour is byte-identical). The fourth, `conversations.rs`'s `/extended`, had **no** outer guard, so the broken predicate was the only gate: after any earlier edit in the session, a zero-change `/extended` turn ran the full lint→fix→test cycle and, on failure, `build_watch_fix_prompt` told the agent *"Your changes caused failures"* about a pre-existing failure the turn did not cause — precisely the misattribution the gate exists to prevent, costing a wasted watch run plus up to `MAX_WATCH_FIX_ATTEMPTS = 3` API fix turns aimed at a lie. That call site samples its baseline **before** the prompt runs (a baseline read afterwards would already contain the turn's own writes — the Day-165 shape, where a guard that reads the world after its own action is blinded by that action). The **counter**, not the vec length, is the right input: a turn that re-edits an already-touched file grows `edit_count()` while leaving `snapshot().len()` unchanged. **Why no test caught it:** both pre-existing gate tests drive a *fresh* `SessionChanges`, where session-wide and per-turn state are identical, so neither could distinguish the two readings — vacuous green in exactly the direction that mattered. Both are kept verbatim (baseline `0` preserves their exact meaning) and a third drives a **non-fresh** tracker, asserting the skip in the direction the old code got wrong *and* the near-miss that must still pass through (re-editing the same file is work this turn), with an explicit precondition that the deduped set did not grow. Verified to fail against the old body before landing, so it is a real regression guard rather than a fixture agreeing with the fix.
- `prompt_budget.rs` — session wall-clock budget + audit log helpers (extracted from `prompt.rs`). Day 180 (#848): `.yoyo/audit.jsonl` carries a **second line kind** — one **per-run usage record** beside the existing per-tool-call entries. **What is written:** `usage_audit_record(usage, model, duration, num_turns, is_error)` (pure, so the emission point is testable) builds `ts`, `type: "usage"`, `model`, the four token counts (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`), `cost_usd`, `duration_ms`, `num_turns` and `is_error`; the I/O is `audit_log_usage_when(enabled, …)` with `audit_log_usage` as the thin wrapper that is the **only** global read (`is_audit_enabled()`), the `apply_effort_hint`/`apply_effort_hint_with` split. **The measured defect, which is the reason the sink is a file:** @yuanhao measured the dashboard's cost figure frozen at **$1,077.59 for 102 days** — it never went to zero, it stopped growing, so no non-zero check could see it (**a frozen number is not a zero**). Two changes closed the only channel: Day 73 broke the dashboard's regex on the usage line, and Day 77 (`f38e9be1`) closed the leak that let `print_usage` through quiet mode. `cli.rs:1312` auto-enables quiet whenever **both stdin and stdout are non-terminal**, and `scripts/evolve.sh` pipes the prompt from a file and pipes stdout to `tee` — so **every CI session is quiet by construction** and printed no tokens and no cost. The numbers were never missing: `build_json_output` (`src/main.rs:170`) already computed every one of them, including `cost_usd` from `estimate_cost(usage, model)`, and emitted them only under `--output-format json`, which `evolve.sh` does not pass. **Nothing needed computing; it needed persisting.** **So this is a file write specifically so that quiet mode cannot suppress it** — printing to stderr would reintroduce the exact bug — and `scripts/evolve.sh:3419` already copies this file to `$SESSION_STAGING` and pushes it to the `audit-log` branch, so the data reaches the dashboard with **no harness change**, which matters because `evolve.sh` is protected and cannot be edited to fix it. **`"type": "usage"` is what distinguishes the two kinds, and the compatibility rule runs the other way: a line with NO `type` key still means a tool call.** Every reader of this file predates the usage record, so `write_audit_entry` deliberately carries no `type` and its output shape is byte-unchanged — a comment at that literal says so, and `tool_call_lines_are_unchanged_and_still_carry_no_type_field` pins it, because the whole regression surface is every existing reader. Both kinds go through **one** writer, `write_audit_line` (path resolution + append), rather than a second `OpenOptions` on the same path — two writers on one file is how two formats drift. **`cost_usd` is `null`, never `0.0`, for a model with no price:** `format::estimate_cost` returns `Option<f64>`, and a consumer must be able to tell *"no price for this model"* from *"this run was free"* (the absence-gets-its-own-name rule; folding them is how the frozen figure would have been rebuilt one layer down). **Product-safe by construction:** the write is gated on the pre-existing `is_audit_enabled()`, so a user who never passed `--audit` / set `YOYO_AUDIT=1` / wrote `audit = true` gets **no new file and byte-identical behaviour** — pinned by `audit_disabled_writes_nothing_and_creates_no_file`; there is no new config key, no new directory, no flag, and nothing is written to stdout or stderr. Tests assert on **the bytes in the file** (a tempdir via the `#[cfg(test)]` `with_audit_base_dir` thread-local seam, never the real `.yoyo/audit.jsonl`, and never that a function returned `Ok`): the record round-trips all four token fields plus a `cost_usd` **derived from `estimate_cost` rather than hardcoded** (a literal would pass even if the record were built from the wrong fields), appends rather than overwrites, and leaves the tool-call line parsing as before. **Two limits, stated rather than implied.** (1) **The three-mode and quiet-mode properties are pinned by *source-level* guards, not by driving `emit_output` three times**: `emit_output` prints to stdout and is not unit-drivable, so `main_tests.rs`'s `emit_output_records_usage_before_branching_on_the_output_mode` asserts the `audit_log_usage(` call sits **above** the `if print_mode {` branch (needles assembled at runtime so the test cannot match itself), and `usage_writing_path_never_consults_quiet_or_the_output_mode` asserts the write path's source contains no `is_quiet()` / `is_plain_output()` / `print_mode` / `json_output` (comment lines stripped first, or the doc comment *documenting* the fix would satisfy the test). They prove the call is **positioned and ungated**, never that a given run wrote a line — the same deliberately-weak discipline the `format/mod.rs` wrapper guards state about themselves. (2) **This records usage; it does NOT yet check that usage is being recorded.** Nothing anywhere asks *"of the last N sessions on `audit-log`, how many carry ≥1 usage record?"* — so the exact failure shape that hid for 102 days (a channel that silently stops producing, with no consumer that notices) is **not** closed by this task, only re-opened. That coverage check is the named follow-up on **#848** (three states, not two — *recorded* / *absent* / *could not check* — and a consumer that **acts** rather than prints, since a warning nothing reads is the `tests/module_size.rs` branch-2 lesson: eight days of a visible warning that no reader consumed). Deliberately **not** started here, because "implemented step 1 correctly, never reached step N" was four of four reverts in the Day 159–160 window. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 181, the #848 follow-up):** the sentence above — *"Nothing anywhere asks 'of the last N sessions on `audit-log`, how many carry ≥1 usage record?'"* — was true for exactly one day and is false as of that follow-up. `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` now asks precisely that question every session and renders the answer as *k of N* into the trajectory block handed to Phases A1 and A2 (`classify_session_usage` / `usage_coverage` / `collect_usage_coverage` / `render_usage_coverage` — see that bullet for the three states, the coverage-not-magnitude rule and the anti-vacuous empty-scan branch). The three-states-not-two half of the requirement landed intact: *recorded* / *absent* / *could not check*, never folded. **The half that did NOT land is named here rather than letting the correction read as complete:** the follow-up also asked for a consumer that **acts** rather than prints, and what shipped *prints* — one line in a prompt. That is a **reader** surface, not a gate: nothing fails, reverts, or files an issue when the coverage line reads `0 of N`, so whether it is consumed still depends on a planner reading it, which is the same dependency the `tests/module_size.rs` branch-2 warning had for the eight days nobody acted on it. What it does buy is that the question is now **asked out loud every session** instead of asked nowhere at all, and that a frozen producer surfaces as `0/N` on the very next session rather than as a number that merely stops growing.
- `prompt_retry.rs` — error diagnosis and retry logic: retry prompt construction, exponential backoff, error classification, API error diagnosis (extracted from `prompt.rs`). Day 174 (blind round 65, the darkest non-risk room at 145 snapshots unobserved — **all three registered hypotheses lost** (0 hit / 1 partial / 2 miss); this was an **unpredicted find while grading h1**, and the ledger says so): `diagnose_api_error` decided four of its five branches with **bare substring matches on three-digit HTTP status codes** (`lower.contains("401")`, `"402"`, `"429"`, `"403"`) — the most collidable shape there is inside an error string that routinely carries other numbers. A context-overflow error reading `prompt is too long: 402134 tokens > 200000 maximum` contains `402`, so it was diagnosed as **exhausted credits**, telling the user "This is not a transient error — retrying won't help" when the real remedy is `/compact`. A confident *wrong* diagnosis in error-recovery code is worse than none, because the user acts on it. All four sites now go through the pure `contains_status_code(haystack, code)`, which requires a **digit** boundary on both sides — deliberately **not** a word boundary, so `HTTP 401`, `status=401`, `(401)` and `401:` all still match while `402134`, `4290ms` and `14012` no longer do; it never slices on a byte index that could fall inside a char (matches start on ASCII digits by construction, and the boundary checks read bytes rather than slicing). Pinned at the **emission point** — the string `diagnose_api_error` returns, not the helper one layer below: a token-count error must not read as billing *or* auth, a request-id's digits must not read as a 401, and the **near-miss guard** that a real standalone `401` still reaches the auth branch (a discriminator tested only on the side that blocks is vacuous green). **Deliberately left alone:** `tool_recovery_hint`'s hand-written tool-name table has drifted behind `agent_builder::BUILTIN_TOOL_NAMES` — there is no arm for `shared_state`, `todo` or `ask_user`, so they fall to the `_` catch-all (round 65's h2, graded *partial*: `sub_agent`, the other instance named, does have arms in both branches). Not fixed, and not for lack of clock — the catch-all returns an honest generic hint, so the fallback is degraded-but-true rather than wrong, and adding arms would be cargo-culting coverage where the absence costs nothing observable. Also confirmed **already correct** and worth not re-litigating: the provider named in an auth diagnosis comes from `cli::configured_provider()` → `cli::provider_api_key_env` (#590), and both retry-prompt builders cap the interpolated error via `safe_truncate` (200 / 300 chars). **Superseded reading, recorded rather than erased (Day 181, #852) — and the sentence that misled is Day 174's own, four clauses up.** "All four sites now go through the pure `contains_status_code`" is **true**, and it is true *of `diagnose_api_error`*, which is the function it names; read as a statement about the **file** it is false, because the sibling `is_retriable_error` — same module, same three-digit codes, same collidable shape — was **never swept**. Its non-retriable list carried `"400"`/`"401"`/`"402"`/`"403"`/`"404"` and is scanned **first** with an early `return false`, so `error: Rate limited, retry after Some(14454000)ms` — the digits `400` sitting inside `14454000` — classified as a **terminal client error**. That is the exact string CLAUDE.md records as killing 2 of the 4 gradeable post-#808 sessions. **The consequence is worse than a wrong label, because every link in the chain is single-sited**: `is_retriable_error` returns `false` → `PromptEventState::retriable_error` is never set (one assignment site in `src/prompt.rs`, gated on exactly this predicate) → `PromptResult::RetriableError` is never constructed → **`retry_wait_decision` is never called**, so `--wait-for-reset` (shipped Day 178, documented, opt-in, config key, 6h ceiling) **could not fire for the one error shape it was built for**, and ordinary exponential backoff was silently disabled for the same message. Seventh instance of "two doors, one policy, one deaf" (#745, #767, #769, #816, `/config show`, sub-agent fallback) — and the first where both doors are **in the same file**, which is precisely why a topical sweep missed it: the class is *numeric matching*, not *diagnosis*. **The fix routes all ten numeric entries — the five terminal and the five transient (`429`/`500`/`502`/`503`/`504`) — through the same `contains_status_code`**, in one diff and in **both** lists, since fixing only the list that bit me leaves the mirror alive and a code buried in a longer digit run is just as wrong in the retriable direction (measured: `request id 14290173` contains `429` and used to classify as retriable). They are lifted into two named consts (`NON_RETRIABLE_STATUS_CODES` / `RETRIABLE_STATUS_CODES`) so the phrase lists keep plain `contains`; **no second matcher was written** — one rule, one statement, one table test, the discipline `significant_braces` and `char_literal_len` already follow. **Membership and precedence are untouched**: which codes are terminal did not change, and the terminal family is still scanned in full first, so a message carrying both still wins as terminal (pinned by the pre-existing `terminal_family_wins_when_a_message_carries_both`, which was not edited). **The change is a narrowing, and that is what makes it safe on a product default** — a digit boundary can only make matching stricter, so the only classifications that move are ones where a code matched inside a longer number; the reasoning is written at the call site so a later reader does not "simplify" the helper away. The Day-174 characterization test that **pinned the defect on purpose** (`recorded_defect_status_code_substring_match_hides_a_real_rate_limit`) was **inverted and renamed in the same diff** — a fixture asserting a known-wrong output that outlives its fix converts a defect into a green invariant — keeping its mechanism assertions so a regression cannot be mistaken for something about the words "rate limited". Pinned at the **emission point** (the `bool` a caller of `is_retriable_error` receives) in both directions, with the **near-miss guards as the half that matters**: every genuine standalone terminal shape (`HTTP 400`, `401 Unauthorized`, `402 Payment Required`, `status=403`, `(404)`, `error 400:`, `402:`) must still be non-retriable, and a real `429`/`504` still retriable. **Positive control run rather than assumed, and serially**: neutering both call sites back to plain `contains` fails **exactly 2** tests — the rate-limit fix and its retriable-side mirror — while **every near-miss guard stays green**, which is the direction that proves the guards test the pass-through rather than the fix; restoring returns 114/114. **Deliberately out of scope and named rather than smuggled in: the non-numeric entries are unswept.** `"connection"`, `"timeout"`, `"retry"` and `"capacity"` are broad words that can appear in unrelated prose (`"retry"` matches the very rate-limit string above, which is now harmless but is luck, not design); auditing or narrowing them is a separate behaviour change with its own enumeration burden and was not started.
- `prompt_retry_limits.rs` — rate-limit aware retry (Day 177): the pure trio `retry_after_hint` / `retry_wait_decision` / `rate_limit_giveup_message`, split out of `prompt_retry.rs` in the same task because adding them pushed that file 156 lines past the fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` (registering a brand-new 156-line overshoot would have laundered exactly the design event branch 1 exists to catch). **The measured defect:** `retry_delay` caps at `CAP_SECS = 60` plus ±50% jitter, so ~90s is the longest wait it can ever produce, while `is_retriable_error` classifies a rate limit as retriable and *nothing anywhere parsed a reset time* — so a provider answering `error: Rate limited, retry after Some(14454000)ms` (~4h) was met with five ≤90s sleeps against the same refusal, then a final error that never said when the door reopens. Not hypothetical: **2 of the 4 gradeable post-#808 sessions died this way**, which Day 175 handled by excluding them from a meter (`scripts/measure_abstentions.py`'s `EXCLUDED_PROVIDER_ERROR` bucket) — that treated a capacity loss as noise. `retry_after_hint` reads only the shapes that occur (`retry after Some(N)ms`, `retry after Nms`, `retry_after: N ms`, and the HTTP header `retry-after: N` whose unit is **seconds**), requires an explicit `ms` suffix for the millisecond forms, and returns **`None` for everything else** — including a marker followed by a unit it refuses to guess about (`retry after 5 minutes`), a `u64` overflow, and any number with no marker in front of it, so a token count or request id can never be mistaken for a reset (the Day-174 `contains_status_code` lesson: the whole digit run is consumed, so `144` cannot be read out of `14454000`). `retry_wait_decision(attempt, error_msg, budget_remaining)` returns `Wait` or `GiveUp`, and rule 1 is the load-bearing one: **no hint → `Wait(retry_delay(attempt))`, byte-identical to the pre-existing behaviour**, which is every user whose provider does not name a reset and was the regression risk. A hint longer than the session can wait gives up; a shorter one is honoured (the provider's own number beats an exponential guess), floored at the shared `prompt_retry::MIN_RETRY_DELAY_MS` so `retry after 1ms` cannot spin. `MAX_INLINE_RETRY_AFTER = 120s` is a **judgment threshold, not a measurement** — nothing measured says 120s is right; it is the point past which sleeping is worse than reporting. `budget_remaining` can only make that ceiling *smaller*, never larger (a deliberate deviation from a naive "prefer the budget": a session with three hours left still should not sleep an hour inside one retry). **Both** retry loops consult it — `prompt.rs`'s text path and its image/content path, which were the only two `retry_delay` callers and are now zero, so this is not the "two doors, one works" shape (#745, #767, #769, #816). Pinned at the emission point in both directions: the verbatim observed string, the near-miss guards that must return `None`, the no-hint `Wait` range that *is* the byte-identity assertion, and the `GiveUp` message asserted as the literal string a caller receives (it names the reset in human units via the existing `format::format_duration`, says the stop was **deliberate**, and says a retry will not fix it). Verified by positive control — mutating the `ms` unit and the give-up comparison each kill tests. **What it deliberately does not do, stated plainly: it does not reschedule or resume the session after the limit resets.** That lives in `scripts/evolve.sh`, which is protected and which yoyo may not edit; this only stops wasting the remaining attempts and reports when the door reopens. **Day 178 — the opt-in `--wait-for-reset`, and a superseded claim recorded rather than erased.** The sentence above used to end there, and its stated reason (the resume lives in a protected file) was doing double duty as a *product* design decision it does not license: that reason is true about my evolve loop and irrelevant to a user sitting at a terminal, where there is no `evolve.sh` and the process that must wait is the one already running. It is still true that **nothing here reschedules or resumes a session** — this lets an already-running process *wait*, it restarts nothing. What changed is that a user who would happily leave the terminal open for four hours now has a way to say so. `retry_wait_decision` keeps its exact 3-argument signature (so `prompt.rs`'s two retry loops needed no edit) and becomes a one-line wrapper reading `cli::is_wait_for_reset()` over the pure, table-tested `retry_wait_decision_with(attempt, error_msg, budget_remaining, wait_for_reset)` — the `apply_effort_hint`/`apply_effort_hint_with` split, so a test never writes a process global. **`wait_for_reset == false` is byte-identical to the Day-177 rule on every input**, which is every existing user and was the regression risk, and it is pinned by an explicit equality test that drives a 9-message × 6-budget × 3-attempt table through *both* the new function and a verbatim transcription of the old body (a second statement of the old behaviour, so the new code cannot pass by agreeing with itself). Opted in, the ceiling becomes `min(MAX_RESET_WAIT, budget_remaining)` — the budget still only ever *shrinks* a ceiling, the `MIN_RETRY_DELAY_MS` floor is unchanged, and the no-hint path is untouched. `MAX_RESET_WAIT = 6h` is a **judgment threshold, not a measurement** — nothing measured says 6h is right; it is the point past which a "wait" is really "come back tomorrow", and it exists so that opting in cannot mean an *unbounded* sleep (a 24h reset still gives up, pinned by test). `reset_wait_ceiling` is a separate `fn` beside `inline_retry_ceiling` rather than one parameterised clamp, deliberately: cargo-mutants cannot substitute one method call for another, so a clamp only enters the mutant population when it owns a function boundary (blind round 80). **An invisible multi-hour sleep is a bug even when it is the right sleep** (the #794 `⚡ auto-continuing` rule), so the pure `rate_limit_wait_notice(delay, plain)` returns `None` at or below `MAX_INLINE_RETRY_AFTER` — short waits stay silent, so nothing new prints on the common path — and above it names the wait in human units, says it was **deliberate and opted into**, and names the flag that turns it off; glyph-free under `is_plain_output()` (bullets *and* em dashes), silent under `is_quiet()`, emitted from the impure wrapper so `prompt.rs` stays untouched. Pinned at the emission point in **both** directions, including the inclusive boundary (`120s` silent, `121s` speaks) — a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. Verified by positive control, twice: flipping the flag argument in one assertion row kills a test, and neutering `MAX_RESET_WAIT` to equal `MAX_INLINE_RETRY_AFTER` kills two — a new parameter that changes nothing under test is the vacuous-green shape. **The default is OFF and that is a deliberate divergence from a rival, not an oversight:** Claude Code v2.1.234 (~Aug 20) ships session-continuation-on-reset **default-ON with an opt-out in `/config`**; a process that can silently sleep for hours is not a product-safe default (#448), so yoyo ships the same capability opt-in. The flag is in `KNOWN_FLAGS`, takes no value (so deliberately *not* in the value-taking list `check_flag_values` scans), has one `set_wait_for_reset()` call site in `parse_args`, and is documented in `cli_help_text()` — an undocumented flag is the discoverability defect already fixed three times (#745, #767, #769). It is **deliberately not part of the project-config trust boundary** (`gate_mcp_sources` / `gate_project_permissions` / `gate_project_hooks`) and a comment at the call site says so, because it grants no privilege — the worst case is a process that sleeps — the same reasoning already written down for `continue_on_silence`. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, second task):** this bullet used to end *"**Deliberately not done, named rather than implied: there is no config key.** `wait_for_reset = true` in `.yoyo.toml` (the `config.rs` + `SETTABLE_KEYS` + `validate_config_value` trio) is real follow-up work; the flag is the product door and the config key is separate."* That was true for exactly one day. The config key **shipped**, in the same three-part shape `continue_on_silence` established on Day 172, and the reason it had to is a product one: the whole point of the flag is a person willing to leave the terminal open for four hours, and that person wants to say so **once** rather than retype a flag on every invocation — a capability reachable only by retyping a flag is the shape that trains people to alias it away. `config::parse_wait_for_reset_from_config` joins the `config_flag`-shaped boolean readers (`auto_watch`, `quiet`, `no_bell`, `continue_on_silence`), **default `false`, opt-in**; `"wait_for_reset"` was added to `SETTABLE_KEYS` with its matching `validate_config_value` arm **in the same diff**, so `/config set wait_for_reset true` validates and `/config get wait_for_reset` reads it back — a key nothing can set through the normal door is half a feature. `set_wait_for_reset()` still has **exactly one** call site in `parse_args`, which now has **two sources OR'd together**: the `--wait-for-reset` flag, **or** the config key. There is deliberately **no disable flag** — an OR is the whole rule and a fourth precedence value was not needed. **The shipped default is unchanged: off unless the flag is passed or the key is written**, which is every existing user's path and was the regression risk here; pinned by the three-state reader test (absent → `false`, `= true` → `true`, `= false` → `false`, the last being the near-miss guard so the discriminator is not covered only on the side that fires) and by both positive controls (flipping the reader's default kills a test; renaming the `validate_config_value` arm kills another). The key is **deliberately not** part of the project-config trust boundary (`gate_mcp_sources` / `gate_project_permissions` / `gate_project_hooks`) and a comment at the call site says so: it grants no privilege — a repo's `.yoyo.toml` setting this can cost you wall-clock, never access — unlike an MCP command, a `permissions.allow` entry or a shell hook, whose entire content is executable code. **One thing deliberately left undone and named rather than implied:** `cli_help_text()`'s `--wait-for-reset` line was **not** extended with "(or `wait_for_reset = true` in your config)", because that text lives in `src/help.rs` — a fourth file, past this task's stated three-file scope. The config door is not undocumented, though: its `SETTABLE_KEYS` description ships in the same diff and is what `/config set` lists, so each door is documented where its own audience looks; the cross-reference between them is the missing half.
- `prompt_utils.rs` — message search, highlighting, summarization, output file writing, tool result preview, fenced code block extraction (`extract_code_blocks` — line-based, multi-byte-safe, triple-backtick only) (extracted from `prompt.rs`)
- `session.rs` — session tracking types: SessionChanges, TurnSnapshot, TurnHistory, format_changes (extracted from `prompt.rs`). Day 169 (#774): `SessionChanges` carries a **monotonic edit counter** (`edits: Arc<AtomicUsize>`, read via `edit_count()`, bumped unconditionally by `record` on *both* the dedup and push branches, zeroed by `clear()`) alongside the path-deduped vec, which keeps its exact meaning — `snapshot().len()` still counts **distinct files** and both dedup tests (`test_session_changes_deduplicates_same_path`, `test_to_json_summary_deduplicates_paths`) are untouched. Three **per-turn** checks in `repl.rs` now read the counter instead of that length: the per-turn change summary (`files_modified`), the tool-usage argument to `should_auto_continue` (the `--continue-on-silence` gate — since Day 172 that one reads `tool_call_count()` instead, see below), and `did_work` (the turn-end marker). Why: the deduped length answers a *session-wide* question ("did the session grow its distinct-file set"), and `clear()` only fires from `/clear`, so turns 2..N editing the **same** file grew nothing and all three checks silently read as "no work" — the most common editing shape there is. Day 172 (#794 half (a)): a **second, independent** monotonic counter sits beside it — `tool_calls: Arc<AtomicUsize>`, read via `tool_call_count()`, bumped by `record_tool_call()` and zeroed by the same `clear()`. It counts **every tool call the turn started**, write-class or not (`bash`, `read_file`, `search`, `web_search`, `sub_agent` included), which `edit_count()` structurally cannot: `edits` is bumped only by `record`, i.e. only by `write_file`/`edit_file`/`rename_symbol` writes. The two are pinned as independent by test (`test_tool_call_count_and_edit_count_are_independent` — a `record` does not bump `tool_call_count` and a `record_tool_call` does not bump `edit_count`), because collapsing them *is* the defect #794 half (a) fixed. `edit_count()` and `snapshot().len()` keep their exact prior meanings and every existing test is untouched; the tool-usage argument to `should_auto_continue` (in both `repl.rs` and `main.rs`) is the one thing that moved from `edit_count()` to `tool_call_count()` — the per-turn change summary and the turn-end `did_work` still read the edit counter, correctly, since those are questions about *edits*.
- `commands_project.rs` — `/context`, `/init`, `/docs`, project-type detection, `auto_context_for_prompt` (automatic file injection for prompts — scores repo files against query keywords, returns top matches with content), `format_auto_context`. Day 175 (#817): `auto_context_for_prompt(prompt, recent_context, restrictions: &DirectoryRestrictions)` takes the **caller's** directory restrictions and filters candidate paths through `restrictions.check_path` **before** the read loop — so a denied file is never opened, never consumes one of the `AUTO_CONTEXT_MAX_FILES` slots, and never reaches the signature block. It had no restriction check of any kind (`grep -n 'dir_restrictions\|check_path' src/commands_project.rs` returned zero), while every *model-facing* file tool is wrapped by `maybe_guard`/`maybe_guard_arc` in `src/tools.rs` — so the same file was **refused when the model asked for it** and **silently pasted into the same API request** when auto-context picked it, under a green `📎 Auto-attached: <path>` note. Fourth instance of "same rule, two doors, one deaf" (#745, #767, #769, #816). Threading rather than a process-global: there is exactly **one** non-test call site (`src/repl.rs`, inside `run_repl`, which already holds `agent_config`), so it passes `&agent_config.dir_restrictions`; `commands.rs`'s entry is a name re-export and needed no edit. **When `restrictions.is_empty()` the returned vec is byte-identical to before** — `check_path` short-circuits to `Ok` — which is the case for almost every product user and was the regression risk. **Deliberately no refusal block is printed**: a denied file is simply not attached, because auto-context is a silent convenience the user did not ask for, unlike #748/#761 where the user *asked* for the thing being refused. **What it does not cover, stated plainly:** `permissions.deny` (the bash-command patterns) is a different mechanism and is untouched here; this is the directory fence only. Pinned at the **emission point** — the vec a caller receives — in both directions in one test (`test_auto_context_respects_directory_restrictions`): the denied path is absent when denied, and present with empty restrictions, since a discriminator tested only on the side that blocks is vacuous green. Day 179 (#837) changed **what enters the ranking**, on both sides of the scorer — this path injects files into every user's prompt on every turn, so a wrong ranking is a product defect paid invisibly. (a) **Query side:** keywords are matched as **substrings** against path components and symbol names, so a noise word is not merely useless, it is actively harmful — every file it happens to touch gains points, and per-keyword scoring caps each real keyword's contribution, so enough noise hits outrank a file matching fewer but genuinely relevant terms. `STOP_WORDS` was audited and extended with the scaffolding a natural-language *question* is built from: the auxiliary/modal family (`do`, `does`, `did`, `done`, `be`/`been`/`being`, `am`/`was`/`were`, `has`/`have`/`had`, `can`/`could`/`should`/`would`/`will`/`shall`/`may`/`might`/`must`) and the interrogatives (`how`, `what`, `where`, `when`, `why`, `who`, `whom`, `which`). **`work`/`works`/`working` is a judgment call, not a measurement, and it is deliberately included** — the reasoning is written beside the list, not only here: in "how does the watch loop work" it reads as a content word but discriminates nothing (every file "works"), while as a substring it matches the entire `worker`/`workflow`/`workspace`/`worktree` family, so its expected contribution is noise under *both* readings; a user who actually wants worker code writes `worker`/`worktree`/`workflow`, which are separate tokens and unaffected. The decision is pinned by test either way. (b) **Symbol side:** `score_files` skips symbols whose names look like tests. The measured reason is self-boosting — `src/commands_project.rs` earned `web` and `search` points for this very query from its own test's name, `test_auto_context_web_search_returns_relevant_files` — and the general form is worse: `#[cfg(test)]` names are English prose, and English prose is exactly what a natural-language query matches, so it is a noise source that **grows with every session that adds a test**. **This is scoring only, and that is the whole boundary: `/outline`, `/def`, `/index` and every display path still see every symbol** — it changes what gets *ranked*, never what gets *shown*. **It is a name heuristic, not a real signal**, stated rather than implied: `crate::symbols::Symbol` carries no `#[cfg(test)]` information and adding one would ripple into three other commands (a different task, deliberately not taken), so `symbol_name_looks_like_test` matches a `test_` prefix, a `_test`/`_tests` suffix, and the bare `test`/`tests` (Rust's `mod tests` is a Module symbol named exactly that). The known cost of the approximation: a production symbol genuinely named `test_connection` is dropped from *scoring* — still displayed everywhere, one query ranks it slightly worse. The mandatory near-miss guard is beside it (a real production symbol must still score), since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. (c) **Test side:** `test_auto_context_web_search_returns_relevant_files` used to assert a ranking property of *this repository as it exists right now* — which drifts with every session that adds a test, and whose recency boost (`get_recent_git_files` → `git diff --name-only HEAD~5`) reads the working tree and so behaves differently on shallow vs deep clones (green on CI, red on a full clone). It was **converted, not deleted**: same name, reduced to what it can honestly assert about a live repo (it runs, does not panic, respects `AUTO_CONTEXT_MAX_FILES`, returns no empty entries), with the ranking claim moved to **fixture** tests over synthetic `FileSymbols` — a `commands_web.rs`-shaped fixture ranks first against a `commands_project.rs`-shaped one that matches four keywords, two of them noise and one via a test-named symbol. The positive control was run rather than assumed: the fixture fails against the pre-fix behaviour — and it fails **reproducing the reporter's own numbers**, `src/commands_project.rs` at **score 11** and `src/commands_web.rs` at **score 7, rank 3**, i.e. the fixture preserves the reported defect as a runnable artifact even though the live tree has since stopped exhibiting it (see the measurement below). That is the point of moving the claim into fixtures: a ranking assertion over synthetic input still fails when the scorer regresses, on any tree, at any clone depth.

  **The measurement, dated, and it does not say what the issue predicted — recorded as the reading it is rather than the headline that was wanted.** Taken Day 179 on this tree by driving the real scorer (`build_repo_map` → `tokenize_query` → `score_files`, raw scores, recency boost deliberately excluded so the number is not itself clone-depth-dependent) at `adf87505` and at its parent `6a2304bf`. Query, the issue's: `"how does the web search tool work in this project"`. **Keywords 6 → 4** (`does` and `work` dropped). **`src/commands_web.rs`: rank 2 (score 7, matched `["web","search"]`) before → rank 2 (score 7, same matches) after — unchanged, because it was *already in the top 3 on this tree*.** The reporter's `rank=9` was measured at `6438b3ff` and **does not reproduce here**; so did their `SCORE 11 src/commands_project.rs matched=["web","search","tool","project"]`, which reads `score 5 matched=["project"]` on both sides of this diff. The tree moved between the two readings and I did not determine what in `build_repo_map`'s output changed — stating that plainly beats inventing a mechanism. **The reported numbers are not lost, though, and this is why the fixture route was the right one:** driving the *pre-fix* scorer against the synthetic fixtures reproduces them exactly — `src/commands_project.rs` **11**, `src/commands_web.rs` **7 at rank 3** — so the defect the reporter measured is now pinned in a form that does not depend on which tree it is run against. **What the fix demonstrably did do here is one slot, and it is the slot that matters:** `AUTO_CONTEXT_MAX_FILES = 3`, and rank 3 before was `tests/integration.rs` (score 6, matched `["does","tool","work"]` — **two of its three keywords are exactly the noise words this change removes**), i.e. one of the three files injected into every such prompt was bought almost entirely by question-scaffolding. After, it is off the list entirely and rank 3 is `src/commands_project.rs` (score 5, matched `["project"]`). Rank 1 is `src/tools.rs` (score 9, `["web","search","tool"]`) on both sides. So the honest claim is **not** "the canonical file climbed into the top 3" but "a noise-bought file left it" — the same defect, observed from the other end, on a tree where the canonical file happened to already be safe. **Explicitly out of scope and named rather than smuggled:** `build_signature_block`'s starvation (the 2000-char budget consumed by the first file's 100+ symbols, `break`ing before later files emit anything — the reporter's fourth finding, a different function with a different fix), widening `AUTO_CONTEXT_MAX_FILES`, re-weighting filename matches against bulk symbol matches, and `symbols.rs`, which was not touched.
- `commands_dev.rs` — `/doctor`, `/health`, `/fix` handlers. `run_doctor_checks` builds the structured `DoctorCheck` list, including a **skill context-cost audit** (`skill_context_cost_status`/`skill_bytes_to_tokens`): sums `SKILL.md` bytes across **every directory skills can be loaded from on this run** — the three auto-discovery dirs (`.yoyo/skills/`, `~/.yoyo/skills/`, and `/skill install`'s destination `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/yoyo/skills` or `~/.config/yoyo/skills` — added Day 164, #728) **and** any `--skills <dir>` passed on the command line (`cli::skill_flag_dirs`, recorded at parse time) — then estimates recurring context spend (~bytes/4) and warns over ~8k tokens. Day 151 (blind-spot experiment): the `--skills` half was previously missing, so a repo that loads all of its skills that way (this one loads `./skills`) was audited at ~0 tokens and told "no skills loaded" — a silent under-report dressed as a pass. Enumeration and summing are split into pure/testable halves (`skill_source_dirs` — no I/O; `skill_bytes_in_dirs` — dedups by canonicalized path so a dir named twice, e.g. `--skills .yoyo/skills`, is counted once). Reports cost honestly — does not claim to detect *unused* skills (no usage telemetry in a product context)
- `commands_info.rs` — the read-only status handlers: `/version`, `/status`, `/tokens`, `/cost`, `/model`, `/provider`, `/think`, `/profile`, `/changelog`, `/evolution`, `/tips`. Day 179 (#832): `handle_evolution` is a **thin wrapper** — it injects the two subprocess resolvers (`count_tests_via_cargo`, and `|| fetch_ci_runs(10)`) into the pure-ish `handle_evolution_with`, which holds the whole report and shells nothing; the resolvers are called at the same points in the same order, so `yoyo evolution` output is **byte-identical**.
