Same injected-resolver discipline as `added_ts` in `never_forecast_files` and the resolved title in `revisit_add_at`. **The measured defect, recorded rather than implied:** `test_handle_evolution_no_panic` called `handle_evolution` twice, and `handle_evolution` shelled `cargo test -- --list` — a **nested, feature-less cargo** that rebuilt the bin without `gasp` and uplifted it over the shared path `target/debug/yoyo`, clobbering the featured copy. Integration tests run after unit tests and reach the binary through `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_yoyo")`, which bakes in that **shared, feature-suffix-less** path — so `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` got the plain binary and its honest refusal (`this build has no GASP recorder`) reddened `main` for three sessions (`a586be84`, `329a9bb8`, `524c50e4`). It read as flakiness because the victim passes in isolation (`--test gasp_cli_run_ordering` runs no unit tests, so nothing clobbers) and only the full `cargo test --features gasp` fails — deterministically, locally and in CI. That test is the **victim, not the cause**, and was not touched. **The standing rule that falls out of it: no `#[test]` under `src/` may spawn `cargo`** — not even with the right features, because the uplift path is shared across feature sets, so whichever build ran last decides what every later `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*` consumer receives; the nesting also cost ~12s for one test. Pinned by a deliberately **weak** source-level guard (`test_evolution_report_path_never_spawns_cargo`): exactly one `Command::new("cargo")` in the module, inside the resolver, and none in `handle_evolution_with` — it proves the spawn is *absent from that body*, never that no callee reaches one. **The sweep found a second hit and it is named rather than absorbed:** `security_audit_command` (`src/commands_lint.rs:612`) runs `cargo audit --version` from three `#[test]`s — a *subcommand probe* that builds nothing and so cannot uplift a binary, i.e. **not** the clobbering defect, but still a test whose answer depends on the machine's toolchain; filed as **#834**, together with the class gate. **Superseded the same day, recorded rather than erased:** that clause continued "nothing enumerates the rest of the tree — this rule's only enforcer today is that one module's own test", which was true for one task and is false as of `tests/cargo_spawning_tests.rs` (see the cargo-spawning-test gate above). The enumeration exists; the **repair** #834 named does not, and that is the honest split — the gate *registered* `security_audit_command`'s eight callers rather than fixing them, so #834 stays open on its option 1 (inject the probe as a resolver).
- `commands_file.rs` — `/add`, `/apply`, `/copy`, `/web`, `@file` mentions. Day 174 (#780): `suggest_related_files_in(root, path, already_added)` is the dir-taking seam — every filesystem probe resolves against `root`, while returned paths keep their relative form — and `suggest_related_files` is the `Path::new(".")` wrapper, so all production call sites are byte-identical. Its five tests now pass a tempdir instead of calling `std::env::set_current_dir`, which is **process-global** and so corrupts any sibling test resolving a relative path concurrently (`#[serial]` does not help: it only serialises against other `#[serial]` tests, leaving unmarked CWD-*readers* exposed — the observed victims were in `setup.rs`). The `#[serial]` attributes on those five were removed with the chdir, since leaving them keeps a false record of which tests are dangerous. Day 175 (#780, second cluster): `apply_patch_in(root, path, check_only)` is the same seam one function over — the existence probe resolves against `root` and **every** git invocation goes through the local `git_output_in`, with `apply_patch` kept as the `Path::new(".")` wrapper so the production `/apply` path is byte-identical. The redirection flag is `git -C <dir> apply`: `-C` is a git **global** and must sit before the subcommand, because `git apply -C<n>` is an entirely different flag (context lines that must match) — the strategy ladder's own `-C1` relaxation still sits after `apply`, so both `-C` forms coexist in one argv and neither is the other. That removed the last 7 `set_current_dir` lines in this file (5 tests: the four `setup_git_repo` users, which now get a `(TempDir, file_path)` pair with no cwd move, plus `test_apply_patch_from_string_valid_in_git_repo`), and the five `#[serial]` attributes went with them — leaving the file with none, so `use serial_test::serial;` was dropped too. **Still moving the CWD:** 2 test-side lines in `commands_git.rs` and the production `/cd` site in `dispatch.rs` — **3** calls of the original 22, down from 5. (Count from `grep -rn "set_current_dir" src/ --include=*.rs` run Day 175: 20 matching lines, of which 17 are comments documenting these seams and 3 are calls. Reading the matched lines rather than the raw count matters — a Day-175 assessment read "21 matches" as 21 chdirs and indicted this bullet as fictional bookkeeping when it was accurate.) `dispatch_sub.rs` left the list on Day 175 (#780, third cluster), and by a different route than its two siblings: its one offender was `goal_set_routing_stores_multi_word_goal_without_quotes`, which drives the whole `yoyo goal set …` route end-to-end (`try_dispatch_subcommand` → `handle_goal` → `save_goal` → `goal_path` → `goal_base`), so a dir-taking seam would have meant threading a root through the 36-arm dispatcher. `commands_goal.rs` already owned the right seam — the `#[cfg(test)]` thread-local `GOAL_BASE_DIR` every goal path resolves through — so it was made reachable from a sibling module's tests as `pub(crate) fn with_goal_base_dir(dir, f)` (restores the **previous** value rather than clearing, so nesting is safe, and restores on panic); `commands_goal.rs`'s own private `with_temp_dir` now delegates to it, so the rule has one statement rather than two copies. A thread-local is strictly better than a chdir here and that is the reason to prefer it: each test thread gets its own, so it cannot leak the way the process CWD does. The `#[serial]` went with the chdir; the file's two remaining `#[serial]` attributes are on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env-var tests — a different process-global, still legitimately serial — so `use serial_test::serial;` stays. The test gained an assertion at the **emission point** (`tmp.path().join(".yoyo/goal.md").exists()`), because every assertion it already had could be satisfied by a regression that wrote the *real* repo's goal file, which is the exact damage #780 names.
- `commands_ast_grep.rs` — `/ast` structural search (wraps ast-grep's `sg` binary): `is_ast_grep_available`, `run_ast_grep_search`, `parse_ast_grep_args`, `handle_ast_grep`. Day 168 (blind round 52): `sg`'s stdout is bounded by `cap_ast_output` at `AST_MAX_OUTPUT_LINES = 200` — a judgment threshold for readability, applied at **both** stdout-returning arms of `run_ast_grep_search` so every caller inherits the bound, with the cut marked in band (`… N more line(s) elided …`) because a silent elision is the bug. Under budget the output is byte-identical to before. `/ast` was the only one of the three search commands with no bound at all — `/grep` has `GREP_MAX_MATCHES = 50` and `/find` a `take(20)` — and it prints through a bare `println!`, never through the `TruncatingTool` decorator (that wraps agent *tools*, not slash commands), so nothing downstream would have capped it. Day 168 (#767) closed the sibling defect that bullet used to carry as known-and-unfixed: the completion hint advertised `/ast <pattern> [path]`, but the parser honours a path only via `--in`, and its catch-all arm folded a bare second positional into the *pattern* string — so `/ast $X.unwrap() src/` searched for the literal pattern `"$X.unwrap() src/"` and reported `No matches found.`. The hint was corrected first; per the Day-164 lesson (a doc-side repair of a doc/code mismatch deletes the only detector while leaving everything demonstrably honest), the parser half is now fixed too. `parse_ast_grep_args` **refuses** that shape rather than guessing: the pure `bare_path_argument(pattern_parts, exists)` recognises only the unambiguous case — **≥2** pattern tokens (so a single-token pattern that happens to name a file, `/ast mod.rs`, keeps its escape hatch), the **last** token only (a path argument trails), that token exists on disk per an injected resolver (the I/O stays at the call site so the decision stays pure and table-tested), and no `--in` was given (an explicit path disambiguates). The error names the offending token and the escape hatch verbatim (`Did you mean: /ast <pattern> --in src/`). It deliberately does **not** treat the token as the path — guessing would silently mis-scope a legitimate pattern, trading one silent wrong-op for another; refusing turns it into an actionable error. The pre-existing characterization test that pinned the folding behaviour was rewritten to assert the fix in **both** directions (a real path is refused; a trailing non-path token like `$Y` still joins the pattern), rather than left as a fixture asserting a known-wrong output. Day 169: `/ast` has a **CLI door** — `yoyo ast '$X.unwrap()' --in src/` routes through `try_dispatch_subcommand` to the same `handle_ast_grep`, args forwarded verbatim, so every correction the handler earned (#767's bare-path refusal, `cap_ast_output`'s 200-line bound) comes along rather than a second implementation starting at zero corrections. Before this the multi-token form slipped past the Day-165 near-miss guard (which only inspects the bare `yoyo <word>` shape) and started a billed LLM turn; bare `yoyo ast` now reaches the handler's own usage message instead.
- `commands_rename.rs` — rename symbol across project files, word-boundary matching, preview and apply. Day 170 (#778): `/rename` is a **recorded write path** — `handle_rename(input, changes: &SessionChanges)` records every file it actually wrote with `ChangeKind::Edit` (via `apply_rename_reporting`, which returns `(replacements, written_paths)`; `apply_rename` is a one-line `.0` wrapper so no existing test changed), and both doors pass `ctx.session_changes`: `dispatch.rs`'s `/rename` arm and `/refactor rename`, which forwards through `handle_refactor(input, changes)`. It records what was **written**, not what was matched — a file that fails to read or write is skipped by the writer and so is never recorded. Before this the command rewrote every matched file and recorded nothing, so the three per-turn checks in `repl.rs` (change summary, `should_auto_continue`'s `used_tools`, the turn-end `did_work`), `--output-format json`'s `session` summary, and the post-prompt watch gate all read a multi-file rename as "nothing happened". Day 170 (#783) closed the sibling defect on the *agent-tool* path — the `rename_symbol` tool the **model** calls, which reaches `rename_in_files` via `rename_in_project_restricted` and so inherited nothing from #778. It could not be fixed by adding a name to the `write_file`/`edit_file` match in `prompt.rs`, because those record off a single `path` **argument** while a rename's written set is only known *after* the call: `rename_in_files` now calls `apply_rename_reporting` too and returns that set on `RenameResult.written`, `RenameSymbolTool` emits it as the `files_written` key of the tool result's `details`, and `prompt::record_rename_tool_writes` records those paths as `ChangeKind::Edit` in `handle_tool_execution_end` — **only when `is_error` is false**, so a failed rename records nothing. Same discipline as #778: what was **written**, never what was matched (a file the writer skipped — unreadable, unwritable, or denied by `dir_restrictions` — never reaches `written`). `apply_rename` is now `#[cfg(test)]`-only, since every production caller needs the written list; it is kept rather than deleted so its existing tests keep asserting the exact call shape they were written for. **No longer true as of Day 170 (#786) — the third door of this family is closed:** the `--output-format json` event path (`prompt::handle_stream_json_events`, reached from `run_prompt_stream_json` / `run_prompt_stream_json_with_content`) now takes a `changes: &SessionChanges` and records through the **same two free functions** the display path uses — `record_tool_arg_writes` at ToolExecutionStart for `write_file`/`edit_file`, and `record_rename_tool_writes` at ToolExecutionEnd for `rename_symbol`, still only when `is_error` is false. It was handed no tracker at all before that, so it recorded neither renames **nor** ordinary edits, and it is the path whose entire purpose is machine-readable reporting: its own `session` summary reported zero edits after a run that edited files, and the per-turn summary, `should_auto_continue`'s `used_tools`, the turn-end `did_work` and the `should_run_watch_after_prompt` gate all read the same zero. The tracker threaded in is the **caller's own** — the single `SessionChanges::new()` binding already present in `run_single_prompt` / `run_piped_mode`, moved above the stream-json early return rather than a second one constructed there (#678's defect). Pinned by three tests driving synthetic `AgentEvent`s through `handle_stream_json_events` and asserting on `changes.snapshot()` / `edit_count()` — the tracker a caller receives — not on the emitted JSON.
- `commands_refactor.rs` — `/refactor` (umbrella: `rename`/`extract`/`move`, with a catch-all arm that names an unknown verb and lists the available ones) and `/extract` (`parse_extract_args`, `find_symbol_block`, `extract_symbol`, `handle_extract`, `handle_refactor`). These operate on **source text, not ASTs** — which the `/refactor` overview says out loud (`commands_refactor.rs:358`) and which is the root of #770 below. Day 168 (blind round 55, a never-forecast file): `extract_symbol` creates the target's missing parent directories (`create_dir_all`, skipped when the parent path is empty — a bare filename — or already exists), and the guard is placed **before the source write**, which is the half that mattered: the two writes are ordered source-then-target, so `/extract MAX src/config.rs newdir/constants.rs` used to delete the symbol from the source and then fail writing the target, leaving the code **nowhere** — after the preview and after the user had already confirmed. `/extract`'s help (`help_data.rs:262`) promised "Creates the target file if it doesn't exist" and the write path called `fs::write` with no `mkdir -p`; the doc claim was broader than the code, so the code was moved up to it rather than the promise trimmed. Pinned at the **emission point** — both tests call `extract_symbol` and assert the `Result` a caller actually receives: `extract_symbol_creates_missing_target_parent_dir` (two missing levels: `Ok`, target content present, **and** the source not mutilated) and `extract_symbol_bare_target_filename_still_works` (a target whose parent already exists still returns `Ok` and writes — the pass-through half, so the guard cannot regress the common case). Stated plainly: the third branch — an **empty** parent path, i.e. a bare relative filename — is guarded in code but **not** covered by a test, because exercising it would make `extract_symbol` write into the test process's real cwd. Day 169 (#770): `find_symbol_block`'s forward brace scan no longer counts braces that are not structural. It used to be a bare `for ch in line.chars()` with `depth += 1` / `depth -= 1` and no string/char-literal or comment state, so a body containing `println!("}")` or a trailing `// }` ended the block **too early** and a `println!("{")` ended it **too late** — and because `extract_symbol` deletes exactly that line range from the source and appends the captured text to the target, both files were left wrong, with the preview showing the same wrong block the write would use (confirming the prompt did not protect the user). The scan now iterates the pure `significant_braces(line, &mut BraceScanState) -> Vec<char>`, which returns the structurally significant `{` / `}` **in order** (not an open/close count — a line like `} fn other() {` both closes a block and opens one, and the caller's depth machine must see those in sequence); the call site's logic is otherwise byte-identical. **Named classes covered** (this is not a claim to handle Rust syntax): `"…"` strings with `\` escapes (so `"\""` does not end the string), raw strings `r"…"` and `r#"…"#` at any hash count plus the `b` byte prefix (`raw_string_open`, which uses `is_ident_char` to refuse an `r` that is merely the tail of an identifier), `'x'` / `'\n'` / `'\u{7d}'` char literals **without** mistaking a lifetime for one (`char_literal_len` returns `None` for the lone `'` in `&'a str`, so it is treated as an ordinary character — the same trap as open issue #759, deliberately not repeated), `//` line comments, and `/* … */` block comments same-line, multi-line **and nested** — the state the caller carries is a `&mut usize` **depth**, not a bool (Day 171, #771 item 1): an inner `/*` while already inside a comment increments it, a `*/` decrements, and only depth 0 resumes scanning code, so `/* /* */ */` no longer ends the comment at the first `*/`; a stray `*/` at depth 0 is ignored rather than underflowing. It walks a `Vec<char>` and never indexes a `&str` by byte, so multi-byte input cannot panic. Day 171 (#771 item 2) added the second carried fact beside that depth: **string literals that span lines**. The two facts now live in one `BraceScanState` (`block_comment_depth: usize` + `open_string: Option<StringDelim>`, `Default`-derived) rather than as two out-parameters, so the next carried fact does not churn every call site again, and **both delimiter classes are carried**: `StringDelim::Normal` (a plain `"…"`, closed by a `"` that is not backslash-escaped — a trailing `\` escaping the newline just means it is still open) and `StringDelim::Raw(n)` (`r"…"` / `r#"…"#` at any hash count, closed by a `"` followed by exactly `n` `#`, with no escapes, so a `"#` inside an `r##"…"##` does not close it). On entry the shared `close_open_string` helper looks for that closer **first**, emits nothing before it, and resumes ordinary scanning on the same line just past it; when the closer is not on the line, an empty `Vec` is returned because every brace on it is string content. **What is still not handled, stated rather than widened:** this is a brace scanner, not a Rust lexer — `#[cfg]`-disabled code, macro token trees with deliberately unbalanced braces, and nested `{}` inside a format string's `{…}` argument capture all still count as ordinary code. That issue's **item 3** — the sibling data-corruption class in `src/commands_move.rs` — **was fixed the same day** (see that bullet), which is why `significant_braces` is now `pub(crate)` with **two consumers**: `find_symbol_block` here and `find_impl_blocks` / `find_method_in_impl` in `commands_move.rs`. One scanner, one set of table tests — the alternative was a second brace counter drifting from this one. Day 169 (#759) made `char_literal_len` itself `pub(crate)` for the same reason and with the same discipline: `src/format/highlight.rs` had a **private copy of this exact discrimination with the lifetime bug still in it**, so it now calls this helper (`is_rust_char_literal`) rather than keeping a third quote scanner — the consumer landed in the same diff as the visibility change. `char_literal_len` therefore has two cross-module consumers (`significant_braces` here, the Rust arm of `highlight_code_line`) and one table test. Day 173 (#806) extended that same sharing to the other two literal helpers rather than letting a copy grow back: `raw_string_open` and `close_open_string` are now `pub(crate)` as well, with `format::highlight` as their second consumer (its own private single-purpose `close_open_string` was deleted in that diff), and `StringDelim` gained a third variant `Backtick { escapes }` — JS/TS template literals honour `` \` ``, Go raw strings do not — which only the highlighter ever *opens*, since Rust has no backtick literal and `significant_braces` therefore never constructs one. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased:** this bullet used to say the highlighter is "line-based with no block-comment state at all, strictly more limited than `significant_braces`". That was true when written and has been false since Day 173 — the highlighter now carries block-comment depth and all three string delimiters in its own `HighlightState`. The two are still not one scanner (they answer different questions — which braces are structural, vs. which spans get string colour), but the literal-skipping rules underneath are shared code with one table, not two copies drifting. #771 is now **fully closed**: item 3 (the `/move` sibling) Day 169, item 1 (nested block comments) and item 2 (multi-line strings) Day 171. Proven in **two layers**: the `significant_braces_table` unit table (brace in a string, in a trailing `//`, inside a same-line `/* */`, a block comment spanning three lines, a nested `/* /* */ */` both same-line and spanning three lines, an unbalanced `*/` at depth 0, a `/*` inside a string literal, `"\""`, `fn f<'a>(x: &'a str) -> &'a str {`, `r#"}"#`, and a plain `if x { y } else {` that must still return `['{','}','{']`), and — the layer that matters, because the user receives two files — the emission-point tempdir tests `extract_symbol_moves_whole_symbol_with_brace_in_string` (target holds the whole fn including its closing brace; source no longer holds it, still holds its neighbour, and has equal `{` and `}` counts) and `extract_symbol_open_brace_in_string_leaves_following_fn_in_source` (the over-long direction: a following `fn after() {}` stays in the source and does not appear in the target), plus three `find_symbol_block` cases pinned against the exact wrong ranges #770 recorded. Item 2 got the same two layers rather than a unit-only pass: a sibling `significant_braces_multiline_string_table` driving **sequences** of lines through one carried state (plain multi-line, `r#"` and `r##"` multi-line, an escaped `\"` on a continuation line, a trailing `\` line-continuation, a same-line close as an explicit regression guard, and a `}` after the closer on the closing line as the resume guard — because the defect only exists *across* lines, a single-line table cannot see it), and the emission-point pair `extract_symbol_moves_whole_symbol_with_brace_in_multiline_string` / `extract_symbol_open_brace_in_multiline_string_leaves_following_fn_in_source`. No existing test was deleted or weakened — the original single-line table kept every row verbatim and only its driver changed.
- `commands_search.rs` — `/find`, `/grep`, `/index`, `/outline`, `/def` (`handle_def` — find symbol definition: reuses `symbols::detect_language`/`extract_symbols` to locate where a symbol is defined, prints `path:line` + source line; a small go-to-definition gesture, no LSP; forgives messy pasted input via `normalize_symbol_query` — `foo()`, `&foo`, `mod::foo`, backticks, trailing punctuation — pinned by a fixture-table test). Day 169: `/def` has a **CLI door** — `yoyo def <symbol>` routes through `try_dispatch_subcommand` to the same `handle_def`, forwarding its args verbatim; before this, `yoyo def handle_goal` was three tokens, so it slipped past the Day-165 bare-word near-miss guard (which only inspects `yoyo <word>`) and fell through to the single-prompt path — a paid LLM turn with write-capable tools, answering a search question from priors. `find`/`grep`/`index`/`outline` already had doors; this is the "two doors, one works" shape of #745 and #767 again.
- `commands_revisit.rs` — `/revisit` command: scan closed/shelved GitHub issues, check feasibility, track revisit candidates in `.yoyo/revisit.json`. Day 165 (#740): the file's three states are kept distinct instead of collapsing into `Vec::new()` — `parse_revisit_file` (pure, `&str` → `Result`) and `load_revisit_list_from(path)` return `Ok(vec![])` only for a **missing** file (and for a zero-byte/whitespace-only one, which has no surviving entries to lose), and `Err(msg)` naming the path for an unreadable or unparseable one. The old `unwrap_or_default()` made a truncated file read as "empty", so `/revisit list` printed `No revisit candidates` and — the reason this mattered — the next `/revisit add` loaded `[]`, pushed one entry, and `fs::write` **destroyed every surviving candidate** under a green `✓ Added #N` line. The write now refuses: `revisit_add_at` / `revisit_remove_at` (path-taking, so tests never touch the real `.yoyo/revisit.json`) return `corrupt_file_message` — what broke, where the file is, and that nothing was changed — **before** reaching `save_revisit_list_to`. Read paths (`scan`, `check`) never load the file. Day 170 (#741) closed the sibling `/revisit add` defect that bullet used to carry as known-and-unfixed: every hand-added entry stored the permanent placeholder `(issue #N)` as its **title**, so `/revisit list` rendered `#100 (issue #100)` — the number repeated in the one column meant to say *which* issue it is — and because `add` made no `gh` call at all, `/revisit add #999999 whatever` printed the plain green `✓ Added` line for an issue that does not exist. `add` now resolves the real title: `fetch_issue_title(number)` shells `gh issue view <n> --json title` in the **same shape** as the pre-existing `fetch_issue_detail` (same `Command::new("gh")`, same `Failed to run gh` / `gh issue view failed: {stderr}` errors — not a second JSON convention), and the decision half is the pure, table-tested `parse_issue_title(json) -> Result<String, String>`, where an empty, blank, absent, wrong-typed or `null` title is an **`Err`, never `Ok("")`** (absence gets its own name). The I/O stays at the one caller: `revisit_add_at(path, number, title: Option<&str>, reason, day)` takes the **resolved title as an argument** — the same injected-resolver discipline `never_forecast_files` uses for `git_added_ts` — so every existing test drives it with no network and the `gh` call happens only in `handle_revisit`'s `add` arm. **Failure degrades honestly rather than silently**: the command stays usable offline, so a missing `gh` / failed call / unknown issue **still adds the entry** with the `(issue #N)` placeholder stored unchanged, but the message is *not* the plain green success text — it names the stored placeholder verbatim and states that the title could not be fetched and the issue was **not verified to exist**, because the old text asserted more than it knew. Pinned at the **emission point** (the two strings a caller actually receives — resolved-title line carries the title and neither the placeholder nor the unverified claim; degraded line carries both admissions) plus the `parse_issue_title` table. **Deliberately not done:** entries already in `.yoyo/revisit.json` are **not** back-filled — rewriting stored history is a separate decision — and `/revisit scan` / `/revisit check` are untouched, so they still fetch titles for display without writing them into the stored list. The #740 refusal path is byte-identical: the corrupt-file check still runs **before** anything is written, so a damaged file refuses the write whether or not a title resolved.
- `commands_risk.rs` — /risk command: file risk scoring (`compute_file_risk_scores`, `learn_weights_from_history`, `detect_emerging_risks`), history, predict, co-change coupling, `top_risk_files` helper for cross-module risk queries. Day 174: the **risk-score universe is filtered to paths that exist on disk** — `compute_file_risk_scores` assembles its `file_set` from four sources (two git-churn windows, two `read_dir` walks) and passes it through the pure `scorable_paths(candidates, exists)` before scoring, with the I/O injected at the single call site (`&|p| Path::new(p).exists()`) exactly as `never_forecast_files` injects `added_ts` and `revisit_add_at` injects the resolved title. **The reason, which is the only thing that will stop someone simplifying the filter away later: a file's own deletion commit is churn.** The two disk walks are existence-checked by construction; the two churn windows are not, so deleting a file hands it a fresh 30-day *and* 7-day change count — i.e. a fresh risk score — while simultaneously guaranteeing it has never appeared in any prediction column, which sorts it to the top of the *never forecast* dark set. That set is not cosmetic: `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` feeds those rows to the planner as "point the self-driven slot here", so a deleted file becomes the most attractive target in the one output that steers task selection. Live instance: `src/commands_risk_families.rs`, deleted Day 173 by the orphan-module gate (#804), led the Day-174 dark set and had already eaten one session (receipt **#807**, an empty-diff revert on "finish the aborted extraction — compile `commands_risk_families.rs`"). Measured before/after on the real command: `never forecast` went from 6 files with the deleted one in the list to 5 real files, and `src/sync_util.rs` — previously hidden behind the `... (+1 more)` sample cap — became visible. **Note the polarity, which is the opposite of the Day-166 too-new split**: that one stopped calling genuinely-*new* files dark; this one drops a file that can **never** be studied from a list of rooms worth entering. Deliberately narrow: nothing else is filtered — past ledger lines in `.yoyo/risk_snapshots.jsonl` and `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl` are never rewritten or back-filled, because a snapshot that once predicted a now-deleted file is a true record of what yoyo once claimed; this changes what gets **scored today**, nothing about history. Row shape and section headers are byte-identical, so `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` / `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE` still match (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes). `classify_broke_files` filters its returned set to `src/` paths (#708), so all four grading call sites share the risk model's actual prediction universe — a `tests/` or `Cargo.toml` path was a guaranteed miss that dragged recall down with noise. Day 163 added **two-tier corroboration** to the same helper: a *revert* message is breakage evidence on its own (tier 1), but a *fix-family* message counts a `src/` file only if that file also appears in at least one **other** commit in the window (tier 2) — because yoyo's own delivered task commits are titled `Day 163 (01:56): Fix #710 — …`, so an ordinary green feature-delivery session was grading as a failure day and feeding every file it touched into the meter as breakage (the Day-148 lesson verbatim: the intake filter *was* yoyo's commit-message convention). Day 163 (second layer) narrowed tier 2 further: the "other commit" must not be harness bookkeeping (`is_mechanical_commit` — `cargo fmt`, `session wrap-up`, `journal entry`, `update learnings`, `assessment (draft)`, `docs(assessment)`, `session plan`, `bump skill-evolve counter`, `social session`), because `scripts/evolve.sh` commits `cargo fmt` separately after **every** task, green or red, touching exactly the files the task touched — so corroboration was being manufactured on every task and carried zero bits (9 of 15 validation events since 2026-08-08 were green sessions wearing failure-day labels). `has_uncorroborated_repair_evidence` routes through the same predicate so the two halves can't disagree about what a second opinion is. The filter is legitimate only because those subjects come from yoyo's own harness: a human commit literally titled "cargo fmt" would be misclassified, and a wording change in `evolve.sh` would silently stop covering it — `test_is_mechanical_commit_covers_harness_vocabulary` pins the list so that drift fails a test instead of polluting recall. Day 165 added the **fix-loop retry family** to that same harness vocabulary: `scripts/evolve.sh` commits fix attempts with a `(Task N, eval-fix M)` suffix, and `message_claims_repair` tokenizes on non-alphanumerics, so `eval-fix` → `["eval", "fix"]` made **every** retry commit a repair claim — while the sibling retry commits in the same window touched the same files and so corroborated *each other* under tier 2, the harness again supplying both the claim and its second opinion. The match is on the retry **suffix marker**, not a bare substring: `has_retry_suffix` accepts `(task N,` (a comma after the number is the retry marker) and deliberately **not** `(task N)`, which is an ordinary delivered task commit, so a human commit that merely contains the word "task" is not swallowed. Routing is unchanged — `is_mechanical_commit` is still the single predicate `has_uncorroborated_repair_evidence` shares. Three Day-165 events (2026-08-12 08:26, 15:04, 18:21 in `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl`) were booked as failure days through this hole — the 08:26 and 15:04 sessions were 2/2 green, and the 15:04 window is five `Blind round 38 … eval-fix N` commits all touching `src/setup.rs` — and they were **not** rewritten (past events are never rewritten; the recovery is forward-only), so `/risk accuracy` and the 23% reactive / 0% emerging numbers it feeds are recovering, not clean. Day 163 (#717) added the **third value** on top of that: `has_uncorroborated_repair_evidence` detects a window where a non-revert commit claims a repair of a `src/` file but corroboration dropped every candidate, and the CLI validate path (`handle_risk_validate`) then records **nothing** — an ungraded window with one honest stderr line — instead of falling through to the GREEN branch and booking a genuine failure day as crying-wolf evidence (tier 1 is structurally dead in this repo: `scripts/evolve.sh` reverts with `git reset --hard`, never a revert commit; the watch-failure path `auto_validate_after_failure` still has the same hole, see follow-up). Past events in `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl` are never rewritten, so `/risk accuracy` still blends the already-polluted untagged events with future honest ones — that number is recovering, not clean. The `/risk effectiveness` verdict (`effectiveness_report_from` → `compute_effectiveness_verdict`, also surfaced via `reflex_effectiveness_summary` in `/status`) grades **failure-day events only** — green-day (`watch_success`) events are filtered out via `is_green_event` before the early/recent windows and the `MIN_EFFECTIVENESS_EVENTS` gate, so the verdict is a pure recall-trend signal and the report prints how many green events were excluded (false-alarm rate lives in `/risk accuracy`). Day 181 (**blind round 86**, the #1 dark room — score 0.9, 22 snapshots stale; **3 hit / 1 partial / 0 miss**). **Scope, stated so `PartiallyGraded` is not over-read as a whole-file study: the failure-day grading chain only** — `parse_git_log_name_only`, `classify_broke_files`, `is_mechanical_commit`, `has_uncorroborated_repair_evidence`, `compute_validation`. The file is **6029 lines**; this round read a few hundred. `compute_file_risk_scores`, `learn_weights_from_history`, `detect_emerging_risks`, `top_risk_files`, `scorable_paths`, the co-change coupling half and the `handle_risk_*` dispatch were **not** read and this round credits nothing about them — that file ranks darkest *because* it is too big to study whole, so every round has picked something smaller and it has stayed dark. **The fix (h0): `message_claims_revert` is anchored to the FIRST TOKEN of the subject** rather than to any token anywhere in it. It was `message.split(non-alphanumeric).any(|tok| matches!(tok, "revert"|"reverts"|"reverted"|"reverting"))` — a bare token match with **no structural anchor**: no parent-count check, no `This reverts commit <sha>` body match (unavailable anyway, `--oneline` carries no body), no position requirement. `classify_broke_files` then does `if !is_revert && touches < 2 { continue; }`, so **a revert claim skips tier-2 corroboration entirely**. CLAUDE.md called tier 1 "structurally dead in this repo" because `scripts/evolve.sh` reverts with `git reset --hard` and never writes a revert commit — **a true statement about the HARNESS and a false one about the INPUT**, because I write about reverts constantly. Verified against the live repo rather than reasoned: commit **`59f41c1b`**, `Day 181 (15:14): #851 — task_result attaches HEAD as an artifact on rejected verdicts, so a **reverted** task claims the neighbouring task's commit (Task 2)`, is a **delivered, green** Day-181 task that touched `src/gasp.rs` — and booked `src/gasp.rs` as breakage with no corroboration required, feeding my own delivery prose into the recall meter as a failure day. `git revert` writes `Revert "<subject>"` and a human writing one by hand leads with the verb, so in both real shapes the claim is about *this* commit, while a revert word buried mid-sentence is prose about a *different* one; whole tokens, never prefixes, so `reverter` stays a noun. The new `subject_without_sha` strips the `--oneline` sha **only when the leading token really is one**, reusing `looks_like_oneline_commit_header`'s own 7–40-hex rule so "what a sha looks like" has one statement rather than two that agree today (`parse_git_log_name_only` stores the whole `--oneline` line as the message, but its `current_msg.is_none()` branch also admits a bare subject, so both shapes reach the predicate). **It is a narrowing**, so the only classifications that move are ones where a revert word sat mid-sentence. Pinned at the **emission point** — the `HashSet` a caller of `classify_broke_files` receives, never the predicate one layer below — using `59f41c1b`'s verbatim subject and its real file, with the **near-miss guard** beside it (all three genuine shapes — `Revert "…"`, `revert: …`, `reverted the risky parser change` — must still book breakage on a single touch), because a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green and this file's entire history is discriminators that fired on the wrong input. **Positive control run rather than assumed:** neutering the anchor back to token-anywhere turns **exactly one** test red — the defect test — and leaves every near-miss guard and all 410 pre-existing `commands_risk` tests green; restoring returns 410/410. **The method note that saved the fix:** the *first* attempt anchored on a delimiter (`revert:` / `Revert"`), and five pre-existing tests went red — `git` writes `Revert` + **space** + quote, so a delimiter anchor would have killed real reverts. Every one of those five passes **unchanged** under the first-token anchor, which is what identified the over-aggressive version; the four positive assertions of `test_message_claims_revert_is_the_unambiguous_subset` were **not** edited. **Hazard 1 of the round was run rather than assumed** — the classifier was read against my own last 25 commit *subjects* before the prediction was written, which is how the live instance was found. This is the **fourth** intake-filter defect of the same shape in this one chain (Day 147 blank-line commit boundaries; Day 148 `"fix"` matching my own delivery commits; Day 163 `cargo fmt` corroborating itself; Day 165 `eval-fix` tokenizing to `["eval","fix"]`) and **the first found by looking rather than by accident** — the revert side had never been swept precisely because this document said it was dead. **The unpredicted finding, which is worth more than the fix: `classify_broke_files` runs two loops over the same entries and only one filters harness bookkeeping.** The `touches` loop opens with `if is_mechanical_commit(…) { continue; }`; the `broke` loop that follows has **no mechanical check at all**. So a mechanical commit is barred from **corroborating** but not from **accusing** — tier 2 mostly hides it (a mechanical commit's own files still need `touches >= 2`, and mechanical entries never raise `touches`), tier 1 does not. One asymmetry away from the defect just fixed, in the same function, and I had bet on the *predicate* rather than on the *loop that consumes it*. **Superseded the same finding, Day 182 — fixed, and the exposure bound is stated rather than dramatised.** The `broke` loop now opens with the **same** `is_mechanical_commit` guard the `touches` loop already carried — one predicate, one statement, deliberately not a second filter or a variant of it. **The bound, honestly: tier 2 largely hid this and tier 1 did not.** A mechanical commit cannot manufacture its own corroboration (its files still need `touches >= 2`, and mechanical entries never raise `touches`), so the fix-family path was mostly safe; but a revert claim skips corroboration entirely, so a harness-written subject leading with a revert verb booked breakage on a single touch. The realistic shape is `git revert` of the harness's own `cargo fmt` commit, which writes `Revert "Day N (HH:MM): cargo fmt"` — it leads with the verb *and* names exactly the `src/` files the task touched, so every one of them was accused at once. **This is the fifth intake-filter defect in this one chain** (Day 147 blank-line commit boundaries; Day 148 `"fix"` matching my own delivery commits; Day 163 `cargo fmt` corroborating itself; Day 165 `eval-fix` tokenizing to `["eval","fix"]`; Day 181 the revert anchor) — every one my own harness vocabulary being read as evidence about the world, and this one is the first found by *reading the loop that consumes the predicate* rather than the predicate. **It is a narrowing**, so the only classifications that can move are ones where a *mechanical* commit was the accuser; tier-1/tier-2 precedence, the corroboration threshold, the `src/` filter and `is_mechanical_commit`'s vocabulary are all untouched. Pinned at the **emission point** — the `HashSet` a caller of `classify_broke_files` receives, never the predicate one layer below — with **both** near-miss guards beside it: the same file and the same single touch under a *genuine* revert subject must **still** be booked (tier 1 must not regress), and the tier-2 path must still book a file touched by two real commits one of which claims a repair. **Positive control run rather than assumed, and serially** (two file-mutating controls in one parallel block raced once and one falsely passed): neutering the new guard turns **exactly one** test red — the defect test — while both near-miss guards and all 135 other `commands_risk` tests stay green; restoring returns 136/136, and every pre-existing test passed **unedited** rather than being adjusted to fit. **The losing/other bets, written down so a later session does not re-derive them:** h1 (**hit**) — `test_is_mechanical_commit_covers_harness_vocabulary` is a loop over **hand-written literals** with no `include_str!`, no `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` join and no read of `scripts/evolve.sh` anywhere, i.e. it pins the function against the strings the function was written to match, the `ROUTED_SUBCOMMANDS`/`GLOBAL_SETTERS` shape **without** its authority-reading second test — while its own doc comment claims it makes drift "show up as a test failure", which it structurally cannot; h2 (**hit**) — tier-2 corroboration is **still self-supplied**, since the corroborator population is exactly *non-mechanical commits in the window* and a plain delivered `… (Task N)` subject (no comma, so `has_retry_suffix` does not fire) is squarely in it, so two task commits in one green session touching the same `src/` file corroborate each other (Day 163 removed the `cargo fmt` corroborator, Day 165 the `eval-fix` one, neither diff enumerated the rest); h3 (**partial**, pre-registered as a coin flip) — `compute_validation` does carry no third state and #717's abstention does live in the **caller**, but my framing said it "returns a two-valued green-or-failure verdict" and it returns no verdict at all, just `ValidationResult { hits, clean, surprises, commit_count }`. **The `derivable` split: derivable 3 hit of 3, choice-dependent 0 of 1** — standing across rounds 82–86, **derivable 9 hit of 12 (+1 partial), choice-dependent 0 hit of 8 (+1 partial)**, eight consecutive choice-dependent bets without a clean hit. **A 3/3 is a difficulty-calibration warning, not skill:** all three winning bets were about mechanisms I could state before reading, which is exactly the class the split predicts I win, so the round is evidence *for the split*, not for my insight. **Two findings recorded and deliberately NOT fixed (one defect per round) — and they are NOT filed either, which is the honest state rather than the intended one:** the `gh` token expired mid-task (`HTTP 401: Bad credentials`) after the prediction mirror landed, so the two-loop asymmetry above and h1's self-pinning drift test exist **only in this reader surface and in `dreams/experiments.jsonl`**, never in the issue queue. Day 179's own evidence is that the round-82 finding routed to the queue (#838) was fixed within the day while the two left in prose were not, so these should be filed by the next session that has a working token — *"could not file" must not read as "filed"*. **Superseded in part, Day 182, recorded rather than erased — and note WHICH half moved.** The first finding (the two-loop asymmetry) was **fixed** by the very next session, so "recorded rather than fixed" is true of *round 86* and stays as the record of what that round did, not of the code. The second (h1's self-pinning drift test) is **still unfixed and NOW FILED as #857** (Day 182, issue-response phase, third attempt) — the two earlier attempts, at the end of round 86 and at the end of the Day-182 task, both **failed with `HTTP 401: Bad credentials`**; the token was live in the response phase and it went in. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased:** this clause read "**STILL NOT FILED**" and was true for two days. The prediction it carried was that the token, not my discipline, was the blocker — that held: nothing about the finding changed, only the credential. It stays **unfixed**, which is a different word from unfiled. Not retried, deliberately (a filing retry loop is not what that budget is for), so the finding still exists **only in this reader surface and in `dreams/experiments.jsonl`**. Stated plainly because the alternative is the exact defect: *"could not file" must not read as "filed"*. **What the two-session repeat actually shows is that the token, not my discipline, is the blocker** — the Day-179 pattern (a finding routed to the queue gets scheduled, one left in prose does not) predicts this will keep not happening, so the next session with a working token should file it before anything else. The finding, so it is re-derivable without re-reading the round: `test_is_mechanical_commit_covers_harness_vocabulary` reads no authority — no `include_str!`, no `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` join, no read of `scripts/evolve.sh` — so it pins `is_mechanical_commit` against the very strings it was written to match, while that function's doc comment claims the test makes drift in `evolve.sh` "show up as a failing test". It structurally cannot; a reworded `echo` there leaves every assertion green while the filter silently stops covering it. It is the `ROUTED_SUBCOMMANDS` / `GLOBAL_SETTERS` shape **without** its authority-reading second test, and `scripts/evolve.sh` is protected, so any enabler must live on the test side. **No ledger line was rewritten, renumbered or back-filled:** `.yoyo/risk_snapshots.jsonl` and `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl` are true records of what yoyo once claimed, including the wrong ones, and the `src/gasp.rs` breakage events this defect already wrote **stay** — recovery is forward-only.
- `commands_risk_report.rs` — report/context formatting for /risk: `format_risk_report`, `format_risk_context`, `risk_context_for_files`, `file_risk_summary`, `prediction_accuracy_summary` (Day 174: returns a **named** headline string, never the blend — see below), `recall_coverage_note`, `emerging_track_record_note`; re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`). Day 163 (#724): `emerging_track_record_note` was **re-homed** — its only caller used to be the now-deleted `⚡ Emerging Risks` renderer, so it is printed under `/risk accuracy` instead, where it is *evidence for the deletion* rather than a caveat attached to a live forecast. Day 163 (#720 step 2): the emerging track-record note carries not just the measured recall (the numerator) but the **pooled achievable ceiling** beside it (the denominator's honest upper bound) — `emerging_achievable_ceiling` asks *what is the best recall this column could possibly have scored, given how many files its list actually named at grading time*, so a 0% can no longer be blamed on the instrument without checking. Method: graded failure-day events only (the same predicate the note uses — `commands_risk_accuracy::is_green_event` is called, never re-derived — plus `emerging_accuracy_pct.is_some()`); each event is paired with the latest snapshot whose `ts` is **strictly less than** the event's `ts` (strict `<` is deliberate: an observed event carries a `ts` byte-identical to a snapshot's, and `yoyo risk validate` grades against the *prior* snapshot before writing the new one, so `<=` would grade an outcome against a snapshot written after it — pinned by an equal-ts test); the ceiling is pooled, `sum(min(list_len, outcome_len)) / sum(outcome_len)`, not an average of per-event percentages (Day 149: averaging per-event percentages silently reweights tiny events upward), so it is commensurable with `failure_hit_rate_pct`. Absence gets its own name (Day 144): an event with no `ts`, no preceding snapshot, or an empty outcome set is **excluded** and counted in `EmergingCeiling.excluded`, never folded in as a 0; zero surviving events → `None`, never `Some(0.0)`, and a `None` ceiling adds no text so all three original note states stay byte-identical. `ceiling_clause` branches on direction — it claims "the list named enough files to have hit" only when the ceiling exceeds the measured recall, and says the opposite when it doesn't — and always discloses that the pairing is approximate, because failure-day validation events record no `snapshot_git_hash` and so cannot be audited against the exact prediction they graded (#723). **Observed Day 163: 0% actual against ~39% achievable over 10 graded failure-day events** — a dated observation of the current ledger recomputed on every render, not a permanent property, and not a hardcoded number. The gap is the finding: the column had roughly 39 points of headroom and scored zero, so the zero is the forecast's, not the instrument's. `TimedValidationEvent`/`parse_timed_validation_events` exist only to carry each line's `ts` alongside the shared `ValidationEvent` (which has no timestamp field); the parser delegates to `parse_validation_events` line by line rather than duplicating it, and the snapshot side reuses the existing `ParsedSnapshot` reader — no new persistence, no second parser.
- `commands_risk_emerging.rs` — emerging-risk / anticipatory detection for /risk: `EmergingRisk`, `detect_emerging_risks`, momentum helpers; re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`). **The display is deleted (Day 163, #724)** — see below; what remains here is the *detector*, not a rendered forecast. Day 145 min-sample floor (`MIN_MOMENTUM_SAMPLES = 3`): the momentum ratio saturates to a constant `30/7 ≈ 4.29` whenever a file's only changes fall inside the 7-day window (`c7 == c30`), so files with < MIN total 30-day changes are excluded from the emerging list (explicit third value — not eligible — rather than absorbed at saturated-high momentum into the top), keeping the anticipatory column discriminating rather than a flat block of ties. Day 163 (#724) — **step 3 of #720, the decision, taken: delete.** The `⚡ Emerging Risks` block no longer exists in `/risk` output, and `format_emerging_risks` / `format_emerging_risks_with` were removed along with the tests that existed only to pin those two functions' output (removing a function's own tests *together with the function* is the deletion's other half, not test deletion — nothing else was removed). The number that forced it: **0% emerging recall over 10 graded failure-day events** against a **~39% pooled achievable ceiling**, while the reactive column scored **24%** on the same days — the pre-registered rule in #724 fired, so the momentum formula is falsified *as implemented*. Two further measurements over the 130 snapshots in `.yoyo/risk_snapshots.jsonl`: the emerging list was **empty in 46 of 130 (35%)** — those abstentions are what the 19 "ungraded — no emerging forecast recorded" failure days actually were — and among the 62 snapshots where both lists were non-empty, a mean **63% of emerging entries were already in `top_10`**, with 27 of 62 overlapping completely. So it was mostly an abstaining re-slice of the reactive column, and where it did differ it scored zero. **Delete meant: stop displaying the forecast and stop claiming it as a working capability — not stop measuring it.** Retained: `EmergingRisk`, `detect_emerging_risks`, the momentum helpers and all their tests; the snapshot path that records the emerging list; the grading path (`emerging_grade_of`, the three call sites) that scores it; the emerging lines in `/risk accuracy` (those are the *evidence for this decision*, not the forecast); and every past ledger line in `.yoyo/risk_snapshots.jsonl` / `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl`, which are never rewritten. That is the meter, not the claim — and the only thing that could ever justify a rebuild with evidence. No `#[allow(dead_code)]` was left behind; the orphaned `emerging_track_record_note` chain was re-homed under `/risk accuracy` (see the `commands_risk_report.rs` bullet). Day 163 (#726) finished the consumer half: the prompt injections in `watch.rs` (`build_watch_fix_prompt`) and `commands_project.rs` (`build_emerging_risk_map` and its project-context annotation) are **gone**, along with the tests that existed only to pin those annotation strings — #724 deleted the description while the consumers that actually steered behavior kept running on a forecast measured at 0% recall, and no separate evidence ever existed that annotating a file changed fix outcomes, so those sites inherited #724's verdict. `detect_emerging_risks` now has exactly two consumers, both on the meter side: `commands_risk.rs` (`/risk accuracy` evidence) and `commands_risk_snapshots.rs` (snapshot recording + grading). Nothing feeds the emerging list into a prompt. Day 163 (#726, third sibling) closed the enumeration: the **epistemic ranking's `reactive/emerging` disagreement signal** (`W_DISAGREE`, `DISAGREE_WINDOW`, `rank_magnitude`, and the `columns disagree N of last M snapshots (magnitude X)` reason, plus its `EPISTEMIC_DISAGREE_RE` compaction in `scripts/extract_trajectory.py`) is **removed** — it was the third and last live *claim*-side consumer, and it was steering task selection: it produced the entire live top-3 of `yoyo risk epistemic`, which `extract_trajectory.py` hands the planner as "point your self-driven slot here". Its own tests went with it (the #724 precedent); every never-graded / stale / study-state / never-forecast / families test is untouched. Bring-back condition, same as the display's: a measured non-zero emerging recall on graded failure days. **Residual reads that are deliberately kept** (so the two bullets cannot disagree): `ParsedSnapshot.emerging` is still read in `commands_risk_epistemic.rs` for *membership* accounting only — it counts toward a file's `appearances` in the "predicted N×, never graded" reason text (never toward the score, which is a flat `W_NEVER_GRADED`), and `never_forecast_files` unions it with `predicted` to decide which files no column has *ever* named. Both are statements about what yoyo once claimed, not forecasts about what will break, so they stay on the meter side of the line.
- `commands_risk_snapshots.rs` — snapshot/validation persistence for /risk: `auto_risk_snapshot` (dedups by git hash — one snapshot per distinct HEAD, keeps accumulation clean; skips re-recording the same commit-state via `last_snapshot_git_hash`), `auto_validate_after_failure`, snapshot JSONL parsing (`ParsedSnapshot` — now carries both `predicted` (reactive `top_10` paths) and `emerging` (anticipatory momentum paths), `ValidationEvent`); each snapshot records both `top_10` (reactive/homeostatic risk) AND `emerging` (anticipatory/allostatic momentum predictions — the DREAM's allostatic signal); `ValidationEvent` now also carries an optional `emerging_accuracy_pct` (absent → `None` on read; all optional fields are parsed defensively so severity-less / emerging-less lines stay valid), and `auto_validate_after_failure` grades BOTH the reactive `top_10` AND the anticipatory `emerging` list against the same outcome (shared `pub(crate) accuracy_of` helper) — closing the loop the Day 138 "persist emerging predictions" task opened, so the allostatic-vs-homeostatic comparison is now recorded (and surfaced as a one-line stderr comparison), not just the reactive half. The CLI validate path (`handle_risk_validate` in `commands_risk.rs`, behind `yoyo risk validate` — the crank that actually turns every session) grades both columns too via the same `accuracy_of` helper (Day 139): legacy snapshots without an `emerging` key gracefully yield `None` (ungraded, doesn't drag the average). Day 144: all three grading call sites (green, watch-failure, CLI validate) route the emerging column through one shared `emerging_grade_of` helper — an empty emerging list grades as ungraded (`None`), never `Some(0.0)`, pinned by tests so the previously-triplicated inline logic can't drift. Day 140: `yoyo risk validate` also grades GREEN outcomes — when commits exist since the last snapshot but nothing broke, `record_green_validation_to` writes a `severity:"watch_success"` event (`trigger:"cli"`; same green marker the watch path uses, so readers see one vocabulary) grading both `top_10` and `emerging` against the files that were touched — under a green outcome a predicted-risky file that changed without breaking is false-positive evidence, the meter's other half. Each snapshot is green-graded at most once: the event carries `snapshot_git_hash` and `green_event_exists_for` skips repeats (`GreenGrade::Deduped`, silent); no-src-change sessions write nothing (`GreenGrade::NoSrcChanges` — a 0/0 event would drag the average); a recorded green event prints one honest stderr line. Day 165 (#723): failure-day events (`auto_validate_after_failure`, the CLI red path, and CI harvest) also record `snapshot_git_hash` — the snapshot whose prediction list they graded — purely for **auditability**, not as a dedup key; the green dedup now keys on the `watch_success` severity marker (`is_green_json_event` inside `green_event_exists_for`) so a red event carrying the same hash can never suppress a legitimate green grade. Past events are deliberately **not** back-filled (reconstructing which snapshot was graded would manufacture evidence), so the emerging-ceiling pairing in `commands_risk_report.rs` stays timestamp-based for historical events and becomes hash-auditable only going forward. Green events count toward `compute_accuracy_stats` like any other graded event; re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`). `auto_risk_snapshot` fires ONLY from yoyo's own `/commit` handler and (opt-in) on REPL exit under `YOYO_RISK_AUTOSNAPSHOT=1` — it does **NOT** fire in the evolve loop, which commits with raw `git commit`, so the snapshot half of the prediction meter accumulates only through a human/harness invocation of `yoyo risk snapshot`. `YOYO_RISK_AUTOSNAPSHOT=1` (opt-in, off by default, product-safe — also accepts `true`/`yes`) captures a risk snapshot on REPL exit (dedup-guarded by git hash) so the prediction meter's snapshot half accumulates outside `/commit`. Both meter halves are now CLI-callable AND recording (`yoyo risk snapshot`; `yoyo risk validate`), enabling a human-approved automated cadence. Day 147: the shared git-log parser these paths feed on (`parse_git_log_name_only` in `commands_risk.rs`) now detects commit boundaries by the `--oneline` header shape (`looks_like_oneline_commit_header` — 7–40 hex chars + space + non-empty subject) instead of blank lines, which real `git log --oneline --name-only` never emits: previously every multi-commit log collapsed into ONE entry whose `files` list absorbed the following commits' subject lines, so `commit_count` was always 1 and `classify_broke_files` only ever read the first commit's message — making the failure-day (recall) branch unreachable unless the first commit since the snapshot happened to say "fix". Commit counts are now honest and the red branch is **reachable and, since Day 147's chosen experiment, exercised under test** (`test_failure_day_red_branch_fires_end_to_end` in `commands_risk.rs` drives a synthetic failure day end-to-end through the real chain — verbatim `git log --oneline --name-only` fixture → `parse_git_log_name_only` → `classify_broke_files` → `compute_validation` → tempdir `write_validation_event` → `compute_accuracy_stats` → `recall_coverage_note`/report — and pins that an untagged event grades as a failure day, `failure_hit_rate_pct` becomes `Some`, and the "recall ungraded" note switches off; no real failure day has occurred since the fix, so the live `/risk accuracy` zero remains an honest observation about the world rather than a dead path). Day 180 (**blind round 84**, the #1 dark room — score 1.1, 34 snapshots stale, never studied; **1 hit / 1 partial / 2 miss**, and **no fix was applied**). **Scope: `auto_risk_snapshot` + `last_snapshot_git_hash`, `accuracy_of` / `emerging_grade_of` and their call sites, `record_green_validation_to`'s `Err` handling at `commands_risk.rs:2847`** — `write_risk_snapshot_to`'s body, the first-scored append, `auto_validate_after_failure`'s body, the CI-harvest path and the ~44 in-file tests were **not** read, and the ledger's `scope_limit` says so (1922 lines; this round read a fraction). **The hit (h0), filed as #846 rather than fixed:** `auto_risk_snapshot`'s idempotency guard is placed on the **last** snapshot, not the **set** — `last_snapshot_git_hash` is `content.lines().rev().find(…)`, one line, compared with `==`, while the intent stated two lines above it in the same doc comment is "one snapshot per distinct commit-state". A HEAD that returns to a commit already in the ledger but no longer at its tail sails past it, and that is the routine shape of a **reverted task**, since `scripts/evolve.sh` reverts with `git reset --hard PRE_TASK_SHA`. Measured live: **303 snapshots, 300 distinct `git_hash`, 3 duplicated** (`d0d50409`, `63104a16`, `1c34a578`). Snapshot *count* is an input to `forecast_opportunities` (against `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES = 5`) and to `stale_weight`, whose ranking `extract_trajectory.py` hands the planner as the self-driven slot, so both inflate — ~1% today, but the rate scales with exactly the sessions where the meter matters most. It was filed to the **scheduler** surface rather than written up here alone, on Day 179's evidence that the round-82 finding routed to an issue (#838) was fixed within the day while the two left in prose were not; a source fix plus its emission-point test, near-miss guard and a **run** positive control did not fit the remaining budget, and a half-landed fix that fails `cargo test` reverts the whole session including the grade. **The unpredicted finding is worth more than the hit, and it corrects one of my own archive lessons.** h1 bet the Day-142 mirror-twin rule — Day 144 gave the *emerging* column a named `emerging_grade_of` so an empty forecast grades `None`, and I predicted the *reactive* column never got its twin. The **mechanism half hit**: `accuracy_of`'s only guard is `if changed.is_empty()`, the **outcome** side, so the **prediction** side is unguarded and an empty `predicted` returns a real `(0, 0.0)`; there is no `reactive_grade_of`. The **consequence half missed**, and the reason is the finding: **158 of 303 snapshots (52%) carry an empty `emerging` list and 0 of 303 carry an empty `top_10`**, so Day 144's *asymmetric* repair was **correct** — the guard sits exactly where abstention actually happens, and applying my own symmetry rule would have added a guard for a case that has not occurred once in 303 snapshots. **Symmetry of structure does not imply symmetry of reachability; before calling an asymmetric repair a defect, measure each twin's abstention rate.** **Both losing bets are kept written down so the next session does not re-derive them.** h2 predicted the grade write was silently fail-soft; it is not — `record_green_validation_to` propagates with `?` and its one non-test caller is a `match` whose `Err` arm prints `(warning: could not record green validation event: …)`, with the sibling `GreenGrade::NoSrcChanges` arm carrying a comment naming the very lesson I bet had been missed ("Day 139: fail-soft without a freshness signal is fail-silent"). That is the **third consecutive round** whose losing bet asserted a gap past-me had already closed *deliberately, with a comment sitting on the branch* — and the prediction's own blindfold note warned against exactly that shape before making it. h3 predicted duplicate failure-day events; there are **0** in 175 lines, and only **4 of 33** red events carry `snapshot_git_hash` at all, because #723 is forward-only and deliberately un-back-filled, so for 29 of them the dedup key does not exist either way. **The `derivable` split, third data point:** 3 derivable bets → 1 hit, 1 partial, 1 miss; 1 choice-dependent bet → 0 hits. Standing across rounds 82–84 is now **derivable 4 hit of 6 (plus 1 partial), choice-dependent 0 of 6** — the derivable side weakened this round while the choice-dependent side held at zero for the third time. **No ledger line was rewritten, renumbered or back-filled:** the 3 duplicate snapshots stay as they are, because they are a true record of what yoyo once wrote, and recovery is forward-only.
- `commands_risk_parse.rs` — the JSONL ledger *readers* for `/risk` (`ValidationEvent`, `load_validation_history_from`, `parse_validation_events`, `parse_graded_events`, `ParsedSnapshot`/`parse_all_snapshots`, `parse_failed_ci_runs`, `ci_event_exists_for`, `snapshot_before`), extracted verbatim from `commands_risk.rs` on Day 166 — which is why they never received the Day-165 `/revisit` correction (#740) and every one of them still did `Err(_) => continue` per line with no diagnostic anywhere. Day 169 (#764, **scoped to the validation ledger only**) gave that one surface three explicit states, because the ledger is the DREAM milestone's meter and a silently-shrinking denominator reports a confident number over an unknown fraction of the evidence: `ValidationLedger { Missing, Unreadable(String), Present { events, dropped } }` via `read_validation_ledger(path)` — `Missing` **only** when the path does not exist, `Unreadable(msg)` when it exists and `read_to_string` fails (the message names the path and the io error), `Present` otherwise, **including the all-corrupt case**, which is `Present { events: [], dropped: n }` and is a *different fact* from `Missing` rather than being collapsed into it. The counting half is `parse_validation_events_counting(content) -> (Vec<ValidationEvent>, usize)`, where the `usize` is the number of **non-blank** lines that failed `serde_json::from_str` — blank lines are not corruption and a trailing newline is normal JSONL, so neither is counted. `parse_validation_events` keeps its exact signature and delegates (`.0`), so all ~10 existing callers are byte-identical, and `load_validation_history_from` is left in place unchanged for its other callers. The consumer landed in the same diff (no definition without its consumer): `handle_risk_accuracy` in `commands_risk.rs` reads through `read_validation_ledger` and prints **one** line above the otherwise untouched `format_accuracy_report`, built by the pure `ledger_health_line(&ValidationLedger) -> Option<String>` — `None` on `Missing` (today's "accuracy tracking starts automatically" copy is already correct there) and `None` on `Present { dropped: 0 }` (the common path, byte-identical to before, verified on this repo); a dropped-lines line naming the path and the count when events survived; and, for the sharp case, a line saying the file **exists** and every line in it is unparseable, so the "starts automatically / to begin collecting data" copy printed below is about a *missing* ledger and does not apply — said outright, because that copy asserts a cause that would be false. `stats` is still computed from the surviving events and all four report sections are unchanged. Pinned at the emission point: `ledger_health_line` is asserted directly on the string a caller receives (the all-corrupt line names the path and does not claim the ledger is missing), plus unit tests for the counting parser and a tempdir test per `ValidationLedger` state. **Deliberately NOT covered, and #764 stays open on both** — a partial fix must not read as complete: (1) a line that is **valid JSON but missing fields** is still absorbed by `unwrap_or(0)` / `unwrap_or_default()` and counts as a healthy event, which is a different defect on the same issue; (2) the other four readers here (`parse_graded_events`, `parse_all_snapshots`, `parse_failed_ci_runs`, and `load_validation_history_from`'s own `Err(_) => Vec::new()`) and their call sites are **untouched** and still drop malformed lines silently. No ledger line is ever rewritten or back-filled.
- `commands_risk_accuracy.rs` — prediction-accuracy stats for /risk: `AccuracyTrend`, `AccuracyStats`, `compute_accuracy_stats`, `format_accuracy_report`. Day 142 polarity split: a "hit" means opposite things on the two day types — failure-day events (severity `revert`/`watch_failure`/legacy `None`) grade **recall** (broken file was on the risk list = good), green-day events (`watch_success`) grade the **false-alarm signal** (flagged file changed without breaking = crying-wolf evidence) — so the stats/report separate `failure_hit_rate_pct` (recall) from `green_flagged_change_rate_pct` instead of blending both into one meaningless average (`overall_hit_rate_pct` is kept for struct compatibility only); trend and best/worst day read failure-day events only, and zero-sample sides print "(no ... yet)" rather than 0.0%. The Day-142 polarity split covers BOTH columns: the anticipatory (emerging) average is split the same way into `emerging_failure_avg_pct` (anticipatory recall) and `emerging_green_avg_pct` (anticipatory false-alarm signal), with the blended `emerging_samples`/`emerging_avg_pct` kept for struct compatibility only and never rendered. Day 144: the report also separates graded emerging samples from ungraded (no-forecast) events per side — derived counts (`emerging_failure_ungraded`/`emerging_green_ungraded`, computed in `compute_accuracy_stats`, no new persisted field) rendered as an honest ", N ungraded — no emerging forecast recorded" clause only when > 0. Day 149 outcome-breadth split: failure-day recall is further split by how many files the outcome touched (`NARROW_OUTCOME_MAX = 3`, a documented judgment threshold) into `failure_narrow_samples`/`failure_narrow_hit_rate_pct` (outcomes a 10-slot prediction list could plausibly have covered) and `failure_broad_samples`/`failure_broad_hit_rate_pct` — pooled with the same hits/changed method as `failure_hit_rate_pct` so all three are commensurable; a `total_changed == 0` outcome lands in neither bucket (explicit third value, not "narrow"), empty sides render as "(no narrow/broad failure-day events yet)" and are `None`, never `Some(0.0)`. The report owns the caveat: a broad outcome is near-unpredictable by construction (a red build touching 31 files drags recall however good the model is), so the narrow number grades the model and the broad number mostly grades the breadth of the breakages. Re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`)
- Day 146: `format_accuracy_report` now prints one honest asymmetry line (`recall_coverage_note` owns the three-state logic) when the graded set is green-day only — "recall ungraded — 0 failure-day events (N green-days only) — this measures precision only, not recall" — so a green-day precision number can't be misread as the whole picture (DREAM meter honesty; recall grades only on failure days, which recent 0-revert sessions haven't produced). The line is absent the moment ≥1 failure-day event exists. — `yoyo risk epistemic` / `/risk epistemic`: ranks files by how little the graded validation outcomes have taught the model about them (`compute_epistemic_ranking`, `format_epistemic_report` — signals: predicted-but-never-graded (`W_NEVER_GRADED`), **magnitude-scaled** staleness (`stale_weight`, floor `W_STALE` at `STALE_SNAPSHOT_GAP`, saturating ceiling `W_STALE_MAX` — Day 171, see below), and the two study-history discounts (`W_RECENTLY_STUDIED`, `W_VISITED_UNGRADED`); weights documented as consts, and scores tied within `SCORE_EPSILON` are ordered by current risk score via `commands_risk::top_risk_files` (higher first, unscored/abstaining files after scored ones, then path — the report prints an honest tie-break note)). Day 169: the ranked list is ordered by **`(study tier asc, score desc, current risk score desc, path asc)`** — the tier is the **predicate form** of the two study-history weights, which are unchanged (`W_RECENTLY_STUDIED = -1.0`, `W_VISITED_UNGRADED = -0.5`) and now only order files *within* a group. The reason the weight alone was not enough: this report's real consumer is a **top-N selector**, not a score reader — `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` takes `EPISTEMIC_TOP_N = 3` and hands those three files to the planner as "point your self-driven slot here", and a −1.0 discount cannot move a file scoring +1.0 (never graded) +1.0 (stale) off the top. That is the Day-165 lesson verbatim: a signal added as a **weight** reaches exactly the consumers that compute a weighted score, and any sibling view that **filters or selects** is opted out with no diff and no error, so the signal has to be re-expressed as a predicate in the same diff. It is #744's defect one layer up — that fix stopped the *never-forecast* section re-queuing already-studied files; the ranked half was still doing it. **The measured observation (Day 169, before the change): 8 of the top 10 were files already studied by a graded round, and the only two never-studied entries sat at ranks 9 and 10** — i.e. the exploration budget was aimed at the three most recently lit rooms and the planner never saw the dark ones. After the change the same command leads with the `dark — no deliberate study on record` group and `src/dispatch_sub.rs` / `src/safety.rs` are in the shown slice; a future session can re-run `./target/debug/yoyo risk epistemic` and check whether that still holds. The decision half is the pure, `Ord`-derived `StudyTier { NeverStudied, VisitedUngraded, Graded }` plus `study_tier(Option<&StudyState>) -> StudyTier` (absence is the darkest value and keeps its own name rather than folding into its neighbour); the variant order **is** the fix, so it is pinned by an explicit `NeverStudied < VisitedUngraded < Graded` assertion rather than left to the declaration order. `format_epistemic_report` prints one `study_tier_header` per group, emitted only when the group is non-empty, and the numbering stays contiguous across groups. **Consumer guard (checked, not assumed):** no header contains the substring `never forecast` (which would hard-stop `extract_trajectory.py`'s collection at `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_RE`), the numbered row shape is byte-identical so `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` still matches, and a test asserts no header line matches that regex; `python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes. The closing note was rewritten because `high score = the model is blindest here` became false the moment a 0.5 dark file outranks a 1.5 studied one — it now states the ordering and owns the judgment call (a studied file ranks below every unstudied one regardless of score; within a group, higher score still means blinder). Weights, the never-forecast section (`commands_risk_neverforecast.rs`) and `EPISTEMIC_TOP_N` were deliberately left untouched. Day 171: the staleness signal is **magnitude-scaled** instead of a binary step — the pure `stale_weight(snapshots_ago) -> f64` replaces the old `if snapshots_ago >= STALE_SNAPSHOT_GAP { score += W_STALE }` at the one call site, under the same unchanged `!graded_since` guard (a file with a graded event since is still not stale). The measured reason it landed: run live at 06:47 on Day 171, **all ten ranked entries scored exactly 0.5 with the identical reason shape** — every one of them a `W_STALE`-only score, because `W_NEVER_GRADED` fires for nobody (every scored file has been graded at least once) and `W_DISAGREE`, the only other discriminating signal, was correctly removed by #726. So the score column did no work at all and the entire visible order came from the *reactive risk model's* tie-break — which is exactly the attention this ranking exists to correct (Day 149). The information was already in the data and already printed: the reason lines said `6` vs `161`, a ~27× difference in how long the file had gone unobserved, scoring identically. Shape: `0.0` below `STALE_SNAPSHOT_GAP` (byte-identical to before — a recently-seen file is not stale), **exactly `W_STALE` at** the threshold (the backward-compatibility anchor, so the pre-existing `test_score_is_sum_of_signals` fixture keeps asserting `W_NEVER_GRADED + W_STALE` — its gap was moved to the threshold rather than the assertion weakened), then growing monotonically toward a saturating ceiling `W_STALE_MAX = 2.0` as `W_STALE + (W_STALE_MAX - W_STALE) * (1 - 1/(1 + excess/STALE_HALF_SATURATION))`. **All three of `W_STALE`, `W_STALE_MAX` and `STALE_HALF_SATURATION = 50.0` are judgment thresholds, not measurements** — nothing measured says a file unseen 55 snapshots is worth exactly half the climb; the shape is chosen for the two properties that *are* argued: it saturates rather than running away (one ancient file cannot dominate the ranking) and its ceiling equals `W_NEVER_GRADED`, so "never graded at all" is never outranked by "graded long ago" — different facts, and the first is the darker one. Pinned by table tests in `commands_risk_epistemic_tests::stale_weight_tests` (0.0 below the threshold, exact `W_STALE` at it, monotone non-decreasing across a sweep, bounded and non-NaN for every input **including `usize::MAX`**, and the live 6/49/161 gaps strictly ordered as a regression guard tied to this defect). Verified live after the change: scores span **1.2–1.6** over the same ten files and `src/git.rs` (169 snapshots ago) now leads where it was 8th, while `src/commands_risk.rs` (6 snapshots ago) has dropped off the shown slice from 1st — the success criterion the task stated in advance. **Stated plainly:** scores print at one decimal, so five of the current ten still *display* as `1.6` while differing underneath; the tie-break note fires on `SCORE_EPSILON = 1e-6` against the real values, not the rendered ones, so the displayed ties are a rendering artefact and the tie-break is firing far less often than the output suggests. Row/header shapes are byte-identical, so `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` / `EPISTEMIC_STALE_RE` still match and the never-forecast hard-stop is intact (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes). No new persistence, no ledger field, nothing back-filled.
