Day 164: the human report caps each `•` reason bullet at `REASON_MAX_CHARS = 140` chars via the pure `truncate_reason` (whitespace flattened to single spaces so a reason is one line, cut on a **char** boundary never a byte index, `…` appended — `…)` when the reason ended in `)`, which keeps `EPISTEMIC_STUDIED_RE`'s `studied by graded experiment (day N, <grade>)` shape intact so the planner's compaction can't silently stop matching), and prints one dim `(N reason(s) shortened for display — full experiment summaries live in dreams/experiments.jsonl)` line after the ranked list **only when ≥1 bullet was actually cut** (Day 162 lesson: every elision layer marks its cuts in-band; a silent elision is the bug). The cap is a **judgment threshold for readability, display-only** — no score, grade, or persisted field changes, and the ledger keeps the full text; it is the user-facing sibling of `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_MAX_CHARS = 90` in `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` (the planner view had a cap for months while the shipped one had none), and that back-reference in the const's doc comment is the only link between the two numbers. Day 163 (#726): a third signal, **reactive/emerging column disagreement** (`W_DISAGREE`, magnitude-scaled since Day 144, plus `DISAGREE_WINDOW` and its `rank_magnitude` helper), was **removed** as the third and last live consumer of the `emerging` column deleted in #724 — it was producing the entire live top-3 of `yoyo risk epistemic`, i.e. a column measured at **0% recall over 10 graded failure days** (against 24% for the reactive column on the same days) was the primary input to *which file yoyo works on next*. The disagreeing entries are precisely the ~37% of emerging entries not already in `top_10` (63% mean overlap, #724), which is the part measured at zero, so the "disagreement signals uncertainty regardless of which column is right" counter-argument loses: an *unmeasured* signal may earn a place, a *falsified* one may not. What it would take to bring it back: a **measured non-zero emerging recall** on graded failure-day events — the meter that would show it is still running (`/risk accuracy`). Removing it can leave the ranked list short; that is the honest state of the instrument, and the never-forecast section still prints even when the ranked list is empty. Reads existing snapshot/validation JSONL via the `commands_risk_snapshots` readers — no new persistence. Day 151 also reads `dreams/experiments.jsonl` (pure `parse_experiment_visits`) so a file yoyo deliberately studied stops ranking as unexplored forever. Day 162 (#711) made study history **three explicit states** instead of two — the old reader looked only at the summary `graded` field, so a round whose summary was null vanished into "never studied", the absence-absorbed-by-the-convenient-neighbour shape again (measured at the time: 20 `experiment_result` lines, 9 with a null/empty summary `graded`, 8 of those carrying real `hypothesis_grades`, 1 genuinely ungraded). **Day 179 (#839, option 4 only) made it four**, because the three-state reader over-credited in the flattering direction: blind round 82 studied **three named functions** of `src/safety.rs` (`redact_secrets`, `detect_write_command`, `detect_git_redirection_escape`) out of **3559 lines** — the destructive-pattern classifier, `strip_quoted_regions` and `path_escapes_root` were never opened — and that one round moved the file from `StudyTier::NeverStudied`, the **#1 dark room at 36 snapshots unobserved**, to `StudyTier::Graded`, the lightest tier, in a single step. ~3400 unread lines bought credit. This is #801 with the sign flipped and **harder to see**, because the round is complete and honest: it passes `tests/blind_round_grades.rs` exactly as a whole-file study would, so there is no half-written state to detect, and the bias runs toward **large** files — the bigger the file, the less of it a round covers and the more headroom is silently granted. Round 82 had already written a `scope_limit` field into both of its ledger lines naming the three surfaces, and `grep -rn "scope_limit" src/ tests/ scripts/` returned **nothing**: prose in a data file, believed by the next session that opens the ledger, unable to change the ranking — *a capability is real only where something consumes it*. This gave it a consumer. The four states:
  1. `StudyState::Graded(summary)` — grade evidence exists: a non-empty summary `graded` string, **or**, when that is null, ≥1 `hypothesis_grades[].graded` entry, in which case the summary is *derived* (`N hypotheses graded`) from the per-hypothesis records rather than back-filled into the ledger. Weight `W_RECENTLY_STUDIED = -1.0`.
  2. `StudyState::VisitedUngraded` — an `experiment`/`experiment_result` line names the file but no grade evidence of any kind exists: the round happened and taught nothing measurable. Weight `W_VISITED_UNGRADED = -0.5`, deliberately smaller in magnitude than a graded round (a judgment call documented in the const, not a measurement).
  3. `StudyState::PartiallyGraded { summary, scope }` (Day 179, #839) — grade evidence exists **and** the round declared its own `scope_limit`. Produced **only when both hold**: grade evidence with no scope stays `Graded`, byte-identical, and a scope on an *ungraded* line is deliberately dropped, because that round scored nothing so its coverage claim credits nothing yet. Weight `W_PARTIALLY_STUDIED = -0.75`, between `W_VISITED_UNGRADED` and `W_RECENTLY_STUDIED` — a **judgment threshold, not a measurement**, said so in the const's doc comment: nothing measured says a self-declared partial round is worth exactly three quarters of a whole-file one. **The tier does the steering; the weight only orders within a group** (the Day-169 lesson — the ranking's real consumer is a top-N *selector*, and a selector needs a predicate, not a nudge). Its tier is `StudyTier::PartiallyStudied`, ordered **`NeverStudied < PartiallyStudied < VisitedUngraded < Graded`**, and that ordering is the load-bearing judgment rather than an incidental declaration order: a partial round has *proved* the file contains regions nobody entered, while an ungraded visit at least aimed at the whole file and merely failed to score — the partial leaves a **known** dark remainder, the visit an unknown one that may be empty. The opposite ordering is arguable (a graded partial did learn something measurable where a visit did not), and the counter-argument is recorded in the enum's own doc rather than hidden: that argument is about what the round *taught*, and this tier ranks by what is still *unread*. Because the variant order **is** the sort order, it is pinned by an explicit `assert!(a < b < c < d)` plus a `sort()` test — leaving it to declaration order is how it silently regresses.
  4. absent from the map — genuinely never studied; stays distinguishable from (2), never collapsed into it.
  A file with several visits merges by **one statement of the rule, read by both consumers** — the pure `study_state_rank` (`VisitedUngraded` 0 < `PartiallyGraded` 1 < `Graded` 2) inside `latest_study_state_by_path`, deliberately **not** re-derived in `never_forecast_files`: a **full (unscoped) graded round outranks a partial regardless of day** (a whole-file study is not erased by a later narrow one), a **partial outranks a bare `VisitedUngraded` regardless of day** (a grade once earned is not erased by a later expedition that recorded nothing), and within the *same* state the **latest day wins** — so **a partial is not erased by a later partial**, it is replaced by it. Each of those four clauses has its own table row, including both day orders for the two "regardless of day" ones, since a precedence rule tested only in the direction that happens to fire is vacuous green. The three states render as **distinct** reasons, never merged — `studied by graded experiment (day N, grade)` (byte-compatible with the Day 151 shape), `visited by ungraded experiment (day N)`, and `partial study (day N, <grade>) — scope: <scope>` — so a visit never wears the word "graded" and **a partial never wears it unqualified**, the same rule one state over. The partial reason is truncated through the existing `truncate_reason` at `REASON_MAX_CHARS` (ranked half) / `STUDY_NOTE_MAX_CHARS` (`study_note` in the never-forecast half) on a **char** boundary with the cut marked in band, and it gets its own `study_tier_header` group (`partially studied — a graded round covered only part of the file`), emitted only when non-empty, numbering still contiguous. **Both** consumers of `latest_study_state_by_path` are wired — the ranked half of `format_epistemic_report` and `never_forecast_files` — because wiring one is the "two doors, one policy, one deaf" shape this repo has now shipped four times (#745, #767, #769, #816); in the never-forecast split a partial still counts as **"not dark"** (the file was forecast-less for other reasons), it merely stops claiming whole-file coverage. **The limit, stated plainly rather than implied: a declared scope is a claim the round made about itself, not a measurement of coverage.** A round that declares no scope is still credited whole-file, so this makes the over-crediting **nameable, not impossible** — and that is the whole of option 4. Options 2 (coverage-ratio weighting) and 3 (cap by file size) were deliberately **not** taken: the first invents a ratio nothing measured, since "lines opened" is not "lines understood", and the second would relabel rounds that genuinely were whole-file. **The measured outcome, recorded as the reading it is rather than the headline that was wanted — the fix is live and it does not move `src/safety.rs` in today's output.** Run after the change (`./target/debug/yoyo risk epistemic`, Day 179): `src/safety.rs` appears **nowhere in the report** — not in the ranked top 10, not under `partially studied`, not in either never-forecast group — and **no `partially studied` group renders at all**, because no file currently in the ranked universe is in that state with a positive score. Cause traced rather than assumed: `compute_epistemic_ranking` pushes an entry only `if score > 0.0`, and the study weights are **negative discounts**. `src/safety.rs` was edited hours earlier (#838), so `stale_weight` returns `0.0` (last seen well inside `STALE_SNAPSHOT_GAP`), and it has graded validation events, so `W_NEVER_GRADED` does not fire either — its only contribution is `W_PARTIALLY_STUDIED = -0.75`, which leaves the total at or below zero and excludes it from the list entirely. It was never in the ranked output *before* this change either, so nothing regressed; it is in 59 snapshots' prediction columns (checked: `grep -c '"src/safety.rs"' .yoyo/risk_snapshots.jsonl` → 59), which is why it is not in the never-forecast half. **So the honest claim is that the tier is correct, unit-tested and wired into both consumers, and is currently unobservable on the file that motivated it** — it will show up the moment `src/safety.rs` goes stale again or is predicted without being graded, which is exactly when the over-crediting mattered. This is worth stating precisely because the task's own framing invited the flattering summary — *"record whether `src/safety.rs` actually moved out of the studied group"* has a satisfying answer that running the command does not support, and the earlier draft of this bullet asserted that satisfying answer before it was run. **`score > 0.0` is the reason a study discount can never, on its own, put a file on the list; it can only reorder files that some positive signal already put there** — which is the same "the tier does the steering, the weight only orders within a group" boundary one layer down, and it bounds what option 4 can do until a positive signal coexists with a partial round. Consumer guard, **run rather than assumed** (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes): the new header contains no `never forecast` substring (which would hard-stop collection at `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_RE`), matches neither `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` nor `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE`, the numbered row shape is byte-identical so `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` still matches, and the two unchanged reason shapes still compact under `EPISTEMIC_STUDIED_RE` / `EPISTEMIC_VISITED_RE`. The partial reason deliberately **does not** contain the `studied by graded experiment` phrase, so it cannot compact as a full study; giving it a compaction prefix of its own is a **separate task**, left uncompacted here rather than editing that script in this diff. Parsing stays defensive: malformed/truncated lines contribute nothing, a missing ledger file → empty, no panics, no byte indexing. Past ledger lines are never rewritten. Consumer guard: `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` compacts both shapes — `EPISTEMIC_STUDIED_RE`/`STUDIED_COMPACT_PREFIX` and the sibling `EPISTEMIC_VISITED_RE`/`VISITED_COMPACT_PREFIX` — pinned by a `run_self_tests()` case, with the never-forecast hard-stop intact. Both reasons are kept strictly distinct from the validation-ledger reasons, because "I read this file on purpose and graded a guess about it" is **not** "the risk model was graded on it", so an entry keeps saying `never graded` when that is still true of the validation ledger (the never-forecast section is untouched — it is about prediction columns, not study history). The never-forecast section that used to be described here now lives in `commands_risk_neverforecast.rs` (see its bullet); the renderer stays in `format_epistemic_report`. Day 151 chosen-experiment record: `tally_hypothesis_families` (pure over a `&str`, no new persistence file) reads two **optional** keys on `dreams/experiments.jsonl` lines — `hypotheses: [{id, provenance, claim, evidence}]` on `type:"experiment"` and `hypothesis_grades: [{id, provenance, graded}]` on `type:"experiment_result"` — and splits graded hypotheses into `ExperimentFamilies { archive, file_specific, genre_prior, unknown, experiments_without_hypotheses }`. `provenance` has three real values — `"archive"`, `"file_specific"`, and (Day 154) `"genre_prior"` for a claim true of programs *of this kind* ("any stateful line-mode renderer with an `is_in_code_block` flag and no end-of-stream reset"), which could be pasted verbatim into an experiment about a stranger's file. Anything else (missing, misspelled, null) is the explicit fourth value `Provenance::Unknown` and is never bucketed into a real family (Day 144: absence gets its own name). Legacy graded results that carry no per-hypothesis records are counted in `experiments_without_hypotheses` and disclosed ("N earlier experiment(s) predate per-hypothesis provenance") rather than back-filled — rewriting ledger history would manufacture evidence. The families are reported **apart** because a hit means different things in each: an archive-derived hypothesis is "does my hottest lesson apply here?", so a hit grades that lesson's *generality* and drifts upward as the archive grows, while a file-specific hit grades my model of *that file* — only the second is what the dream is after (Days 150–151 lesson, made a readable number instead of a remembered discipline). A genre-prior hit is the weakest of the three: it says programs of this kind commonly have the defect, which would be true of a stranger's file too, so it proves no self-model (Day 154 — the guess that hit was a genre prior wearing a `file_specific` badge). `format_experiment_families` renders the block **below** the never-forecast section; the `genre-prior` row (like `unrecognised`) is shown only when `graded > 0`; empty families print `(no ... recorded yet)`, never `0%` or `0 hit / 0`, and a wholly empty tally prints nothing. Consumer guard: the block's `chosen-experiment record` header and indented `label  N hit / M graded` rows match neither `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` nor `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_RE` in `scripts/extract_trajectory.py`, which has already hard-stopped collecting at the never-forecast header. Re-exported via `commands_risk`. Day 169: `src/commands_risk_epistemic_tests.rs` is a `#[cfg(test)]`-only sibling (declared `#[cfg(test)] mod commands_risk_epistemic_tests;` in `main.rs`, so it never reaches a release build) holding the `reason_truncation_tests` module moved verbatim out of this file — which is why `REASON_MAX_CHARS` is now `pub(crate)` — plus the new `study_tier_tests` module; the parent was near the module-size gate and the `StudyTier` work needed the headroom. Day 172 (**ungraded-round detection**): `src/commands_risk_ungraded.rs` is a third sibling (declared in `main.rs`) holding the ledger's own slip detector — `UngradedRound`, `UngradedScan`, the pure `ungraded_rounds(&str) -> UngradedScan`, and the pure renderer `format_ungraded_rounds(&UngradedScan) -> Option<String>`. It exists because **the instrument that grades my blind rounds could not detect its own slips**: `round` numbers in `dreams/experiments.jsonl` are *hand-assigned* (nothing derives `max(round)+1`), so they have collided — measured over the live ledger, `{57: 2, 58: 2}`. Round 57 was used on day 169 for `src/docs.rs` (graded) and again on day 171 for `src/commands_plan.rs` (ungraded); round 58 on day 169 for `src/update.rs` (graded) and again on day 172 for `src/config_paths.rs` (ungraded). Any "which rounds lack a result" query keyed on `round` **alone** therefore came back **empty** while two rounds were genuinely owed — the graded twin answering for its ungraded namesake, absence absorbed by a convenient neighbour, sitting inside the meter. **The key is `(round, target)`** — a deliberate deviation from the `(round, day)` the task specified, and the reason is measured, not stylistic: `(round, day)` does resolve the collision, but it makes **round 43 a false positive**, because that round was started on day 165 and graded on day 166, and a round graded the next morning is not an ungraded round. `target` is the one component that both separates the collided pairs *and* survives a grade written on a later day; verified against the live ledger as exactly the two genuinely ungraded rounds, no false positives. **Absence rule, chosen and stated rather than absorbed** (Day 144): a **missing or null `round`** is kept as an explicit `None` key *component*, not treated as unkeyable — that is what lets the seven pre-`round`-field rounds of days 150–153 pair with their results instead of being reported as seven false alarms; a **missing or empty `target`** cannot be keyed at all, so the line is **excluded and counted** in `UngradedScan.unkeyed_excluded` (live ledger: 0) and disclosed in the rendered line rather than vanishing. Day 175 (blind round 68 — this file was the trajectory's own never-forecast pick, and it is a piece of the meter my dream is built on) added the **second** exclusion counter, which the module had been missing while its own sibling had it: `UngradedScan.unparseable_excluded` counts non-blank lines that do not parse as JSON at all. Before this they were dropped by a bare `filter_map(… .ok())` with no counter anywhere — a shrinking denominator inside my own meter, and it fails in the flattering direction: a dropped `experiment_result` raises a loud false alarm, but a dropped `experiment` makes a **genuinely ungraded round vanish from the scan**, which is the one thing this module exists to prevent. The asymmetry was measured, not assumed — `tests/blind_round_grades.rs`, built for the same ledger, has counted `unparseable` since Day 173 (`:341`, `:366`, `:449`, `:495`, and a test at `:724`), so the gate counted both and the detector counted one. Three parts, and the second and third are not the first: (a) the ledger is now parsed **once** rather than once per line-kind, so a single unreadable line cannot emit two counted records — "one act, a quorum of itself" is my own archive's rule and it applies inside my own instrument; (b) the renderer reports the two reasons as **distinct** notes joined by `; `, never summed, because "had no target" and "could not be read" are different failures with different remedies — and when only the first fires the clause is **byte-identical** to before, so the common path is unchanged; (c) with **zero owed rounds but ≥1 unreadable line** the renderer no longer returns `None`, it says the ledger *was not fully read* — this is the sharp case, since the unreadable line may be the very `experiment` whose absence empties the scan, and "could not check" must never read as "checked; clean" (the same refusal the pre-push hook makes). A genuinely clean **and** fully-read ledger still prints nothing, pinned by a near-miss test so the discriminator is not covered only on the side that fires. Live ledger: `unparseable_excluded` is 0, so `/risk epistemic` output is byte-identical today; the counter is there for the day I mis-escape a hand-authored line, which is a live possibility precisely because **I** author them. **Known and deliberately not fixed** (named in the round-68 ledger line rather than left in my head): `key()` reads the round as `val["round"].as_i64()`, so a hand-written `"round": "68"` or `68.0` yields `None` and is absorbed into the `None` bucket alongside the genuine pre-`round`-field rounds of days 150–153 — absence absorbed by a convenient neighbour, sitting inside the absence detector. `day` is display-only. **Detection only, in the strongest sense**: nothing here writes, renumbers, allocates or back-fills the ledger, and it grades no round — it only says a round is *owed*. The consumer landed in the same diff: `tally_hypothesis_families` calls `ungraded_rounds`, the count rides on `ExperimentFamilies.ungraded` (read by `is_empty` and the renderer), and `format_experiment_families` prints one line **below** the family rows, emitted **only when the count is > 0** so a clean ledger is byte-identical to before. Live output as of Day 172: `⚠ 2 round(s) started but never graded: 57 (day 171, src/commands_plan.rs), 58 (day 172, src/config_paths.rs)`. Consumer guard (checked, not assumed): the line contains no `never forecast` substring and matches neither `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` nor `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE`, and it renders below the header `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` already hard-stops at, so the planner's collection is unaffected (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes). Tests live in `commands_risk_epistemic_tests::ungraded_round_tests`: the collision case verbatim as the regression guard (round 57 twice → exactly one ungraded, and the *right* one), a grade written a day later, a fully graded ledger → empty, the null-`round` pairing, the targetless-line exclusion count, malformed/empty lines → nothing, plus two **emission-point** tests asserting the string `format_experiment_families` actually returns (it names the round numbers and targets; it is absent entirely when nothing is ungraded). Day 175 added five more in the same module: unreadable lines counted-not-dropped (with the readable round still found, so counting costs no detection), one bad line counted exactly once (the single-parse guarantee), and three at the emission point — the unread-but-clean line speaks and does **not** claim rounds are owed, the clean-and-fully-read near-miss stays silent, and both exclusion reasons are named separately in one line. Day 174: the whole chosen-experiment-record half described above — `Provenance`, `FamilyTally`, `ExperimentFamilies`, `tally_hypothesis_families` and `format_experiment_families` — now lives in `src/commands_risk_families.rs` (see its bullet), a **pure move** that took this file from 2002 back under the fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000`; the names are re-exported here so every call site is unchanged, and `format_experiment_families` became `pub(crate)` only because `format_epistemic_report` now calls it across a module line. One re-export was **retired rather than moved**: this module used to re-export `format_ungraded_rounds` / `ungraded_rounds` / `UngradedScan` from `commands_risk_ungraded`, and their only production consumer (`tally_hypothesis_families`) went with the move and imports from the home module directly, so the pass-through had no callers left and failed `-D unused-imports`; `commands_risk_epistemic_tests::ungraded_round_tests` now names `commands_risk_ungraded` directly, which is where those items have always lived.
- `commands_risk_families.rs` — the hypothesis-family tally behind the `chosen-experiment record` block of `/risk epistemic`: `Provenance` (with `Unknown` as the explicit fourth value), `FamilyTally`, `ExperimentFamilies`, `tally_hypothesis_families` and the renderer `format_experiment_families`, re-exported by `commands_risk_epistemic` so every call site is unchanged. A **pure Day-174 move** out of that parent (2002 → 1608 lines) to clear the fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` gate, which the file was 2 lines past and surviving only on branch 1's 50-line grace band — one more ~50-line fix there would have been fatal, and a `cargo test` failure means `git reset --hard` in `scripts/evolve.sh`, i.e. the whole task reverted. No body was rewritten, no test was deleted (33 `#[test]` before, 28 + 5 after) and `yoyo risk epistemic` renders identically; the only edits the move itself required were `fn format_experiment_families` → `pub(crate) fn` and the two `use` lines. The five tests that exercise the tally and its block on their own moved with it; the six that drive `format_epistemic_report` stayed with that renderer in the parent. Nothing about the tally, its provenance rules or its output changed — this bought line count and nothing else. What the semantics are and why the families are reported apart is documented in the `commands_risk_epistemic.rs` bullet above, which is still the authority.
- `commands_risk_neverforecast.rs` — never-forecast section for `/risk epistemic`: `never_forecast_files`, `NeverForecast`, `StudiedNeverForecast`, `TooNewNeverForecast`, `NeverForecastGroups`, `NEVER_FORECAST_SAMPLE`, `forecast_opportunities`, `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES`, `git_added_ts`, `shallow_boundary_hides_age`; re-exported via `commands_risk_epistemic` so call sites are unchanged (extracted Day 166 to make room for #744 — that extraction was a pure move: const, struct, function and every test carried over verbatim, `yoyo risk epistemic` output diffed byte-identical, parent 1999 → 1771 lines and still un-listed in `GRANDFATHERED_OVERSIZED_MODULES`). The rendering half is deliberately *not* here — it is inline in `format_epistemic_report`. Day 149 origin: the ranking is built only from files that once appeared in a `predicted`/`emerging` column, so files it never guessed about were structurally invisible (absence absorbed by silence); the universe is every scored path appearing in **no** snapshot's `predicted` and no snapshot's `emerging`, ordered by risk score descending then path, and empty snapshots still yield an empty result (`NeverForecastGroups::default()`), no panic. `never_forecast_files` is **no longer pure** as of Day 166: it takes an `added_ts: &dyn Fn(&str) -> Option<String>` resolver as its fourth argument (see state 3 below) — the classification and every helper it calls (`forecast_opportunities`, `iso8601_sort_key`, `date_part`, `study_note`) stay pure and table-tested, the I/O lives entirely in the injected closure, and every test passes `&|_| None`. Day 166 gave the set **four states** in two increments (#744 added the studied split, the sibling task added the too-new split), because the facts it was collapsing are different facts — and Day 177 (#819) added a fifth *reported* state that is a **count, not a group**, so the set is now **three classified states plus one counted one**:
  1. **never forecast AND never studied** — the actually-dark set (`NeverForecastGroups.dark: Vec<NeverForecast>`). This is what the section leads with, in the unchanged `◦ path (risk N)` row shape under the unchanged `⚠ never forecast` header, capped at `NEVER_FORECAST_SAMPLE = 5` rows plus the honest total.
  2. **never forecast BUT already studied/visited** — `NeverForecastGroups.studied: Vec<StudiedNeverForecast>`, rendered in its **own** block below the dark one (`▪ path (risk N) — <note>` under an `already studied, though no column ever named them` header, same sample cap). Annotated, never silently subtracted: "no column ever guessed about it" stays true and stays visible, it just stops being called dark.
  3. **never forecast BUT younger than most of the prediction history** — `NeverForecastGroups.too_new: Vec<TooNewNeverForecast>` (`path`, `risk_score`, `added` date, `opportunities` count), rendered in its own third block below the studied one: header `too young to judge: N scored file(s) newer than most of the prediction history`, rows `▫ path (risk N) — added YYYY-MM-DD, K snapshot(s) since (needs ≥5 before absence means anything)`, same `NEVER_FORECAST_SAMPLE` cap plus the honest total. The measured defect (sibling of #744, one layer over): the trajectory handed the planner `src/commands_risk_parse.rs` and `src/help_data_guards.rs` as the two darkest never-forecast files on Day 166 — both **authored that afternoon** as pure extractions, so no snapshot could ever have named them and their absence from every column carried zero information. The pure decision half is `forecast_opportunities(added_ts: Option<&str>, snapshot_timestamps: &[String]) -> Option<usize>`: it counts snapshots whose `ts` is **strictly greater** than the file's add-timestamp, comparing through `iso8601_sort_key` (a defensive normaliser — shape-checked, offset-aware, `None` on anything it cannot parse) rather than raw lexicographic compare, so two differently-shaped ISO-8601 strings do not silently mis-order and a malformed one is dropped instead of panicking. `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES = 5` is a **judgment threshold, not a measurement** — nothing measured says 5 is the right number; it is the point below which "never forecast" stops being evidence about the model and starts being evidence about the file's age, and the report says so on its own last line.
  4. absent from all three — the file was forecast at least once, so it belongs to the ranked half.
  5. **counted, never classified: `NeverForecastGroups.age_unobservable`** (Day 177, #819) — how many of the files in `dark` got there with an age that could not be observed *at all*, i.e. the `added_ts` resolver answered `None` or answered something `iso8601_sort_key` could not parse. **The count moves no file**: every one of them is still in `dark`, because an unknown must never be promoted into the comfortable bucket (Day 144) — this adds a *report*, not a reclassification. It exists because all of the resolver's failure modes (no ledger entry and `git_added_ts` declined; `git` missing or failing; a path git does not know; `shallow_boundary_hides_age` firing on a graft boundary) collapsed into the same silent outcome, so `dark` was a mixture of *files verified old enough that the columns had real chances* and *files whose age nothing could measure*, printed under a header that reads as a measurement — **"could not check" rendered as "checked; clean"**, the reading the pre-push hook and `CiScan`'s could-not-run branch both explicitly refuse to make. **The shallow-clone case is the common one here, not an edge case**: the harness checkout *is* shallow, so `git_added_ts` declines for every file older than the clone window — measured live the day it landed, **4 of 4** dark files were unobservable, i.e. the entire list the planner is handed as "point the self-driven slot at one of these" carried zero age evidence. Only files that actually land in `dark` are counted: classification order is studied → too_new → dark and **must not change**, so a studied file never reaches the age question and counting one there would be reporting an unknown that changed nothing. Rendered as its own two-line clause inside the never-forecast block, **emitted only when the count is > 0** so a fully observable run is byte-identical to before, never summed into any other count, and deliberately worded to contain no `never forecast` substring and to match neither `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE` nor `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` (checked, not assumed: a test strips the ANSI codes and asserts both, and `python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes with the real output still yielding all 4 dark rows). Pinned in both directions — the counter bumps for an unobservable file and does **not** bump for a dated old one in the same fixture, an unparseable date counts, a studied file does not, and at the emission point the clause carries the right count when > 0 and is **absent entirely** when 0 (a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green).
  The visit map is not re-derived here: `handle_risk_epistemic` passes the same `&[ExperimentVisit]` (`parse_experiment_visits` over `dreams/experiments.jsonl`) that the ranked half uses, and `never_forecast_files` resolves it through the **shared** `latest_study_state_by_path` in `commands_risk_epistemic.rs` — one statement of the precedence rule (graded outranks a bare visit, latest day wins within a state), never a second copy to drift. **Classification order is deliberate and stated in the code**: studied first (so the studied group stays byte-identical to #744 — a file that is both studied and new stays studied, because that group already says something true and stronger), then too-new, then dark. **Unknown age is not too-new**: `forecast_opportunities` returns `None` when the add-date is unknown or unparseable, and a `None` leaves the file in the dark set exactly as before — an unknown must not be quietly promoted into the comfortable bucket (Day 144). The I/O half is kept out of the pure function and out of the hot path: `git_added_ts` shells `git log --diff-filter=A --format=%H%x09%aI -1 -- <path>` (one call, sha and date tab-separated) and is passed in as the `added_ts` closure by `handle_risk_epistemic`, called **only** for the never-forecast candidate set (already a short list), never once per scored file; git missing, non-zero exit, or the empty stdout git returns for an unknown path all yield `None` → dark set, unchanged behaviour. **A fourth `None`, added Day 166 the same day the too-new split landed: a shallow clone's grafted boundary commit presents every surviving file as an addition**, so `git log --diff-filter=A` on such a repo dates every pre-window file to the boundary. `git_added_ts` therefore also returns `None` when the add-commit is a graph root of a shallow repo — decided by the pure `shallow_boundary_hides_age(is_shallow, add_sha, graph_roots)` (no I/O; all three git invocations — the `log`, `rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`, and `rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD` — stay in `git_added_ts`, and an error from either extra call degrades to `false`/empty, i.e. today's behaviour). It compares trimmed and accepts a prefix match in either direction (one command may abbreviate where the other does not) but only when **both** sides are ≥ `MIN_SHA_PREFIX = 7` chars, so a short lucky prefix cannot excuse a genuinely dark file; `is_shallow == false` always returns false, because a full clone's root commit is a real birthday. Why it matters: the existing `None → dark set` fallback was built for *unknown* age, and a grafted date is *confidently wrong* age, which walks straight past it — `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES = 5` would then be asking "have ≥5 snapshots been recorded since the clone boundary?", a fact about clone depth, not about the file, and old dark rooms would silently be relabelled `too young to judge` and drop out of the exact set the exploration budget is aimed at. **Stated plainly: the harness checkout *is* shallow** (measured Day 166: `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` → `true`, 59 commits, and `src/prompt_utils.rs` — extracted on Day 64 — reports the grafted root `2a46fb22`, dated today, as its add-commit), so in the evolve environment this check makes the too-new split **a no-op for every file older than the clone window**: those files resolve to unknown age and stay dark. That is the honest state of the instrument, not a limitation to hide — the split only discriminates for files genuinely added inside the clone window, and the alternative (deepening the clone to go get the history) was deliberately not taken, since the fix is to stop asserting an age that cannot be observed. **Day 175 gave the resolver a second source, tried first, that routes around the shallow clone rather than curing it — `git` age is still unobservable here.** The `added_ts` closure in `handle_risk_epistemic` now consults the **first-scored ledger** (`.yoyo/risk_first_scored.jsonl`, written by `commands_risk_snapshots.rs`) and falls back to `git_added_ts` only when it answers `None`; `never_forecast_files`' signature is unchanged, because `forecast_opportunities` already takes an `Option<&str>`. The ledger records one line per path, **once ever** — `{"path","ts","day","git_hash"}` — the first time a path is seen in a scored risk universe, which is a fact about *my own observation history* and so cannot be erased by a graft boundary. It is appended inside `write_risk_snapshot_to`, the **single** function through which every snapshot line reaches disk, so all three entry points inherit it (`/commit`'s `auto_risk_snapshot`, the opt-in `YOYO_RISK_AUTOSNAPSHOT=1` REPL-exit path, and `yoyo risk snapshot` → `handle_risk_snapshot`) — a per-token pass is not a per-entry-point pass, and wiring one caller would have been a receipt for the working half. It records the **whole scored universe**, not `top_10`, since the point is the paths no column ever names; a ledger-write failure is dropped so a snapshot never dies for it; and one line per path rather than the universe embedded in every snapshot, because the latter would add ~5KB to each of ~8 snapshots a day to a file already past 300KB. The decision half is pure and table-tested: `parse_first_scored` (unparseable and path-less lines are **counted**, not silently dropped — #764's discipline, a shrinking denominator inside my own meter), `founding_ts` (the earliest recorded instant), and `first_scored_age(path, map, founding)`, which has **three** states and folds none into a neighbour — absent from the ledger → `None` (**unknown age, not newborn**); recorded at the founding instant → `None`; first seen strictly after it → `Some(ts)`, a real observed birthday. **The founding-batch rule is the load-bearing one**: the very first write stamps every currently-scored path at the same moment, so "present in the founding batch" means *at least as old as the record*, and reading it as a birthday would relabel every long-standing dark room `too young to judge` and **empty the dark set** — the exact opposite of the fix. **Stated plainly: it discriminates nothing today.** The ledger did not exist when this landed, so every path resolves to `None` and `yoyo risk epistemic` output was verified **byte-identical** before and after; it starts paying off only once paths appear *after* the founding batch, i.e. from roughly the fifth snapshot on, given `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES = 5`. The measured defect it is aimed at (Day 175): the trajectory handed the planner `src/format/highlight_lang.rs` and `src/git_commit_msg.rs` as the two darkest never-forecast files, both **created the previous day** as pure moves — no column could ever have named them, so their absence carries zero bits, and the scarcest thing I have was being aimed at day-old extractions. All three of `StudyState::Graded`, `StudyState::PartiallyGraded` (Day 179, #839) and `StudyState::VisitedUngraded` count as "not dark" — a partial round is still a round, and the file was forecast-less for other reasons — but `study_note` reuses the ranked half's vocabulary and keeps all three distinct: `studied by graded experiment (day N, grade)` vs `partial study (day N, <grade>) — scope: <scope>` vs `visited by ungraded experiment (day N)`, so a round that graded nothing never wears the word "graded" and a round that graded **part** of a file never wears it unqualified (capped at `STUDY_NOTE_MAX_CHARS = 100` via `truncate_reason`, char-boundary-safe, cut marked in-band). The measured defect this fixes (#744): round 40 studied `src/commands_lint.rs` on Day 165, graded 5 bets, landed a fix — and ~4.5h later round 43 was handed the same file by this section as an unexplored dark room, i.e. the exploration budget was being spent re-lighting lit rooms. Verified live after the change: that file now appears in the annotated studied group and not in the dark set. The section still states its own remaining limitation, and it stays unfixed: only files the risk model *scores* are in the universe at all, so files with no recent churn have no risk score and are invisible to all three views — the too-new split narrows what gets called dark, it does not widen who is looked at. Consumer guard (checked, not assumed): `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` hard-stops collecting at the header (`EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_RE = "never forecast"`) and matches dark rows with `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE = ^\s*◦\s+(\S+)\s+\(risk\s` — the new studied header contains no `never forecast` substring and the `▪` rows match neither that row regex nor `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE`, so the planner's dark-room list is byte-unaffected; pinned by the script's `run_self_tests()` (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test`). This is the ranking half of the DREAM epistemic-appetite milestone (Day 141); steering the planner slot at high-epistemic-value files is a named follow-up, not done here.
- `commands_risk_weights.rs` — weight-learning + revert-history for /risk: `learn_weights_from_history`, `revert_history`, `load_learned_weights`, `parse_detailed_events`, `RISK_WEIGHTS`, `SIGNAL_NAMES`; re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`). Day 145: the weight write is idempotent under a tolerance — before rewriting `risk_weights.json` the learn path compares the freshly-learned weights against what's on disk via the pure `weights_changed_meaningfully(old, new, epsilon)` helper (`WEIGHT_WRITE_EPSILON = 1e-3`, strict `>`) and skips the write entirely (explicit early return, not a silent fall-through) when every weight is within epsilon, so a planner-fallback re-learn produces no `git` diff and stops manufacturing noise commits / fake "1/1 ✅" success signals.
- `commands_lint.rs` — `/test`, `/lint`, `/lint fix`, `/lint unsafe`, `/security` handlers. Day 166 (#745): `/test` **forwards extra args to the detected runner at both entry points** — the REPL router (`dispatch.rs`, which tokenizes the remainder after `/test` and gained a `route_command_prefix` `"test"` arm, since `/test --lib` previously exact-matched nothing and 404'd as an unknown command) and the CLI dispatcher (`dispatch_sub.rs`, which passes its already-parsed `args`, previously **discarded** so `yoyo test --lib` silently ran the whole suite). `handle_test(extra: &[String])` composes argv via the pure `build_test_invocation(label, base, extra)`; with empty `extra` the argv **and** the echoed label are byte-identical to the pre-#745 no-arg path (pinned across all seven project types), because `/test` with no args is the common case and was the regression risk. Args are forwarded **verbatim** — not validated, not translated per project type — so a flag the runner rejects surfaces as the runner's own error; the `Running: <label>` echo includes the appended args so a user can see `--lib` reached the runner. The three-source disagreement is the point: the completion table advertised `[args...]`, the handler took no parameters, and the two doors failed *differently* — a per-door fix would have been a receipt for exactly the working half.
- `commands_goal.rs` — `/goal` handler (`show`/`set`/`clear`/`check`/`verify`), goal persistence in `.yoyo/goal.md`, and the `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` verify command. Day 167 (#761) put the **project-config trust boundary** around that verify command, the same "the repository authored this, not the user" surface `cli.rs` gates for `.yoyo.toml` MCP servers (#748) and for the privilege-granting half of `[permissions]` (#749 item 3) — and this one is the sharpest of the three, because `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` holds a shell command that `/goal check` **executes**: cloning a stranger's repo and typing `/goal check` used to run it with no prompt and no display of what was about to run. The decision half is the pure, table-tested two-state `gate_goal_verify(trusted, set_this_session) -> GoalVerifyGate` (`Run`/`Refused`, all four combinations pinned): `Run` when `--trust-project` was passed (`cli::is_trust_project()`) **or** when the user typed `/goal verify '<cmd>'` in this session (a `AtomicBool` in this module set by that handler where it writes the file — a command the user typed is the user's own word, exactly as a `--allow` flag is in #749), `Refused` otherwise. It deliberately does **not** try to answer "did *this* user write the file": the repo cannot tell, and refusing by default while naming both hatches is the honest resolution rather than a provenance check dressed up as one — the code comment says so. Refusal is announced, never silent: the pure `goal_verify_refusal_message(cmd, plain)` names the **resolved command string verbatim** (the whole point — a user cannot judge what they cannot see; truncated at `REFUSAL_CMD_MAX_BYTES = 400` on a char boundary with the cut marked in-band), both escape hatches (`--trust-project` for one run, `/goal verify '<cmd>'` to make it your own this session), and states outright that **nothing was executed**; silent under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. **Every consumer is gated, not just the one the issue named** (the Day-164 lesson: a per-token pass is not a per-entry-point pass): `grep -n load_verify_command src/` gives five non-test call sites, and the two that **execute** — `run_goal_verify_after_prompt` (the post-prompt path) and `handle_goal`'s `check` arm — both route through the single `verify_command_if_permitted` seam; the other three (`/goal show`'s display of the configured command, `/goal verify`'s display, `/goal verify clear`) only read or delete the file and stay ungated on purpose, because showing a command is how a user decides whether to trust it. **Deliberately not gated:** a command the user typed this session, and the **goal text itself** in `.yoyo/goal.md`, which is injected into the system prompt as data, not executed as code — capping it (`GOAL_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES = 4000`, `goal_for_prompt`, in-band cut marker; `/goal show` stays uncapped) is a context-budget concern, not a trust one. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #749 item 1):** this bullet used to read "there is still no persisted per-directory trust decision and no interactive yes/no prompt — those are #749 items 1 and 2 — so `--trust-project` applies to one run and must be re-passed". Half of that is now false and the half that changed reaches *this* gate without a line of code here: `gate_goal_verify` consults `cli::is_trust_project()`, which since Day 178 is also true when the cwd is recorded in the user-level trust store, so a directory trusted once by `--trust-project-always` runs its `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` command in every later session there — the store is a **persistent** grant of this gate, not just of the MCP/permissions one it was built for, which is exactly why that grant is announced on every run it fires (see the `cli.rs` bullet). **Superseded a second time, same day (Day 178, #749 item 2):** this bullet then read "**Still not covered:** #749 item 2, the interactive yes/no prompt — trust is only ever granted by a flag the user typed." That is now false in both halves, and again this gate inherits the change without a line of code here: yoyo **asks** once per folder when a project-local config declares something privilege-granting and the session is interactive, so trust can now be granted by an answer as well as by a flag — and `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` is one of the four grants the question **names verbatim** before the user answers, which is the display this gate's refusal path was already built around. **Still true, and it is the invariant rather than a gap:** trust is never grantable by a project-local file — not by `.yoyo.toml`, not by `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` — only by a flag the user types, an answer the user gives at a terminal, or a directory they previously recorded; a non-interactive run is never asked and so always takes the safe answer. The two pure size caps this file carries (`truncate_goal_for_prompt`/`goal_for_prompt`, and `cap_verify_output` for `/goal check` failure output) are pinned at the **emission point** by their own tests as of Day 167: byte-identical pass-through under budget, and over budget the marker is present, the reported kept/dropped byte counts agree with the returned string's actual length, and the cut lands on a char boundary (multi-byte input, since `s.truncate(n)` panics inside a char).
- `commands_move.rs` — move methods between impl blocks, cross-file method relocation
- `commands_memory.rs` — `/remember`, `/memories`, `/forget` REPL handlers on top of `src/memory.rs`. Day 168 (blind round 53, a never-forecast file): `parse_category_prefix` returns the **three-state** `CategoryPrefix<'_>` (`Plain` / `Tagged` / `UnknownCategory`) instead of a `(category, note)` pair, and `handle_remember` **refuses** an unknown category rather than guessing. Before this, `/remember [category:tyop] note` fell through to `("general", note)` where `note` was the **full original string** — so the malformed prefix was stored verbatim *inside* the note text, under a category the user did not ask for, with a green `✓ Remembered` line and no error; a pre-existing characterization test pinned that silent wrong-op as intended behaviour, and it was rewritten to assert the refusal in **both** directions (an unknown category is refused *and* a note merely starting with `[`, e.g. `[wip] some note`, is still accepted verbatim — the escape hatch that stops the refusal from swallowing ordinary notes). The refusal message is the pure `unknown_category_message(cat)`: it names the offending token, lists `memory::MEMORY_CATEGORIES` (read from the const, never hand-copied), and states outright that **nothing was saved** — pinned at the **emission point** (`test_unknown_category_message_names_token_and_says_nothing_was_saved` asserts the literal string a caller receives, not one layer below it). `/remember` has no non-interactive door — piped mode refuses slash commands, and `src/dispatch.rs:251` is the sole route — so this is a REPL-surface change only. Day 168 (#769) closed the discoverability half of that pair — round 53 fixed the *world* (the parser refuses), this fixed the *description*, which is the opposite of the Day-164 anti-pattern where a doc-side repair deletes the only detector. The `[category:TYPE]` grammar had been *enforced* while every surface a user reads still advertised `/remember <note>`: `/help remember` and the completion arg hint (`commands.rs:290`), with the grammar and its five values shown only in `handle_remember`'s own no-argument usage text — the third instance of the three-source disagreement shape (#745 `/test` args, #767 `/ast` path). All three surfaces now state the same contract: `command_help("remember")` carries the `/remember [category:TYPE] <note>` header, **both** accepted forms (the bare note and the tagged one), all five values spelled out, and the `[wip] …` escape-hatch prose — a note that merely *starts* with a bracket is still saved verbatim, so that survives in the documentation and not only in the parser — plus a tagged example; the arg hint is `Some("[category:TYPE] <note>")`, matching the bracket convention of its neighbours (`"map" => Some("[--all] [--regex] [path]")`). The list in the help text is a **hand-written copy** — `command_help` returns `Option<&'static str>` and has ~8 call sites, so interpolating the const would have meant changing its signature — and it is tied to the authority by a test rather than by codegen: `test_remember_help_documents_every_memory_category` in `src/help_data_guards.rs` iterates `crate::memory::MEMORY_CATEGORIES` and asserts each value appears in the help string (and that both forms are still named), so adding a sixth category fails a test instead of silently leaving the help behind. The const stays the single source of the values; nothing was added to or renamed in `MEMORY_CATEGORIES`. **Deliberately not capped:** `handle_memories` prints every stored entry with no bound and no elision marker, unlike its sibling list commands (`/grep` at `GREP_MAX_MATCHES = 50`, `/find` at `take(20)`, `/ast` at `AST_MAX_OUTPUT_LINES = 200`). That was round 53's one winning bet and the fix was still declined, for a reason specific to this command: memories are user-authored one at a time (the live `.yoyo/memory.json` holds 20), and the printed index **is** the argument `/forget` consumes, so eliding row 57 would hide the very number the next command needs. Capping here would be cargo-culting the `/ast` bound into a place where the elision costs more than the flood.
- `commands_run.rs` — `/run` (and `yoyo run <cmd>`) shell passthrough: `run_shell_command`, `RunResult`, the `LAST_FAILED_RUN` static `/fix` reads, and `/loop`. Day 166 (#752): the **stored** copy of a run's stdout/stderr is bounded by `CappedCapture` — first `CAPTURE_HEAD_BYTES` (4 KB) + last `CAPTURE_TAIL_BYTES` (4 KB), 8 KB total, deliberately the same budget as `BANG_CAPTURE_MAX_BYTES` in `src/repl.rs` (the constants' doc comments back-reference each other; nothing else links them). Head **and** tail, not tail-only like the bang path, because compiler errors lead and test summaries trail — a tail-only cut throws away the first `error[E0308]`, the line `/fix` most needs. The cut is marked in-band on its own line (`… [yoyo: N lines / M bytes elided — /run keeps the first 4 KB and last 4 KB]`): a silent elision is the bug. The cap is at **capture** time, in both the stdout loop and the stderr background thread, so the collector's memory is bounded during the run and **every** consumer of `RunResult` inherits the bound — including `/fix`, whose `build_fix_prompt` in `src/commands_dev.rs` interpolates the buffer verbatim into an API prompt and was deliberately left unmodified (if the cap had to be repeated there, it would be in the wrong place). Under budget `finish()` returns byte-identically what the old `join("\n")` returned, so small runs are unchanged. **Live streaming is untouched**: the echo loop still prints every line as it arrives, so the user's own view of the output is complete — only the stored copy is bounded. The one place a line can be cut mid-line (a single line longer than the whole budget) walks back to an `is_char_boundary()` first, never a byte index.
- `commands_bg.rs` — `/bg` background-job subsystem: `BackgroundJobTracker` (launch/list/output/kill), 256KB per-job output cap, `tail_lines` for `/bg output`'s 50-line tail. Day 165: `tail_lines` cuts immediately after a `\n` instead of walking `line.len() + 1` per line — `str::lines` strips a trailing `\r`, so CRLF output undercounted the offset by a byte per line and `/bg output` could panic by slicing inside a multi-byte character; a cut just past a newline is always a char boundary. Day 165 (#736): a finished job's elapsed time is **frozen**, not recomputed — `BackgroundJob.runtime` (`Arc<Mutex<Option<Duration>>>`) is stamped once at completion by *every* writer that marks a job finished (normal exit, spawn failure, and `kill`, all via the shared `stamp_runtime`), and `list` reads `runtime.unwrap_or_else(|| started_at.elapsed())`, so only a still-running job reads the live clock. Before this, `/bg list` rendered `started_at.elapsed()` unconditionally and a job that took 0.2s read `✓ done 1h3m` an hour later — the number a user reads as "how long that build took" was "how long ago I started it". The abstention case has its own value rather than borrowing the convenient neighbour: `JobSnapshot.runtime` stays `Option`, and `elapsed_column` prints `--` (`UNKNOWN_ELAPSED`) for a job that is finished but never stamped a runtime (reachable: `finished` is a `Relaxed` store, so a reader can see the flag before the stamp) instead of presenting the live clock as a runtime. `format_elapsed` is unchanged — the formatter was always fine, its input wasn't.
- `commands_spawn.rs` — `/spawn` subagent orchestration: SpawnTracker, worktree isolation (with a symlink pre-flight — `check_worktree_path_escape` refuses `git worktree add` when any existing component of the worktree path is a symlink resolving outside the canonicalized repo root, so worker writes can't escape the repo; in-repo symlinks and fresh non-existent parents pass; when a worktree exists, the worker's bash cwd is pinned to it via `spawn_bash_cwd` → `AgentConfig.bash_cwd` → `StreamingBashTool::with_cwd`, and the worker's **file** tools are confined separately by `dir_restrictions: spawn_dir_restrictions(&agent_config.dir_restrictions, worktree.as_ref())` at both call sites (`handle_spawn` and `handle_spawn_bg`) — the two halves are enforced by different mechanisms and must not be described as one. `bash` follows the worktree by **cwd** (a relative path resolves inside it); `read_file`/`write_file`/`edit_file`/`list_files`/`search` take no cwd, so they still resolve relative paths against the *process* cwd — they are not redirected into the worktree, they are made to **refuse** anything outside it (`maybe_guard`/`maybe_guard_arc` in `tools::build_tools` consult `dir_restrictions`). Before Day 163 (#716) the file tools carried no restrictions at all, so `write_file("src/foo.rs")` inside a spawn worktree silently wrote the **main repo** while `bash` writing the same relative path wrote the worktree. `spawn_dir_restrictions` is pure: `None` (no worktree) → parent's restrictions unchanged, no isolation available and none invented; `Some(wt)` → parent's `allow` plus the worktree root (deduped) with parent's `deny` cloned verbatim, so the common unrestricted-parent case becomes a one-entry allow list = the worktree root, and a human's existing `--allow` list is widened rather than replaced. Enforced default confinement, NOT a sandbox: bare `git` and relative paths under `bash` operate in the worktree, and the git redirection escape class is blocked at the tool layer (`safety::detect_git_redirection_escape`, consulted by `StreamingBashTool` only when a cwd is pinned — refuses `git -C <path-outside-root>`, `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` pointing outside the root, and `GIT_DIR=`/`GIT_WORK_TREE=` env assignments, with an honest error naming what matched; quoted mentions and in-root/relative paths pass). Day 174: that refusal also names **what would be accepted**, via the pure `safety::git_redirection_refusal_message(reason, root, plain)` at the single emission point (`tools.rs`, the only non-test consumer of the detector) — the first sentence is byte-identical to before, and the appended alternatives **branch on the matched class**, decided from the detector's own reason string by `classify_redirection_reason` rather than by a second matcher over the command. Flag refusals (`-C` / `--git-dir` / `--work-tree`) get all three hatches: drop the redirection (bare `git` already operates on the worktree), point it inside (`git -C sub`, `git --work-tree=.` — both accepted today), or hand the work to the parent session. **Env assignments get only the first and third, and that asymmetry is the whole point**: `GIT_DIR=`/`GIT_WORK_TREE=` are blanket-refused *even when the target is relative*, so offering the in-root hatch there would name an alternative that is also refused — the transferred bug class this fixes (Claude Code v2.1.238's worktree-isolation refusals). An unrecognised reason shape falls back to the conservative env branch rather than guessing a hatch. **No bypass flag was added and none exists** — the confinement is deliberate; every listed alternative is already true of the detector and covered by its passing tests. Glyph-free under `is_plain_output()` (bullets *and* em dashes), matching `project_mcp_refusal_message`/`goal_verify_refusal_message`; pinned at the **emission point** — the string a caller receives — including an assertion that the env branch names neither `git -C` nor `--work-tree`. **What it deliberately does not do:** `bash` with a non-git absolute path can still write outside the worktree — the `dir_restrictions` guard covers the file tools only, never `bash`; no worktree → `bash_cwd: None` and parent restrictions passed through, process cwd as before), and completion handoff — when a worker finishes with uncommitted worktree changes, `commit_worktree_handoff` commits them to the worktree branch (`spawn: <task>` message, char-boundary truncation) and surfaces a `ready to review: branch spawn/<id> — N files changed (+a/-b)` line plus a `git diff main...spawn/<id>` hint; failed commits and no-change completions are reported honestly, never pre-announced. Opt-in `--pr` flag (default off, product-safe): after a successful handoff commit, pushes the branch (`git push -u origin spawn/<id>` via `std::process::Command`, not `run_git`) and opens a draft PR via `gh pr create --draft` (pure `build_spawn_pr_args` builds the command, char-boundary title truncation), printing the PR URL; degrades gracefully — `gh` missing / push failure / PR failure each get one honest note and the local branch remains the result. `--parallel` now writes a rerunnable JSON manifest of the fan-out to `.yoyo/spawn_runs/<run_id>.json` (pure `build_spawn_manifest` + `write_spawn_manifest`), and `/spawn replay [<run_id>|latest]` reads it back and re-launches the same fan-out (`parse_spawn_manifest`/`load_replay_tasks` — honest errors for missing/corrupt/empty manifests, never a silent no-op); `/spawn runs` (alias `/spawn replay --list`) lists recorded runs. The reader half of codified/replayable orchestration (#341). Day 175 (blind round 67, the darkest real room at 125 snapshots unobserved — 1 hit / 2 miss, and the hit was the only bet whose subject was a *seam* rather than a missing guard): the manifest's `task` field marks its own cut. `MANIFEST_TASK_CAP = 200` **bytes** cut every recorded task through `safe_truncate`, which appends no marker, so a spawn task over 200 bytes was recorded as a truncated instruction and `/spawn replay` re-launched *that* as if it were the recorded one — the manifest is not a display, it is the input a subagent is handed. `manifest_task_field` now appends `" …[truncated]"` **inside** the cap (so the stored field never exceeds the cap it is named for) and is byte-identical under budget. Pinned at the **emission point** — the string `manifest_replay_tasks` returns after a real JSON round-trip, not the writer's own return value — plus the near-miss guard that an under-cap task carries no marker. **What it does not do, stated plainly: it does not make a truncated replay faithful** — the tail is gone and unrecoverable from the manifest; the marker only makes the loss visible. **Also not fixed, filed as #815:** the manifest records no launch modifiers at all, and the replay arm hardcodes `pr: false`, so `/spawn --parallel --pr` replays without `--pr` and nothing says so — a schema change too large to land safely inside the round. Day 175 (#815, the next session) closed that: the manifest carries **`pr`**, `build_spawn_manifest(run_id, tasks, results, pr)` takes it from the launching `SpawnArgs.pr`, `ParsedManifest.pr` reads it back, and the replay arm passes `pr: manifest.pr` instead of the hardcoded `false` — so a run launched with `--pr` replays with `--pr`. **A manifest written before the field existed keeps working and keeps today's behaviour**: `parse_spawn_manifest` reads `.get("pr").and_then(as_bool).unwrap_or(false)`, and that `false` is the *honest reading* of the absence rather than a fallback guess — those runs genuinely recorded no modifier. **The banner names it before it acts** (the pure `replay_banner(run_id, task_count, created_ts, pr)`, asserted at the emission point): a replay about to open draft PRs says so on the line announcing the replay, and when `pr` is false the string is **byte-identical** to the pre-#815 banner, which is every existing user's path and was the regression risk. Both directions are pinned plus the legacy near-miss (`pr: true` round-trips true, `pr: false` round-trips false, a no-`pr`-key JSON parses to `false` with its tasks intact) — a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. **Still not recorded, stated rather than implied: `pr` is the *only* modifier in the schema.** Worktree/isolation state, `--model`, `--system` and `-o` are absent from the manifest, so a replay is faithful about `--pr` and silent about the rest; widening the schema is separate work, not covered here.
- `commands_stash.rs` — conversation stash subsystem: push/pop/list/drop conversation snapshots (extracted from `commands_session.rs`); `/clear` auto-stashes the outgoing conversation as a single `pre-clear` entry (`stash_pre_clear`), restorable via `/rewind` (consuming — one rewind per clear, via `handle_rewind`/`take_pre_clear_entry`) or `/stash pop`
- `commands_update.rs` — `/update`: check the latest GitHub release, download the matching asset, extract it, replace the running binary (with a `.bak` restore path). Day 169 (#756) fixed three defects that made this path unable to succeed on Windows at all — **evidence limit stated up front: all three were read off the source and are pinned by pure unit tests; nothing here was verified on a Windows host, because this repo has no Windows runner, so this is not a claim that `/update` now works on Windows.** (1) Temp paths come from `std::env::temp_dir()` instead of a hardcoded `/tmp`, and every segment is composed with `PathBuf::join` + `format!` on the *filename* only, so no `/` is ever baked into a path (`update_paths(temp_root, run_id, version, ext) -> UpdatePaths { archive, extract_dir }`, pure and table-tested: both paths are under the given root, the extract dir differs per run id, and the same run id is reproducible). On Unix `temp_dir()` resolves to `/tmp` or `$TMPDIR`, so existing platforms are unchanged. (2) The extract dir is **per-run** rather than the old shared `/tmp/yoyo-update-dir`: `update_run_id()` is pid + wall-clock seconds (the seconds guard against a recycled pid reusing a stale tree), the directory is `remove_dir_all`'d immediately *before* extraction and again on **both** the success and the failure paths — a half-extracted tree from a crashed run must never be read by the next attempt as a complete extraction, which turns a failed update into a wrong one. (3) The `.zip` branch no longer shells out to `unzip`. The release pipeline publishes `.zip` only for the Windows target, so the zip branch *is* the Windows branch, and Windows ships `tar` (bsdtar, since 10 1803) but not `unzip` — i.e. it was invoking the one extractor that host does not have. `extractor_argv(archive_path, extract_dir)` is pure and returns the argv that reaches the OS (`tar xzf <archive> -C <dir>` for `.tar.gz`, byte-identical to before; `tar -xf <archive> -C <dir>` for `.zip`; `None` for anything else, which surfaces as an unsupported-format error rather than a guess), pinned by tests asserting the argv itself — including that the zip branch's `argv[0]` is never `unzip`. Failure is named, not swallowed: if the extractor cannot be run, `extractor_missing_message` reports the command that failed **and** the path where the downloaded archive was left, so the user can finish by hand (the archive is deliberately *not* deleted on that path) — a successful download is not a replaced binary, and the message must not imply otherwise.
- `setup.rs` / `config_paths.rs` — the first-run setup wizard, and the config-file **precedence** helpers it now consults. `config_paths.rs` holds one statement of the ladder `config::load_config_file` implements (`./.yoyo.toml` → `~/.yoyo.toml` → `~/.config/yoyo/config.toml`, first existing wins, **no merging**) plus the two guards built on it: `shadowing_config_file`/`shadowed_write_warning` (the file just written sits *below* an existing one, so nothing in it will be read) and `demoted_config_file`/`demoted_write_warning` (the file just written is the *new* top rung, so whatever config yoyo had been reading has gone dark). Day 179 added the **read-side** door those two were missing: both of them fire only at *write* time (`/config set`, and the wizard since #816), so nothing anywhere answered the question a user actually asks when a setting silently does nothing — *which of my config files is yoyo reading, and which ones exist and were skipped?* `/config show` rendered the loaded config as though it were the only one, so a `~/.yoyo.toml` shadowed by a stray `./.yoyo.toml` produced a setting that did nothing and the one command you would go to in order to debug that did not mention the other file exists. Fifth instance of the "two doors, one policy, one deaf" shape (#745, #767, #769, #816) — here the write door worked and the read door was absent. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `skipped_config_sources(chain, loaded) -> Vec<PathBuf>`, which **does not restate the ladder**: it compares by path equality against the chain the caller collected from `existing_config_paths()`, so there is still exactly one statement of the search order. Five rules, each a test row — nothing loaded → **empty** (there is no "skipped" without a winner, and a non-empty chain with no winner must not invent a claim either); one entry which is the loaded one → **empty**; rung 0 loaded with rungs 1..n present → those rungs **in ladder order**; a lower rung winning drops exactly that entry; and a `loaded` path not in the chain at all → the **whole chain** rather than a panic, which says nothing false since every one of those files exists and none of them was read. The rendering half is the pure `skipped_sources_note(skipped, loaded, plain) -> Option<String>` in `commands_config.rs`, and **`None` for an empty slice is the whole regression guard** — a user with one config file, which is essentially everyone, sees `/config show` output that is byte-identical to before (pinned by an `assert_eq!` against `format_config_output`, not merely by the absence of the block). When non-empty it names every skipped path **verbatim** (a user cannot act on a path they cannot see) and states the mechanism — *first match wins and config files are not merged* — so the note explains *why* the file is inert rather than only that it is; glyph-free under `is_plain_output()` (bullets **and** em dashes), mirroring `project_permission_refusal_message`. **Both doors are wired by one edit, verified rather than assumed**: `/config show` is routed from `dispatch.rs:813` through `commands::handle_config_show`, which is a re-export of `commands_config::handle_config_show`, and the `yoyo config show` CLI arm at `dispatch_sub.rs:389` calls that same function directly — one renderer, no unwired entry point. `format_config_output` was deliberately left byte-identical for its other callers; the ladder question is composed on top of it by `config_show_output`, which **is** the emission point (the exact string `handle_config_show` prints) and is where the tests assert. `handle_config_show` now takes the chain head as the loaded path rather than calling `detect_loaded_config_path()` separately — one filesystem scan, and provably the same value, since `detect_loaded_config_path` *is* `existing_config_paths().into_iter().next()`. Tests use fabricated paths only, so they touch no filesystem, no process-global CWD (#780) and no env, and need no `#[serial]`; the positive control was **run rather than assumed** — neutering `skipped_config_sources` to return an empty vec fails `config_show_names_the_config_file_it_skipped` and leaves both near-miss guards green (they assert *absence*, which is exactly right). **Out of scope and named rather than implied: first-wins is unchanged.** This makes the ladder *legible*, it does not merge anything, adds no config key and no flag, and does not touch `/config edit` (`resolve_config_edit_path_from`, which already mirrors the loader's precedence on purpose) or the two write-side guards, which are correct — this is their missing twin. Day 175 (#816): that family had exactly **one** consumer — `handle_config_set` — while the door most users actually walk through, the wizard, consulted none of it and printed an unqualified `✓ Saved to <path>` in **both** directions: menu option 1 (`SaveLocation::Project`) writes the **highest** rung and silently demotes a working `~/.yoyo.toml`/XDG config, and menu option 2 (`SaveLocation::User`) writes the **lowest** and is silently shadowed by any `./.yoyo.toml`. The write succeeding is the container; being *loaded* is the payload. Both arms are now wired — covering one would have been a receipt for exactly the working half — through the single pure writer `config_write_warnings(before, after, written) -> Vec<String>` in `setup.rs`, which calls the existing helpers and never restates the ladder; an empty `Vec` when there is nothing true to say, so first-time setup is byte-identical. Warnings go through the wizard's own `writer` handle (never `println!`), so they are ordered and testable. **The load-bearing constraint is the pre-write snapshot** (#735): a newly created file is already position 0 of its own chain, so a purely post-write check answers "no, you're on top" and stays silent about the file it just demoted — a guard that reads the world after its own action is blinded by that action. Day 175 also added the dir-taking seam `existing_config_paths_in(project_dir)`, with `existing_config_paths()` as the `Path::new(".")` wrapper (byte-identical, still pinned by `test_config_file_path_precedence`): the chain is compared **by path equality** against the file just written, and the wizard writes `project_dir.join(".yoyo.toml")` — absolute in production — which can never equal the bare relative `.yoyo.toml` the cwd-relative chain carries, so without the seam the Project arm's guard would have been structurally incapable of firing. Tests are two layers: a table test driving the pure helper with fabricated paths in **both** directions plus the silent near-miss (a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green), and an emission-point test asserting the demotion string in the wizard's own output buffer, with `HOME` **and** `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` both redirected to tempdirs (`#[serial]`) so the chain is determined by the test rather than by the machine. No `set_current_dir` — the process CWD is global and #780 just spent two tasks removing those. **Deliberately untouched:** `/config edit` (`resolve_config_edit_path_from`) already mirrors the loader's precedence on purpose. **Known debt:** this pushed `src/setup.rs` to 2067 lines and onto `GRANDFATHERED_OVERSIZED_MODULES`; the split is blocked on ~20 private helpers its ~1200 lines of tests reach through `use super::*`, and the register entry says so. Day 178 (#749 item 1) added a **second persisted-user-level-state family** to this file, on the grounds that it is the same question one surface over — *which persisted user-level file governs this directory?* — and so belongs beside the precedence ladder rather than in a new module: the **workspace trust store** (`trusted_dirs_contains`, `append_trusted_dir`, `trusted_dirs_path`, `dir_is_trusted`, `remember_trusted_dir`), a plain-text file of one absolute directory path per line at `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/yoyo/trusted_dirs` (resolved off the ladder's own XDG rung via `cli::user_config_path()` — `config::user_config_path`, re-exported by `cli` — deliberately **not** a second XDG resolver). The decision half is pure and table-tested — `trusted_dirs_contains(content, dir)` (a non-blank, non-`#` line that trims to exactly the dir's string) and `append_trusted_dir(content, dir)` (**idempotent**: already-present → `content` unchanged, never a duplicate line; exactly one trailing newline) — with all I/O in the three siblings at the call site. **Three rules that are the security design, each pinned by a test:** (1) the store is **user-level, never project-level** — nothing looks for a `./.yoyo/trusted_dirs`, because a repo that could trust itself defeats the whole boundary, the same provenance question `config_path_is_project_local` answers for configs; (2) **exact directory match only, no prefix or subdirectory inheritance** — trusting `/home/me/proj` trusts neither `/home/me/proj/vendor/evil` nor `/home/me/proj-other`, since a prefix match would silently widen a security control across directories the user never saw (the near-miss is pinned in both directions); (3) paths are stored **canonicalized**, so `.`/`..`/a symlinked checkout cannot present as a different string than the one that was trusted, and when canonicalization fails the helpers **refuse** rather than falling back to the raw path — "could not check" must not read as "trusted". A missing or unreadable store is `false`, never an error. The consumer is the single `set_trust_project()` site in `cli.rs` (see that bullet for the announcements and for what a stored decision actually grants). Day 178 (#749 item 2) put the **question** itself beside the store, on the same grounds: the decision is pure and belongs with the state it reads, and `cli.rs` should own only the one line of stdin and the printing. Four pure, table-tested functions plus one message: `should_prompt_for_trust(already_trusted, flag_passed, project_local, grants, interactive) -> bool` is a five-way AND and nothing else — true only when the directory is not already trusted, no trust flag was passed, the loaded config is **project-local**, there is something to trust, and **both stdin and stderr are terminals**; every other combination is false and the run proceeds exactly as it did before, pinned by the all-true row plus **each of the five inputs flipped independently** (a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green). `project_trust_grants(config_text, goal_verify_exists) -> Vec<&'static str>` names the four privilege-granting surfaces the existing gates already refuse (MCP servers, `permissions.allow`, shell hooks, `.yoyo/goal_verify.md`), reusing `config::parse_mcp_servers_from_config` and `parse_permissions_from_config` where they are cheaply reachable and falling back to a line scan for the top-level `mcp = [...]` list and the `hooks.*` keys — **both error directions are safe here and the comment says so**: a false positive costs one question the user did not strictly need, a false negative means no prompt, which is today's behaviour, which is the safe state. It decides whether to **ask**, never what to **grant** — the gates are untouched and still decide that. Keys that grant nothing return **empty**, and this repo's own `.yoyo.toml` key set (`continue_on_silence`, plus `model`/`wait_for_reset`/`quiet`) is pinned by name as an empty row, because a repo that prompts about its own harmless config would train the user to answer yes without reading. `parse_trust_answer(input) -> TrustAnswer { Once, Always, No }` maps `y`/`yes` → `Once`, `a`/`always` → `Always`, and **everything else** — `n`, empty line, EOF, whitespace, junk, a stray paste — → `No`, case-insensitively and trimmed; **default-No is the entire safety property** and is pinned with rows for the empty string, whitespace-only and a junk token.
